# Part 0 — Orientation

## 0.1 About the Exam

**AZ-104: Microsoft Azure Administrator** validates that you can implement, manage, and monitor an organization's Azure environment — identity, governance, storage, compute, networking, and monitoring.

| Attribute | Detail |
|---|---|
| Certification earned | Microsoft Certified: Azure Administrator Associate |
| Level | Associate |
| Questions | Typically 40–60 |
| Duration | ~120 minutes (seat time ~150 minutes) |
| Passing score | 700 out of 1000 (scaled, **not** a percentage) |
| Prerequisites | None formally; AZ-900 recommended |
| Renewal | Free online assessment annually |
| Languages | English plus many others |

> ⚠️ Exam details change. Always verify against the official *Skills Measured* PDF on Microsoft Learn before booking.

### Question Types You Will Encounter

| Type | Description | Strategy |
|---|---|---|
| **Multiple choice** | One correct answer | Eliminate wrong answers first |
| **Multiple response** | "Select all that apply" — no partial credit | Must get *every* box right |
| **Drag and drop** | Match items to targets | Often ordering of steps |
| **Hot area** | Click regions of a screenshot/dropdowns | Read the exhibit carefully |
| **Build list / order** | Sequence steps correctly | Think about dependencies |
| **Case study** | Long scenario + 5–8 questions | Read requirements **before** the narrative |
| **Yes/No series** | Same scenario, 3 variants, cannot go back | Answer each independently |
| **Labs** (may or may not appear) | Live Azure portal tasks | Time-boxed; do them **last** if you can |

### The "Yes/No Repeated Scenario" Trap

You will see a block of 3 questions with an identical stem and slightly different proposed solutions. **You cannot return to a previous question in this block.** Each proposed solution must be judged independently — a solution may be technically valid but not meet the stated goal, or it may meet the goal in a roundabout way (which still counts as "Yes").

---

## 0.2 Skills Measured & Weightings

| # | Domain | Weight | Approx. questions |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Manage Azure identities and governance | 20–25% | 10–14 |
| 2 | Implement and manage storage | 15–20% | 7–11 |
| 3 | Deploy and manage Azure compute resources | 20–25% | 10–14 |
| 4 | Implement and manage virtual networking | 15–20% | 7–11 |
| 5 | Monitor and maintain Azure resources | 10–15% | 5–9 |

**Reading the weights strategically:**
- Domains 1 and 3 are the largest — nearly half the exam. Do not under-study identity because it "feels easy."
- Domain 5 is the smallest but is the most commonly *neglected*. KQL and Backup questions are free marks if you prepare.
- Networking questions are frequently *scenario* questions that also test compute and storage. Weightings understate networking's true footprint.

---

## 0.3 How to Use This Guide

Every section is built the same way:

1. **What it is** — plain-language explanation.
2. **Key Terms** — a table of terminology with precise definitions. *This is the core of the guide.* The exam is largely a vocabulary test in disguise: if you know exactly what "effective route," "deny assignment," "RA-GRS," and "PIP SKU" mean, most questions collapse into a single obvious answer.
3. **How it works** — mechanics, decision rules, limits.
4. **Commands** — CLI and PowerShell equivalents.
5. **Exam traps** — the specific distinctions Microsoft loves to test.

**Symbols used:**
- 📌 = memorize this exactly
- ⚠️ = common trap
- 💡 = mental model / analogy
- 🧪 = try it in a lab

---

## 0.4 Study Plan

### 8-Week Plan (10 hrs/week)

| Week | Focus | Lab work |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Foundations + Entra ID basics | Create tenant users, groups, dynamic groups |
| 2 | RBAC, Policy, Management Groups, Cost | Build custom role; assign policy with effect `deny` |
| 3 | Storage accounts, blobs, tiers, lifecycle | Create SAS; configure lifecycle rule |
| 4 | Files, File Sync, storage security/networking | Mount SMB share; configure private endpoint |
| 5 | VMs, disks, availability, VMSS | Deploy VM from template; configure autoscale |
| 6 | App Service, ACI, ACR, AKS | Deploy container to ACI from ACR |
| 7 | Networking: VNet, NSG, peering, LB, App GW | Build hub-spoke with peering + UDR |
| 8 | Monitor, Backup, ASR + full practice exams | Write 5 KQL queries; restore a VM |

### The 80/20 of AZ-104

If you only had one week, master these:
1. RBAC scope inheritance and role assignment evaluation.
2. Storage redundancy options and what survives which failure.
3. NSG rule evaluation order and default rules.
4. VM availability: sets vs zones vs scale sets.
5. Load Balancer vs Application Gateway vs Front Door vs Traffic Manager.
6. Backup vault types and policy retention.
7. Azure Policy effects.
8. VNet peering behavior (non-transitive!).

# Part 1 — Azure Foundations

> Not an explicit exam domain, but every domain assumes it. Skipping this is why people fail Domain 1.

## 1.1 Cloud Models and Service Models

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Public cloud** | Resources owned and operated by a third-party provider (Microsoft) and shared among many tenants over the internet. |
| **Private cloud** | Cloud infrastructure dedicated to a single organization, on-premises or hosted (e.g., Azure Stack Hub). |
| **Hybrid cloud** | Combination of public and private, connected so workloads can span both (e.g., VPN/ExpressRoute to on-prem). |
| **Multi-cloud** | Using more than one public cloud provider simultaneously. |
| **IaaS** (Infrastructure as a Service) | You manage OS, runtime, apps, data. Provider manages hardware, virtualization, network. *Azure example: Virtual Machines.* |
| **PaaS** (Platform as a Service) | Provider also manages OS and runtime. You manage apps and data. *Azure example: App Service, Azure SQL Database.* |
| **SaaS** (Software as a Service) | Provider manages everything; you only configure and use. *Example: Microsoft 365.* |
| **Shared responsibility model** | The division of security duties between customer and cloud provider. Shifts left (more on Microsoft) as you move IaaS → PaaS → SaaS. |
| **CapEx** | Capital expenditure — big upfront purchase of hardware. On-prem model. |
| **OpEx** | Operational expenditure — pay-as-you-go running costs. Cloud model. |
| **Elasticity** | Ability to add/remove capacity automatically in response to load. |
| **Scalability** | Ability to handle increased load — *vertical* (bigger machine) or *horizontal* (more machines). |
| **Vertical scaling / scale up** | Increase the size/SKU of a single resource. Usually requires restart. |
| **Horizontal scaling / scale out** | Add more instances of a resource. Requires a load balancer and stateless design. |
| **Agility** | Ability to deploy and change resources rapidly. |
| **Fault tolerance** | System keeps working despite component failure. |
| **High availability (HA)** | Design that minimizes downtime, usually within a region. |
| **Disaster recovery (DR)** | Design that recovers from region-level or catastrophic failure. |
| **RTO** (Recovery Time Objective) | Maximum acceptable time to restore service after an outage. |
| **RPO** (Recovery Point Objective) | Maximum acceptable amount of data loss, measured in time. |

💡 **Mental model for shared responsibility:** *You always own your data, your identities, and your access policies — in every model, no exception.* Everything else slides.

### Responsibility Matrix (memorize the pattern)

| Layer | On-prem | IaaS | PaaS | SaaS |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Data & access | You | You | You | You |
| Applications | You | You | You | Microsoft |
| Runtime / middleware | You | You | Microsoft | Microsoft |
| OS | You | You | Microsoft | Microsoft |
| Virtualization | You | Microsoft | Microsoft | Microsoft |
| Servers / storage / network HW | You | Microsoft | Microsoft | Microsoft |
| Physical datacenter | You | Microsoft | Microsoft | Microsoft |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Who patches the guest OS on an Azure VM?" → **You do** (unless you enable Azure Update Manager / automatic VM guest patching, which is still *you configuring it*). Microsoft patches the *host*.

---

## 1.2 Global Infrastructure: Regions, Pairs, Zones

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Region** | A set of datacenters deployed within a latency-defined perimeter, connected by a dedicated low-latency network. E.g., *East US*, *West Europe*. |
| **Geography** | A discrete market, typically containing 2+ regions, that preserves data residency and compliance boundaries. E.g., *United States*, *Europe*. |
| **Region pair** | Two regions within the same geography paired for DR. Updates roll out to one at a time; recovery prioritizes one region per pair. E.g., East US ↔ West US. |
| **Availability Zone (AZ)** | A physically separate location *within* a region, with independent power, cooling, and networking. Minimum 3 zones in enabled regions. |
| **Zonal service** | A resource pinned to a *specific* zone (e.g., a VM you place in Zone 1). |
| **Zone-redundant service** | A resource automatically replicated across zones by the platform (e.g., ZRS storage, Standard Load Balancer, zone-redundant App Gateway v2). |
| **Non-regional / global service** | Not tied to a region (e.g., Microsoft Entra ID, Azure DNS, Traffic Manager, Front Door). |
| **Fault domain (FD)** | A group of hardware sharing a common power source and network switch — a single point of failure. |
| **Update domain (UD)** | A group of VMs rebooted together during planned platform maintenance. |
| **Sovereign cloud** | Physically isolated Azure instance: Azure Government (US), Azure China 21Vianet. |
| **Availability set** | A logical grouping that spreads VMs across FDs and UDs *within a single datacenter*. |
| **Latency perimeter** | The design constraint that a region's datacenters are within ~2ms round-trip of each other. |

### Region Pair Behavior 📌

- Pairs are **fixed and defined by Microsoft** — you cannot choose your pair.
- Pairs are in the **same geography** (exception: Brazil South pairs with South Central US).
- Planned maintenance is applied to **one region at a time** in a pair.
- In a broad outage, **one region of each pair is prioritized** for restoration.
- Some services (GRS storage, Recovery Services vaults) replicate to the pair **automatically**.

⚠️ **Trap:** "Can I set my paired region to Region X?" → **No.** If you need a custom secondary, you build it yourself (e.g., with ASR or GZRS is not customizable either).

### Availability Zones 📌

- Zones are **numbered per subscription** — Zone 1 in your subscription is not necessarily Zone 1 in mine (this is called *logical zone mapping*).
- Not every region supports zones.
- Inter-zone latency is low but non-zero (<2ms) — matters for chatty apps and clustering.
- **AZ protects against datacenter failure. Region pairs protect against region failure.**

| Protection level | Mechanism | Protects against |
|---|---|---|
| Rack | Fault domain | Power supply / TOR switch failure |
| Datacenter | Availability Zone | Flood, fire, power loss in one building |
| Region | Region pair / GRS / ASR | Natural disaster, regional outage |
| Geography | Manual multi-geo design | Sovereign/compliance events |

🧪 **Lab:** Deploy two VMs, one in Zone 1 and one in Zone 2, behind a Standard Load Balancer. Note that Basic Load Balancer *cannot* do this.

---

## 1.3 The Resource Hierarchy

Azure organizes everything in a strict four-level tree. **Scope inheritance flows downward.**

```
Management Group (root: "Tenant Root Group")
   └── Management Group (nested, up to 6 levels deep)
         └── Subscription
               └── Resource Group
                     └── Resource
```

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Tenant** | An instance of Microsoft Entra ID representing an organization. The identity boundary. |
| **Management group (MG)** | A container for organizing multiple subscriptions; policies and RBAC applied here are inherited by all descendants. |
| **Tenant Root Group** | The single top-level MG created automatically. All MGs and subscriptions live under it. Cannot be moved or deleted. |
| **Subscription** | A billing and scale boundary that holds resources. Trusts exactly one Entra tenant. |
| **Resource group (RG)** | A logical container for resources sharing a lifecycle. Every resource must be in exactly one RG. |
| **Resource** | A manageable item in Azure: VM, storage account, NIC, VNet, etc. |
| **Scope** | The level at which a role assignment, policy, or lock applies: MG, subscription, RG, or resource. |
| **Resource provider** | A service that supplies Azure resources; must be *registered* in a subscription. E.g., `Microsoft.Compute`, `Microsoft.Storage`. |
| **Resource type** | The kind of resource, expressed as `provider/type`, e.g., `Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachines`. |
| **Resource ID** | The full unique path: `/subscriptions/{sub}/resourceGroups/{rg}/providers/Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachines/{vm}`. |

### Rules of the Hierarchy 📌

| Rule | Detail |
|---|---|
| MG depth | Up to **6 levels** of nesting (excluding root and subscription level) |
| MGs per directory | 10,000 |
| Subscriptions per MG | Unlimited |
| Parent per MG/subscription | Exactly **one** |
| Resources per RG | ~800 per resource *type* (soft limit; some types differ) |
| RGs per subscription | 980 |
| RG per resource | Exactly **one** — but a resource can be *moved* |
| RG region vs resource region | An RG has a location (stores its metadata) but can contain resources from **any** region |
| Deleting an RG | Deletes **all** resources inside it — irreversible |
| Nested RGs | **Not possible** |

⚠️ **Trap:** "The resource group is in East US; can it contain a VM in West Europe?" → **Yes.** The RG's location only determines where its *metadata* lives. The only consequence: if East US goes down, you may not be able to *manage* (update metadata for) that RG, though the VM keeps running.

⚠️ **Trap:** A subscription trusts **one and only one** Entra tenant, but a tenant can have **many** subscriptions. Transferring a subscription to a different tenant *removes all existing RBAC assignments*.

💡 **Mental model:** MG = org chart. Subscription = credit card + capacity limits. RG = project folder. Resource = the actual thing.

### Choosing a Resource Group Strategy

| Strategy | Group by | Good for |
|---|---|---|
| Lifecycle | Deploy/delete together | Most common; matches "delete RG deletes all" |
| Environment | dev / test / prod | Clean RBAC and policy boundaries |
| Department | HR / Finance | Cost chargeback via RG |
| Region | East US / West EU | Rarely useful; RGs are cross-region anyway |

---

## 1.4 Azure Resource Manager (ARM)

**ARM is the deployment and management service for Azure** — the control plane. Every request from the portal, CLI, PowerShell, SDKs, or REST API goes through ARM.

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **ARM (Azure Resource Manager)** | The management layer that handles create/update/delete of resources, authentication, RBAC, tagging, and locking. |
| **Control plane** | Operations that *manage* the resource (create a VM, resize a disk). Endpoint: `management.azure.com`. Governed by Azure RBAC. |
| **Data plane** | Operations on the *contents* of a resource (read a blob, query a database). Endpoint varies (e.g., `mystorage.blob.core.windows.net`). Governed by data-plane RBAC / keys / SAS. |
| **ARM template** | A JSON file that declaratively defines resources to deploy. |
| **Bicep** | A domain-specific language that transpiles to ARM JSON. Cleaner syntax, same engine. |
| **Declarative** | You describe the *desired end state*; ARM figures out the steps. (vs. *imperative*: you list the steps.) |
| **Idempotent** | Running the same deployment repeatedly produces the same result — no duplicates. |
| **Deployment mode: Incremental** | **Default.** Resources in the template are added/updated. Resources in the RG but *not* in the template are **left alone**. |
| **Deployment mode: Complete** | Resources in the RG but **not** in the template are **DELETED**. |
| **Resource provider registration** | Before you can create a resource type, its provider must be registered on the subscription (`az provider register`). |
| **Deployment scope** | Where the template deploys: resource group (default), subscription, management group, or tenant. |
| **Template spec** | A first-class Azure resource that stores an ARM/Bicep template so it can be shared via RBAC and versioned. |
| **Deployment stack** | A resource that manages a collection of resources as a single unit, with deny settings and cleanup behavior. |
| **ARM cache** | ARM caches results for up to ~30 seconds — why the portal sometimes shows stale state. |

### 📌 Incremental vs Complete — the #1 ARM exam question

| | Incremental (default) | Complete |
|---|---|---|
| Resource in template + in RG | Updated | Updated |
| Resource in template, not in RG | Created | Created |
| Resource in RG, **not** in template | **Left untouched** | **DELETED** |

⚠️ Complete mode is only available for **resource group** scope deployments. Subscription-level deployments only support Incremental.

### ARM Template Anatomy

```json
{
  "$schema": "https://schema.management.azure.com/schemas/2019-04-01/deploymentTemplate.json#",
  "contentVersion": "1.0.0.0",
  "parameters": {          // Inputs supplied at deploy time
    "vmName": { "type": "string", "defaultValue": "vm-web-01" }
  },
  "variables": {           // Values computed inside the template
    "nicName": "[concat(parameters('vmName'), '-nic')]"
  },
  "functions": [],         // User-defined functions
  "resources": [           // What to deploy — the only REQUIRED section besides $schema/contentVersion
    { "type": "Microsoft.Network/networkInterfaces", "apiVersion": "2023-05-01", "name": "[variables('nicName')]", "location": "[resourceGroup().location]" }
  ],
  "outputs": {             // Values returned after deployment
    "nicId": { "type": "string", "value": "[resourceId('Microsoft.Network/networkInterfaces', variables('nicName'))]" }
  }
}
```

| Section | Required? | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `$schema` | ✅ | Defines template language version |
| `contentVersion` | ✅ | Your own versioning string (e.g., `1.0.0.0`) |
| `parameters` | ❌ | Values you pass in; max **256** parameters |
| `variables` | ❌ | Reusable computed values |
| `functions` | ❌ | Custom functions |
| `resources` | ✅ | The resources to deploy; max **800** |
| `outputs` | ❌ | Return values; max **64** |

### Key ARM Template Functions

| Function | Returns |
|---|---|
| `resourceGroup()` | Object with `.name`, `.location`, `.id` |
| `subscription()` | Object with `.subscriptionId`, `.tenantId` |
| `resourceId(type, name)` | Full resource ID |
| `reference(resourceId)` | Runtime properties of another resource (creates implicit dependency) |
| `parameters('x')` / `variables('y')` | Input/computed values |
| `concat()`, `format()`, `toLower()` | String manipulation |
| `uniqueString(resourceGroup().id)` | Deterministic 13-char hash — for globally unique names |
| `utcNow()` | Timestamp (only usable as a parameter default) |
| `listKeys()` / `listSecrets()` | Retrieve keys at deploy time |
| `copyIndex()` | Loop counter for the `copy` element |

### Dependencies

```json
"dependsOn": [ "[resourceId('Microsoft.Network/networkInterfaces', variables('nicName'))]" ]
```
- **Explicit:** `dependsOn` array.
- **Implicit:** using `reference()` or `resourceId()` inside another resource creates the dependency automatically in Bicep; in JSON you still generally need `dependsOn`.
- ⚠️ Over-using `dependsOn` serializes your deployment and makes it slow. Only declare true dependencies.

### Bicep vs JSON

```bicep
param vmName string = 'vm-web-01'
var nicName = '${vmName}-nic'

resource nic 'Microsoft.Network/networkInterfaces@2023-05-01' = {
  name: nicName
  location: resourceGroup().location
}

output nicId string = nic.id
```

| Bicep advantage | Detail |
|---|---|
| No JSON noise | No commas, quotes, brackets everywhere |
| Automatic dependencies | Referencing `nic.id` creates `dependsOn` for you |
| Modules | Cleaner than nested/linked templates |
| Type safety + IntelliSense | VS Code extension validates before deploy |
| No state file | Unlike Terraform, Azure *is* the state |
| Transpiles to ARM | `az bicep build` — same engine, no runtime penalty |

📌 **Bicep has no state file** — a favorite comparison question against Terraform.

### Commands

```bash
# Deploy to a resource group (incremental by default)
az deployment group create \
  --resource-group rg-prod \
  --template-file main.bicep \
  --parameters vmName=vm-web-01

# Complete mode — DANGEROUS
az deployment group create -g rg-prod -f main.json --mode Complete

# Validate without deploying
az deployment group validate -g rg-prod -f main.json

# What-if — preview changes
az deployment group what-if -g rg-prod -f main.bicep

# Export an existing RG to a template
az group export --name rg-prod > exported.json

# Register a resource provider
az provider register --namespace Microsoft.Compute
az provider list --query "[?registrationState=='Registered'].namespace" -o table
```

```powershell
New-AzResourceGroupDeployment -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -TemplateFile main.json -Mode Incremental
Test-AzResourceGroupDeployment  -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -TemplateFile main.json
Export-AzResourceGroup -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -Path ./exported.json
Register-AzResourceProvider -ProviderNamespace Microsoft.Compute
```

⚠️ **Trap:** `az group export` produces a template that often **won't redeploy cleanly** — it omits some properties and hardcodes others. It's a starting point, not a backup.

## 1.5 Management Tools

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Azure portal** | Web GUI at `portal.azure.com`. Good for exploration, bad for repeatability. |
| **Azure CLI** | Cross-platform command-line tool. Commands start with `az`. Bash-friendly. |
| **Azure PowerShell** | PowerShell module (`Az`). Cmdlets use `Verb-AzNoun` (e.g., `New-AzVM`). |
| **Cloud Shell** | Browser-based shell (Bash or PowerShell) with tools pre-installed. **Requires a storage account** for persistence (`$HOME/clouddrive`, 5 GB file share). |
| **Azure Resource Graph** | KQL-based query service for exploring resources at scale across subscriptions — fast, read-only. |
| **REST API** | The underlying HTTP interface all tools call. |
| **SDKs** | Language libraries: .NET, Python, Java, JS, Go. |
| **Azure Mobile App** | iOS/Android — monitor and take basic actions. |
| **Azure Arc** | Extends Azure management (Policy, Monitor, RBAC, tags) to **on-prem and other clouds**. |

📌 **Cloud Shell facts:**
- Persists files in an **Azure Files share** (5 GB) — you must have or create a storage account.
- Session times out after **20 minutes** of inactivity.
- Machine state is not persisted; only `$HOME/clouddrive` (and `$HOME` in newer versions).
- Free to use; you pay only for the storage.
- Available in the portal, `shell.azure.com`, VS Code, and the mobile app.

### Azure Resource Graph — worth knowing

```kql
Resources
| where type =~ 'microsoft.compute/virtualmachines'
| where properties.hardwareProfile.vmSize == 'Standard_D2s_v3'
| project name, resourceGroup, location, tags
| order by name asc
```

```bash
az graph query -q "Resources | where type=='microsoft.compute/virtualmachines' | summarize count() by location"
```

💡 Resource Graph is for **querying inventory**; Log Analytics is for **querying telemetry**. Both use KQL. Don't mix them up on the exam.

---

## 1.6 SLAs, Quotas, and Limits

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **SLA (Service Level Agreement)** | Microsoft's financially-backed uptime commitment for a service. |
| **Composite SLA** | The combined SLA of multiple services in a dependency chain — **multiply** the individual SLAs. |
| **Service credit** | The refund percentage you claim if an SLA is missed. You must *request* it. |
| **Quota / limit** | A cap on resources. **Soft limits** can be raised via support request; **hard limits** cannot. |
| **vCPU quota** | Per-region, per-subscription cap on total and per-family vCPUs. A classic deployment blocker. |
| **Throttling** | ARM rejects requests over the rate limit (HTTP 429) — retry with backoff. |

### 📌 VM SLA Table (memorize)

| Configuration | SLA |
|---|---|
| Single VM, **Premium SSD or Ultra Disk** for OS + all data disks | 99.9% |
| Single VM, **Standard SSD** | 99.5% |
| Single VM, **Standard HDD** | 95% |
| 2+ VMs in an **Availability Set** | 99.95% |
| 2+ VMs across **2+ Availability Zones** | 99.99% |

⚠️ **Trap:** A *single* VM in an availability set gets **no** availability-set SLA. You need **two or more**.

### Composite SLA math

If a web app (99.95%) depends on a SQL database (99.99%):
```
0.9995 × 0.9999 = 0.99940  →  99.94%
```
Adding dependencies **always lowers** the composite SLA. Adding *redundant* paths raises it:
```
Two independent 99.9% regions:  1 − (0.001 × 0.001) = 99.9999%
```

### Downtime cheat sheet

| SLA | Downtime/week | Downtime/month | Downtime/year |
|---|---|---|---|
| 99% | 1.68 h | 7.2 h | 3.65 days |
| 99.9% | 10.1 m | 43.2 m | 8.76 h |
| 99.95% | 5 m | 21.6 m | 4.38 h |
| 99.99% | 1 m | 4.32 m | 52.6 m |
| 99.999% | 6 s | 25.9 s | 5.26 m |

### Free services have no SLA 📌
Free-tier services (App Service Free/Shared, Dev/Test) carry **no SLA**. Preview services also carry no SLA.

---
---

# Part 2 — Domain 1: Manage Azure Identities and Governance (20–25%)

> The largest domain, tied with compute. Microsoft renamed **Azure Active Directory → Microsoft Entra ID** in 2023. The exam uses the new name; older material uses the old one. **They are the same product.**

## 2.1 Microsoft Entra ID Fundamentals

**Entra ID is a cloud-based identity and access management service.** It is *not* a cloud version of Active Directory Domain Services — this is the single most important conceptual distinction in this domain.

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Microsoft Entra ID** | Cloud IAM service providing authentication, authorization, and directory services. Formerly Azure AD. |
| **Tenant** | A dedicated, isolated instance of Entra ID for one organization. Identity boundary. |
| **Directory** | Synonym for tenant in most contexts — the store of users, groups, apps. |
| **Tenant ID** | GUID uniquely identifying the tenant. |
| **Default domain** | `<yourorg>.onmicrosoft.com` — created automatically, cannot be deleted. |
| **Custom domain** | A domain you own (e.g., `contoso.com`) verified via a DNS **TXT** or **MX** record. |
| **Primary domain** | The default suffix used for new users. Can be changed to a verified custom domain. |
| **Identity** | A thing that can be authenticated: user, group, service principal, managed identity. |
| **Security principal** | An identity that can be assigned permissions. |
| **Authentication (AuthN)** | Proving *who you are*. |
| **Authorization (AuthZ)** | Determining *what you may do*. |
| **Service principal** | The local identity/representation of an application in a tenant; what you grant permissions to. |
| **App registration** | The global definition of an application. Creates a service principal in the home tenant. |
| **Managed identity** | An automatically managed service principal for an Azure resource. **No credentials to store or rotate.** |
| **Object ID** | GUID identifying any Entra object (user, group, SP). |
| **UPN** (User Principal Name) | Sign-in name in email format: `user@contoso.com`. Must be unique in the tenant. |
| **SSO** (Single Sign-On) | Authenticate once, access many applications. |
| **MFA** (Multi-Factor Authentication) | Requires 2+ of: something you know / have / are. |
| **Conditional Access** | Policy engine: *if* signals match, *then* grant/block/require controls. **Requires Entra ID P1.** |
| **Identity Protection** | Risk-based detection (leaked creds, impossible travel). **Requires Entra ID P2.** |
| **PIM** (Privileged Identity Management) | Just-in-time, time-bound, approval-based role activation. **Requires Entra ID P2.** |

### 📌 Entra ID vs Active Directory Domain Services — THE key comparison

| Aspect | Entra ID | AD DS (on-prem) |
|---|---|---|
| Structure | Flat — users and groups only | Hierarchical — forests, domains, OUs |
| Protocols | SAML, OAuth 2.0, OpenID Connect, WS-Fed | Kerberos, NTLM, LDAP |
| Query/management | REST (Microsoft Graph) | LDAP |
| Group Policy (GPO) | ❌ Not supported (use Intune) | ✅ |
| Organizational Units | ❌ (use Administrative Units) | ✅ |
| Trusts | ❌ (use B2B/B2C) | ✅ Forest/domain trusts |
| Domain join | Entra join / hybrid join | Domain join |
| Designed for | Web/SaaS apps, internet identity | LAN, file/print servers, legacy apps |
| Managed by | Microsoft (SaaS) | You (on servers) |

⚠️ **Trap:** "You need to apply Group Policy to VMs in Azure." → Entra ID **cannot** do this. You need **Microsoft Entra Domain Services (Entra DS)** or a domain controller VM.

### Microsoft Entra Domain Services (Entra DS)

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Entra Domain Services** | A **managed domain** offering LDAP, Kerberos, NTLM, Group Policy, and domain join — without you running DCs. |
| One-way sync | Objects sync **from** Entra ID **into** Entra DS. Changes made in Entra DS do **not** flow back. |
| Deployment | Deployed into **one VNet in one region**, one managed domain per tenant. |
| Use case | Lift-and-shift legacy apps needing Kerberos/LDAP/GPO. |
| SKUs | Standard, Enterprise, Premium (differ in object count and forest support). |

📌 **Entra DS is one-way sync, and you get no Domain Admin rights** — you get "AAD DC Administrators" group instead.

### 📌 Entra ID License Tiers

| Feature | Free | P1 | P2 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Users, groups, SSO | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| Security defaults | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| SSPR for **cloud-only** users | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| SSPR **writeback** to on-prem | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ |
| Company branding | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ |
| **Conditional Access** | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ |
| **Dynamic groups** | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ |
| **Group-based licensing** | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ |
| Self-service group management | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ |
| Cloud App Discovery | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ |
| Entra Connect Health | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ |
| **Identity Protection** (risk policies) | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| **PIM** | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| Access Reviews | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| Entitlement Management | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |

⚠️ **The three P1 giveaways: Conditional Access, dynamic groups, group-based licensing.**
⚠️ **The three P2 giveaways: PIM, Identity Protection, Access Reviews.**

### Hybrid Identity: Microsoft Entra Connect

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Entra Connect (Sync)** | On-prem agent that synchronizes AD DS objects to Entra ID. Legacy tool; replaced by Cloud Sync for new deployments. |
| **Entra Cloud Sync** | Lightweight agent-based sync; supports multiple disconnected forests, no on-prem server required for the sync engine. |
| **Password Hash Sync (PHS)** | A *hash of the hash* of the password is synced to the cloud. Simplest. Authentication happens **in the cloud**. |
| **Pass-through Authentication (PTA)** | Passwords are validated **on-prem** by an agent. No password hashes in the cloud. |
| **Federation (AD FS)** | Authentication redirected to an on-prem AD FS farm. Most complex; supports smart cards etc. |
| **Seamless SSO** | Silently signs in domain-joined machines on the corporate network. Works with PHS and PTA (not needed with AD FS). |
| **Password writeback** | Cloud password changes flow back to on-prem AD. **Requires P1.** |
| **Source of authority** | Where an object is mastered. Synced objects are mastered **on-prem** and are read-only in the cloud. |
| **UPN suffix / immutableId** | Anchors that match cloud and on-prem objects. |

📌 **PHS is the recommended default.** It provides cloud resilience: if the on-prem environment is down, users can still authenticate. PTA and AD FS both break if on-prem is down.

⚠️ **Trap:** You cannot delete or fully edit a **synced** user in the Entra portal — you must change it on-prem, or the change is overwritten at the next sync cycle (default **30 minutes**).

---

## 2.2 Users

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Member user** | An internal user native to the tenant. Full default permissions. |
| **Guest user** | An external user invited via B2B. UPN looks like `user_contoso.com#EXT#@yourorg.onmicrosoft.com`. Restricted permissions by default. |
| **Cloud identity** | User created directly in Entra ID. Source = "Azure Active Directory". |
| **Directory-synced** | User synced from on-prem AD. Source = "Windows Server AD". Read-only in cloud. |
| **UPN** | Sign-in identifier. Must use a verified domain. |
| **Display name** | Friendly name shown in the UI. Not unique. |
| **Usage location** | **Required before assigning a license.** Two-letter country code; drives service availability by law. |
| **User type** | `Member` or `Guest` — controls default directory permissions. |
| **Soft delete** | Deleted users go to "Deleted users" and are **restorable for 30 days**. After that, permanent. |
| **Restore** | Recovering a soft-deleted user restores group memberships, licenses, and object ID. |
| **Bulk operations** | CSV-based bulk create / invite / delete / restore. |
| **Password profile** | Initial password + "force change on first sign-in" flag. |
| **B2B collaboration** | Inviting external users into your tenant as guests. |
| **B2C** | A **separate product/tenant** for customer-facing identity (social logins, custom flows). |

### 📌 Usage Location — the classic gotcha

You **cannot assign a license** to a user without a `usageLocation`. Bulk imports that fail on licensing almost always fail for this reason.

```bash
az ad user update --id user@contoso.com --set usageLocation=US
```

### 📌 Deleted Users

| Fact | Value |
|---|---|
| Retention in "Deleted users" | **30 days** |
| Restorable? | Yes, within 30 days — restores object ID, group memberships, licenses |
| After 30 days | Permanently purged, unrecoverable |
| Can you purge early? | Yes — "Delete permanently" |
| Synced user deleted on-prem | Soft-deleted in cloud; restore by restoring on-prem object |

### Default Member User Permissions (Free tier)

Members can, by default:
- Read all users, groups, apps, and directory info.
- **Register applications** ⚠️ (often disabled by admins).
- **Create security groups** ⚠️ (often disabled).
- Invite guests ⚠️ (often restricted).
- Change their own password (with old password).
- Manage their own devices and MFA methods.

Guests, by default, have **much more restricted** directory read access.

### Commands

```bash
# Create a user
az ad user create \
  --display-name "Alice Smith" \
  --user-principal-name alice@contoso.com \
  --password 'P@ssw0rd!Strong' \
  --force-change-password-next-sign-in true

# List and filter
az ad user list --filter "startswith(displayName,'Alice')" -o table
az ad user show --id alice@contoso.com

# Update
az ad user update --id alice@contoso.com --set jobTitle="Engineer" usageLocation=US

# Delete
az ad user delete --id alice@contoso.com

# Invite a guest
az ad user invite --invited-user-email-address bob@fabrikam.com \
  --invite-redirect-url "https://myapps.microsoft.com"
```

```powershell
# Microsoft Graph PowerShell (Az module AD cmdlets are deprecated)
Connect-MgGraph -Scopes "User.ReadWrite.All"

$pwd = @{ Password = "P@ssw0rd!Strong"; ForceChangePasswordNextSignIn = $true }
New-MgUser -DisplayName "Alice Smith" -UserPrincipalName "alice@contoso.com" `
  -MailNickname "alice" -AccountEnabled -PasswordProfile $pwd -UsageLocation "US"

Get-MgUser -Filter "startswith(displayName,'Alice')"
Update-MgUser -UserId alice@contoso.com -JobTitle "Engineer"
Remove-MgUser -UserId alice@contoso.com

# Restore a deleted user
Restore-MgDirectoryDeletedItem -DirectoryObjectId <objectId>
```

⚠️ **Trap:** `AzureAD` and `MSOnline` PowerShell modules are **deprecated**. The exam may still reference them, but `Microsoft.Graph` is current.

---

## 2.3 Groups

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Security group** | Used to grant access to resources (RBAC, apps, licenses). Can contain users, devices, service principals, and other groups. |
| **Microsoft 365 group** | Collaboration group that also provisions a shared mailbox, calendar, SharePoint site, and Teams team. **Users only** — no devices. |
| **Assigned membership** | You add and remove members manually. |
| **Dynamic user membership** | Membership calculated from a rule evaluated against user attributes. **Requires P1.** |
| **Dynamic device membership** | Same, but for device attributes. **Requires P1.** Only available on security groups. |
| **Membership rule** | The expression, e.g., `user.department -eq "Sales"`. |
| **Owner** | Can manage the group's membership and settings. |
| **Nested groups** | A group inside a group. ⚠️ Supported for RBAC, **NOT** for group-based licensing or dynamic groups. |
| **Group writeback** | Syncing cloud groups back to on-prem AD. |
| **Self-service group management** | Users request to join/leave groups. Requires P1. |

### 📌 Security Group vs Microsoft 365 Group

| Feature | Security group | M365 group |
|---|---|---|
| Grant Azure RBAC | ✅ | ❌ (not recommended/supported for Azure RBAC) |
| Assign licenses | ✅ | ✅ |
| Members can be **devices** | ✅ | ❌ |
| Members can be **service principals** | ✅ | ❌ |
| Nested groups as members | ✅ | ❌ |
| Provides mailbox/SharePoint/Teams | ❌ | ✅ |
| Dynamic membership | ✅ (user + device) | ✅ (user only) |
| External members | ✅ | ✅ |

### 📌 Dynamic Group Rules — syntax you must recognize

```
(user.department -eq "Sales") -and (user.country -eq "US")
(user.jobTitle -contains "Engineer")
(user.userType -eq "Guest")
(user.accountEnabled -eq true)
(user.assignedPlans -any (assignedPlan.servicePlanId -eq "guid" -and assignedPlan.capabilityStatus -eq "Enabled"))
(device.deviceOSType -eq "Windows") -and (device.deviceOSVersion -startsWith "10.0")
(user.otherMails -any (_ -contains "contoso"))
```

**Operators:** `-eq`, `-ne`, `-startsWith`, `-notStartsWith`, `-contains`, `-notContains`, `-match`, `-notMatch`, `-in`, `-notIn`, `-any`, `-all`
**Logical:** `-and`, `-or`, `-not`

⚠️ **Traps:**
- Dynamic groups require **Entra ID P1** — one P1 license per user *evaluated by the rule*, not just per member.
- A group is **either** assigned **or** dynamic — you cannot mix. Switching membership type from Assigned to Dynamic **removes all existing manual members**.
- Rule processing is asynchronous — can take minutes to hours for large tenants (a full re-evaluation of a big tenant can take up to 24 hours).
- **Devices cannot be added to M365 groups**, so device-based dynamic groups must be **security** groups.

### Commands

```bash
az ad group create --display-name "Sales" --mail-nickname "sales"
az ad group member add --group "Sales" --member-id <objectId>
az ad group member list --group "Sales" -o table
az ad group member check --group "Sales" --member-id <objectId>
az ad group delete --group "Sales"
```

```powershell
# Assigned security group
New-MgGroup -DisplayName "Sales" -MailEnabled:$false -MailNickname "sales" -SecurityEnabled

# Dynamic security group
New-MgGroup -DisplayName "Sales-Dynamic" -MailEnabled:$false -MailNickname "salesdyn" `
  -SecurityEnabled -GroupTypes "DynamicMembership" `
  -MembershipRule '(user.department -eq "Sales")' -MembershipRuleProcessingState "On"

Add-MgGroupMember -GroupId <gid> -DirectoryObjectId <uid>
Get-MgGroupMember -GroupId <gid>
```

💡 **Best practice for RBAC:** Always assign roles to **groups**, never to individual users. It scales, it's auditable, and it survives staff turnover.

---

## 2.4 Licenses

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Direct assignment** | License assigned to an individual user. |
| **Group-based licensing** | License assigned to a group; all members inherit it. **Requires P1** (for the assigning admin's tenant licensing model). |
| **Inherited license** | A license a user holds via group membership. Cannot be removed from the user directly — only by removing them from the group. |
| **Service plan** | An individual capability inside a license SKU (e.g., Exchange Online inside E3). Can be enabled/disabled individually. |
| **Conflicting service plan** | Two SKUs granting the same underlying plan — causes an assignment error. |
| **Usage location** | Mandatory prerequisite for license assignment. |
| **License reconciliation** | The background process applying group licenses. |

### 📌 Rules

1. `usageLocation` **must** be set first. No exceptions.
2. Group-based licensing **does not process nested groups** — only direct members.
3. If the tenant runs out of licenses, group-based assignment errors and reports "Not enough licenses."
4. Removing a user from a licensing group removes the license — and can **delete data** after a grace period (e.g., mailbox).
5. Direct + group license of the same SKU = user keeps license if either source remains.

```powershell
# Set usage location, then assign
Update-MgUser -UserId alice@contoso.com -UsageLocation "US"
Set-MgUserLicense -UserId alice@contoso.com `
  -AddLicenses @{SkuId = "<skuId>"} -RemoveLicenses @()

# View available SKUs
Get-MgSubscribedSku | Select SkuPartNumber, ConsumedUnits, @{N='Total';E={$_.PrepaidUnits.Enabled}}
```

---

## 2.5 External Identities and Guest Access

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **B2B collaboration** | Invite external users as **guests** into your tenant. They keep their home identity/credentials. |
| **B2B direct connect** | Trust-based connection for Teams shared channels — no guest object created. |
| **Azure AD B2C** | A **separate tenant type** for customer-facing apps: social IdPs, custom user journeys, millions of consumers. |
| **Invitation redemption** | The process where a guest accepts and their guest object activates. |
| **Cross-tenant access settings** | Inbound/outbound controls for which external tenants and users can collaborate. |
| **External collaboration settings** | Who in your tenant can invite guests; whether guests can invite others. |
| **Guest inviter** | A directory role allowing invites even when member invites are disabled. |
| **Domain allow/block list** | Restricts which external domains can be invited. |
| **#EXT#** | The marker inside a guest's UPN. |

### 📌 B2B vs B2C

| | B2B | B2C |
|---|---|---|
| Who | Partners, vendors, contractors | Consumers/customers |
| Tenant | Your **existing** Entra tenant | A **separate** B2C tenant |
| Object created | Guest user in your directory | Local consumer account |
| Credentials | Home tenant / one-time passcode / Google | Local, social (Facebook, Google), or federated |
| Customization | Limited branding | Full custom policies (user flows / IEF) |
| Scale | Thousands | Millions |
| Cost | MAU-based, first 50k free | MAU-based |

### Guest permission levels

| Setting | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Same as member users | Guests get full member-level directory read |
| Limited access (default) | Guests can't enumerate all users/groups |
| Restricted access (most restrictive) | Guests can only see their own profile and members of groups they belong to |

⚠️ **Trap:** Guests **can** be assigned Azure RBAC roles and **can** be members of groups. But a guest **cannot** by default invite other guests unless enabled.

---

## 2.6 Self-Service Password Reset (SSPR)

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **SSPR** | Lets users reset/unlock their own account without a helpdesk call. |
| **Authentication method** | The proof used: mobile app notification/code, email, mobile phone, office phone, security questions. |
| **Number of methods required** | 1 or 2 (default 1 for reset; **admins are always forced to 2**). |
| **Registration** | Users must register methods before they can use SSPR. Can be enforced at sign-in. |
| **Password writeback** | Pushes the reset password back to on-prem AD. **Requires Entra ID P1** and Entra Connect. |
| **Account unlock** | Unlock on-prem AD accounts without resetting the password (requires writeback). |
| **SSPR scope** | `None`, `Selected` (a single group), or `All`. |
| **Notification** | Email the user and/or all admins on reset. |

### 📌 SSPR facts

| Fact | Value |
|---|---|
| Scope options | None / **Selected (ONE group only)** / All |
| SSPR for cloud-only users | ✅ Free tier |
| SSPR **writeback** to on-prem | ⚠️ **Requires P1** |
| Admin accounts | **Always require 2 methods**; security questions **not allowed** for admins |
| Security questions | Only available for **non-admins**; 3–5 to register, 3–5 to reset |
| Office phone | Only usable if the attribute is populated; not user-editable |
| Registration enforcement | "Require users to register when signing in" |
| Re-confirm interval | Default 180 days (0 = never) |
| Number of questions | Register 3–5, answer 3–5 |

⚠️ **Trap:** "SSPR scope — Selected" allows exactly **one** group. If you need multiple teams, nest them into one group or use "All."

⚠️ **Trap:** Enabling SSPR does **not** enable password writeback. Writeback is a separate toggle in Entra Connect **and** in the SSPR "On-premises integration" blade, and it needs **P1**.

---

## 2.7 Devices and Device Identity

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Entra registered** | BYOD. Device gets an identity in Entra but the user signs into the device with a personal/local account. Supports Windows, iOS, Android, macOS. |
| **Entra joined** | Corporate-owned, **cloud-only**. User signs into Windows with their Entra credentials. No on-prem AD. |
| **Hybrid Entra joined** | Corporate-owned, joined to **both** on-prem AD DS and Entra ID. Requires Entra Connect. Windows only. |
| **Device object** | The record of the device in the directory; enables Conditional Access device filters. |
| **MDM** | Mobile Device Management (Intune) — pushes policy/config. |
| **MAM** | Mobile Application Management — protects app data without managing the whole device. |
| **Device compliance** | Intune's evaluation of whether a device meets policy; consumed by Conditional Access. |
| **Device writeback** | Syncing device objects to on-prem AD (needed for hybrid join with AD FS). |
| **Local administrator** | On Entra-joined devices: Global Administrator and **Azure AD Joined Device Local Administrator** roles are local admins by default. |

### 📌 The three join types

| | Registered | Joined | Hybrid joined |
|---|---|---|---|
| Ownership | Personal (BYOD) | Corporate | Corporate |
| Sign-in | Local/MSA account | **Entra credentials** | On-prem AD credentials |
| On-prem AD required | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| OS support | Win 10/11, iOS, Android, macOS | **Win 10/11 only** (+ some Linux) | **Win 7/8.1/10/11, Windows Server** |
| SSO to cloud | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| SSO to on-prem | Limited | Via Entra DS/line-of-sight | ✅ Native |
| Group Policy | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| Best for | BYOD | Cloud-first orgs | Migrating orgs with legacy apps |

### Device Settings (Entra ID → Devices → Device settings)

| Setting | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Users may join devices to Entra ID | All / Selected / None |
| Users may register their devices | All / None (Intune/MDM auto-enables) |
| Require MFA to join/register | Yes/No |
| Maximum devices per user | Default **50**; can be 0–unlimited |
| Local administrators on Entra joined devices | Add extra Entra roles/users as local admins |
| Enterprise State Roaming | Syncs settings/app data across devices (P1) |

⚠️ **Trap:** Max devices per user default is **50**. Blocked joins after that.

---

## 2.8 Administrative Units

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Administrative Unit (AU)** | A container of Entra users, groups, and/or devices that scopes **Entra role** assignments to a subset of the directory. |
| **Scoped admin** | A user holding an Entra role *only over the members of an AU*. |
| **Restricted management AU** | An AU whose members can **only** be managed by admins scoped to that AU — even Global Admins are blocked from direct modification (P1/P2 feature). |
| **Dynamic AU** | AU membership calculated by rule. **Requires P1.** |

### 📌 Facts

- AUs scope **Entra ID roles** — *not* Azure RBAC roles. Azure RBAC scopes to MG/sub/RG/resource.
- An object can belong to **multiple AUs**.
- AUs **cannot be nested**.
- Roles commonly scoped to AUs: User Administrator, Helpdesk Administrator, Password Administrator, Groups Administrator, Authentication Administrator.
- Requires **Entra ID P1** for each administered user.

💡 AUs are the closest Entra ID gets to an OU — but they exist only for delegation, not for policy application.

```powershell
New-MgDirectoryAdministrativeUnit -DisplayName "Europe-Users"
New-MgDirectoryAdministrativeUnitMemberByRef -AdministrativeUnitId <auId> `
  -BodyParameter @{ "@odata.id" = "https://graph.microsoft.com/v1.0/users/<userId>" }
```

⚠️ **Trap:** "Delegate password reset for only the London office" → **Administrative Unit** + Helpdesk Administrator role scoped to it. *Not* RBAC. *Not* a resource group.

---

## 2.9 Role-Based Access Control (RBAC)

**Azure RBAC is the authorization system for the Azure control plane (and some data planes).** This is the most heavily tested topic in Domain 1.

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Role assignment** | The binding of three things: **security principal + role definition + scope**. |
| **Security principal** | Who: user, group, service principal, or managed identity. |
| **Role definition** | What: a named collection of permissions (`Actions`, `NotActions`, `DataActions`, `NotDataActions`). |
| **Scope** | Where: management group, subscription, resource group, or individual resource. |
| **Inheritance** | Assignments flow **down** the hierarchy. An assignment at a subscription applies to every RG and resource inside it. |
| **Additive model** | Effective permissions = **union** of all applicable assignments. |
| **Actions** | Allowed **control-plane** operations, e.g., `Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachines/start/action`. |
| **NotActions** | Operations **subtracted** from `Actions`. **Not a deny** — another role assignment can still grant them. |
| **DataActions** | Allowed **data-plane** operations, e.g., `Microsoft.Storage/storageAccounts/blobServices/containers/blobs/read`. |
| **NotDataActions** | Subtracted from `DataActions`. |
| **AssignableScopes** | Where a role *definition* is allowed to be assigned. |
| **Deny assignment** | Blocks specific actions. **Read-only to users** — only created by Azure (e.g., Blueprints, Managed Apps). **Takes precedence over everything.** |
| **Wildcard (`*`)** | Matches all operations at that path. `*/read` = read everything. |
| **Effective permissions** | The net result after union of allows, minus NotActions, minus deny assignments. |
| **eligible vs active (PIM)** | Eligible = must activate JIT; Active = permanently assigned. |

### 📌 The Evaluation Order — memorize

```
1. Collect ALL role assignments applying to the principal at the requested scope
   (including inherited from parent scopes)
2. Union all Actions  →  subtract all NotActions      = allowed control-plane ops
3. Union all DataActions → subtract all NotDataActions = allowed data-plane ops
4. Check DENY ASSIGNMENTS  →  if matched, BLOCK (overrides everything)
5. If the operation is in the allowed set and not denied → ALLOW
6. Otherwise → DENY (default deny)
```

⚠️ **Critical trap:** **RBAC has no user-authorable "deny" rule.** `NotActions` is *subtraction within a single role definition*, not a deny. If Alice has *Reader* with a NotAction and also *Contributor*, she still gets everything Contributor allows.

⚠️ **Critical trap:** Azure RBAC is **additive/union**, unlike NTFS/AD where an explicit Deny wins. The *only* thing that "denies" is a **deny assignment**, which you cannot create directly.

### 📌 The Built-in Roles You MUST Know

| Role | Actions | NotActions | Can assign roles? | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Owner** | `*` | — | ✅ | Full control including RBAC |
| **Contributor** | `*` | `Microsoft.Authorization/*/Write`, `Microsoft.Authorization/elevateAccess/Action`, `Microsoft.Blueprint/blueprintAssignments/write|delete`, `Microsoft.Compute/galleries/share/action` | ❌ | Can do everything **except** grant access |
| **Reader** | `*/read` | — | ❌ | View only |
| **User Access Administrator** | `*/read`, `Microsoft.Authorization/*` | — | ✅ | **Only** manages access — cannot create resources |

| Specialized role | Purpose |
|---|---|
| **Virtual Machine Contributor** | Manage VMs — but **not** the VNet or the ability to assign RBAC, and cannot grant access to the VM |
| **Virtual Machine Administrator Login** | **Sign in to a VM as an administrator** (data plane) |
| **Virtual Machine User Login** | Sign in to a VM as a regular user |
| **Network Contributor** | Manage networks — not the VMs attached |
| **Storage Account Contributor** | Manage the storage account resource (**control plane**) — includes reading keys |
| **Storage Blob Data Owner** | Full data-plane blob access + POSIX ACLs |
| **Storage Blob Data Contributor** | Read/write/delete blobs (**data plane**) |
| **Storage Blob Data Reader** | Read blobs |
| **Storage File Data SMB Share Contributor** | Read/write/delete over SMB |
| **Storage Queue Data Contributor** | Queue message operations |
| **Backup Contributor / Operator / Reader** | Recovery Services vault management tiers |
| **Monitoring Reader / Contributor** | Read/write monitoring data and settings |
| **Log Analytics Reader / Contributor** | Workspace access |
| **Key Vault Administrator / Secrets User / Certificates Officer** | Key Vault RBAC data plane |
| **Cost Management Reader / Contributor** | Cost views and budgets |
| **Support Request Contributor** | Open support tickets — nothing else |
| **Resource Policy Contributor** | Create/edit policies |
| **AcrPush / AcrPull** | Container registry image push/pull |

### 📌 The single most-tested distinction

> **Contributor can do everything except assign roles.**
> **User Access Administrator can *only* assign roles.**
> **Owner = Contributor + User Access Administrator.**

⚠️ **Trap:** "A user needs to manage VMs *and* grant others access to those VMs, but must not manage the network." → **Virtual Machine Contributor + User Access Administrator** (scoped appropriately). *Not* Owner.

⚠️ **Trap:** "A user needs to read blob data." → **Storage Blob Data Reader** (data plane). **Reader** only lets them *see the storage account exists* — they cannot read blob contents with Entra auth.

⚠️ **Trap:** Storage Account Contributor **can retrieve the account keys**, which gives full data access indirectly. This is why key-based access should be disabled if you want true data-plane RBAC.

### 📌 Limits

| Limit | Value |
|---|---|
| Role assignments per **subscription** | **4,000** |
| Role assignments per management group | 500 |
| Custom roles per tenant | 5,000 (2,000 for Azure China/Gov) |
| Assignable scopes per custom role | 2,000 |
| Time for an assignment to take effect | Usually seconds; can take up to **~10 minutes** to propagate |
| Max management group depth | 6 levels |

💡 **Fix for hitting 4,000:** assign to **groups** instead of users; use PIM for eligible assignments; nest.

### Scope inheritance in practice

```
Management Group "Corp"      ← Reader assigned to "All-Staff" group
   └── Subscription "Prod"   ← Contributor assigned to "Ops" group
         └── RG "rg-web"     ← Owner assigned to "Alice"
               └── VM "vm01"
```
- Alice's effective permission on `vm01`: **Owner** (from rg-web) ∪ Contributor (if in Ops) ∪ Reader (if in All-Staff) = **Owner**.
- A member of All-Staff only: **Reader** on everything under Corp.
- You **cannot** reduce Alice's permission on vm01 by assigning her Reader at the resource scope — RBAC is additive; more assignments never reduce access.

⚠️ **Trap:** "Assign Reader at the VM to *restrict* the Owner-at-RG user." → **Does nothing.** The user is still Owner. To restrict, you must **remove** the higher assignment.

### Commands

```bash
# List role definitions
az role definition list --name "Contributor"
az role definition list --query "[].{Name:roleName, Id:name}" -o table

# Assign at different scopes
az role assignment create --assignee alice@contoso.com --role "Reader" \
  --scope /subscriptions/{subId}

az role assignment create --assignee-object-id <groupObjectId> \
  --assignee-principal-type Group \
  --role "Virtual Machine Contributor" \
  --resource-group rg-prod

az role assignment create --assignee <spAppId> --role "Storage Blob Data Reader" \
  --scope /subscriptions/{sub}/resourceGroups/{rg}/providers/Microsoft.Storage/storageAccounts/{sa}

# List assignments
az role assignment list --assignee alice@contoso.com --all -o table
az role assignment list --scope /subscriptions/{subId} --include-inherited -o table

# Delete
az role assignment delete --assignee alice@contoso.com --role "Reader" --scope /subscriptions/{subId}
```

```powershell
New-AzRoleAssignment -SignInName alice@contoso.com -RoleDefinitionName "Reader" -Scope "/subscriptions/{subId}"
New-AzRoleAssignment -ObjectId <groupId> -RoleDefinitionName "Contributor" -ResourceGroupName "rg-prod"
Get-AzRoleAssignment -SignInName alice@contoso.com -ExpandPrincipalGroups
Get-AzRoleAssignment -Scope "/subscriptions/{subId}" -IncludeClassicAdministrators
Remove-AzRoleAssignment -SignInName alice@contoso.com -RoleDefinitionName "Reader" -Scope "/subscriptions/{subId}"
```

📌 `--include-inherited` / `-ExpandPrincipalGroups` — these flags reveal assignments coming from parent scopes and group membership. Exam questions about "why does this user have access?" usually hinge on them.

---

## 2.10 Entra Roles vs Azure Roles

**This is the most confused pair in the entire exam.** They are two *completely separate* permission systems.

| | **Microsoft Entra roles** (directory roles) | **Azure RBAC roles** |
|---|---|---|
| Controls | Entra ID objects: users, groups, apps, domains, licenses | Azure resources: VMs, storage, VNets |
| Scope | Tenant-wide, or an **Administrative Unit**, or an app | Management group / subscription / RG / resource |
| Example roles | Global Administrator, User Administrator, Application Administrator, Billing Administrator, Helpdesk Administrator | Owner, Contributor, Reader, VM Contributor |
| Where managed | Entra ID → Roles and administrators | Any scope → Access control (IAM) |
| Data store | Entra ID | Azure Resource Manager |
| Custom roles | Yes (Entra ID P1) | Yes (free) |
| Number of built-ins | ~100 | 400+ |

### 📌 Global Administrator ≠ Owner

A **Global Administrator** has full control of **Entra ID** — but **by default has NO access to Azure resources.**

To gain Azure access, a Global Admin must **"Elevate access"**:
- Entra ID → Properties → **Access management for Azure resources** → toggle **Yes**.
- This grants the GA the **User Access Administrator** role at the **root scope** `/`.
- The GA can then assign themselves Owner where needed.
- 📌 **You should turn the toggle back off afterwards.** It's an emergency/break-glass mechanism.

```bash
az rest --method post --url "https://management.azure.com/providers/Microsoft.Authorization/elevateAccess?api-version=2016-07-01"
az role assignment list --scope "/" --role "User Access Administrator"
```

⚠️ **Trap:** "The Global Administrator cannot see any subscriptions." → Not a bug. **Elevate access.**

### Key Entra ID roles to know

| Role | Can do |
|---|---|
| **Global Administrator** | Everything in Entra + M365. Microsoft recommends **fewer than 5** and using PIM. |
| **Global Reader** | Read-only counterpart to GA |
| **User Administrator** | Create/manage users and groups, reset passwords for non-admins |
| **Helpdesk Administrator** | Reset passwords for non-admin users only; cannot create users |
| **Password Administrator** | Reset passwords for a limited set of roles |
| **Authentication Administrator** | Manage authentication methods for non-admins |
| **Privileged Authentication Administrator** | Manage auth methods for **anyone**, including GAs |
| **Privileged Role Administrator** | Assign directory roles + manage PIM. **Effectively as powerful as GA.** |
| **Application Administrator** | Manage all app registrations and enterprise apps, including credentials ⚠️ (privilege escalation risk) |
| **Cloud Application Administrator** | Same but cannot manage the app proxy |
| **Groups Administrator** | Manage all groups |
| **Billing Administrator** | Manage subscriptions/purchases |
| **License Administrator** | Assign/remove licenses |
| **Security Administrator / Reader** | Manage/read security features |
| **Conditional Access Administrator** | Manage CA policies |
| **Directory Readers** | Legacy read role |
| **Guest Inviter** | Invite guests |
| **Reports Reader** | Read sign-in/audit logs |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Reset a password for another *administrator*." → Helpdesk Administrator **cannot**. You need **Authentication Administrator** (limited) or **Privileged Authentication Administrator** (any).

⚠️ **Trap:** The Entra role **"Directory Reader"** does not grant any access to Azure resources. Ever.

---

## 2.11 Custom Roles

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Custom role** | A role definition you author when no built-in role fits. |
| **AssignableScopes** | The list of scopes where this role can be **assigned**. Must contain at least one. |
| **Actions / NotActions / DataActions / NotDataActions** | The permission arrays. |
| **JSON definition** | The canonical format for a custom role. |
| **Wildcards** | `Microsoft.Compute/*/read` — allowed in Actions; ⚠️ **not allowed** in AssignableScopes. |

### 📌 Custom role rules

| Rule | Detail |
|---|---|
| Requires | **Entra ID P1/P2 for Entra custom roles**; Azure RBAC custom roles are **free** |
| Max per tenant | 5,000 |
| AssignableScopes | **Cannot be the root scope `/`** for a custom role |
| AssignableScopes | **Cannot use wildcards** |
| Can it include `*`? | Yes in `Actions` |
| Where is it stored? | The Entra tenant — a custom role is visible in all subscriptions of that tenant |
| Can it be assigned outside AssignableScopes? | ❌ No |
| Data actions + wildcards | If you use `*` in DataActions, you must be careful — no NotDataActions inheritance tricks |

### Example custom role JSON

```json
{
  "Name": "Virtual Machine Operator",
  "Id": null,
  "IsCustom": true,
  "Description": "Can start, restart, and monitor VMs, but not create or delete them.",
  "Actions": [
    "Microsoft.Compute/*/read",
    "Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachines/start/action",
    "Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachines/restart/action",
    "Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachines/deallocate/action",
    "Microsoft.Network/*/read",
    "Microsoft.Storage/*/read",
    "Microsoft.Resources/subscriptions/resourceGroups/read",
    "Microsoft.Insights/alertRules/*",
    "Microsoft.Support/*"
  ],
  "NotActions": [
    "Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachines/delete"
  ],
  "DataActions": [],
  "NotDataActions": [],
  "AssignableScopes": [
    "/subscriptions/00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000",
    "/subscriptions/11111111-1111-1111-1111-111111111111/resourceGroups/rg-prod"
  ]
}
```

### Commands

```bash
az role definition create --role-definition ./vm-operator.json
az role definition update --role-definition ./vm-operator.json
az role definition list --custom-role-only true -o table
az role definition delete --name "Virtual Machine Operator"

# Discover available operations for a provider
az provider operation show --namespace Microsoft.Compute --query "resourceTypes[?name=='virtualMachines'].operations[].name" -o tsv
```

```powershell
# Clone a built-in role as a starting point
$role = Get-AzRoleDefinition "Virtual Machine Contributor"
$role.Id = $null
$role.Name = "Virtual Machine Operator"
$role.IsCustom = $true
$role.Description = "Start/restart VMs only"
$role.Actions.Clear()
$role.Actions.Add("Microsoft.Compute/*/read")
$role.Actions.Add("Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachines/start/action")
$role.Actions.Add("Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachines/restart/action")
$role.AssignableScopes.Clear()
$role.AssignableScopes.Add("/subscriptions/<subId>")
New-AzRoleDefinition -Role $role

Get-AzProviderOperation "Microsoft.Compute/*" | Select Operation, Description
```

⚠️ **Trap:** AssignableScopes **cannot be `/` (root)** for custom roles — a very common wrong answer.

---

## 2.12 Subscriptions and Management Groups

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Subscription** | A billing + scale boundary containing resources; trusts exactly one Entra tenant. |
| **Subscription ID** | GUID identifying the subscription. |
| **Account Administrator** | The billing owner of the subscription (classic). Only role that can transfer billing ownership or cancel the subscription. |
| **Service Administrator** | Classic role equivalent to Owner at subscription scope. |
| **Co-Administrator** | Classic role equivalent to Service Administrator. Being **retired**. |
| **Offer / subscription type** | Pay-As-You-Go, Enterprise Agreement (EA), CSP, MSDN/Visual Studio, Free Trial, Azure for Students. |
| **EA (Enterprise Agreement)** | Volume licensing agreement; hierarchy = Billing Account → Department → Enrollment Account → Subscription. |
| **MCA (Microsoft Customer Agreement)** | Newer model; hierarchy = Billing Account → Billing Profile → Invoice Section → Subscription. |
| **CSP** | Cloud Solution Provider — bought via a partner. |
| **Management group** | Container for subscriptions; policy/RBAC inheritance point. |
| **Tenant Root Group** | Auto-created top MG; ID = tenant ID. |
| **Subscription transfer** | Moving a subscription to a different tenant. **Destroys all RBAC assignments.** |
| **Resource limit boundary** | Quotas (vCPU, storage accounts, etc.) apply **per subscription per region**. |

### 📌 Management Group facts

| Fact | Value |
|---|---|
| Max MGs per directory | 10,000 |
| Max depth | **6 levels** (excluding root and subscription) |
| Parents per MG | Exactly 1 |
| Root MG name | Tenant Root Group; **cannot be deleted or moved** |
| Who can manage root by default | Nobody — a Global Admin must **elevate access** to manage the root MG initially |
| Deleting an MG | Must be **empty** first |
| Direct children of root | MGs and subscriptions |
| New subscriptions land where? | Root MG by default (configurable via "default management group") |

### Common MG design

```
Tenant Root Group
├── Platform
│   ├── Identity      (DCs, Entra DS)
│   ├── Management    (Log Analytics, Automation)
│   └── Connectivity  (Hub VNet, Firewall, ExpressRoute)
├── Landing Zones
│   ├── Corp          (internal apps)
│   └── Online        (internet-facing)
├── Sandbox           (relaxed policy, cost-capped)
└── Decommissioned
```
This is the **Cloud Adoption Framework (CAF) enterprise-scale** pattern.

### Commands

```bash
az account management-group create --name "Platform" --display-name "Platform"
az account management-group create --name "Identity" --parent "Platform"
az account management-group subscription add --name "Platform" --subscription <subId>
az account management-group list -o table
az account management-group show --name "Platform" --expand --recurse

# Subscriptions
az account list -o table
az account set --subscription "Prod"
az account show
```

```powershell
New-AzManagementGroup -GroupName "Platform" -DisplayName "Platform"
New-AzManagementGroup -GroupName "Identity" -ParentId "/providers/Microsoft.Management/managementGroups/Platform"
New-AzManagementGroupSubscription -GroupName "Platform" -SubscriptionId <subId>
Get-AzManagementGroup
Set-AzContext -Subscription "Prod"
```

⚠️ **Trap:** Moving a subscription **between management groups** keeps RBAC intact (only inherited MG-level assignments change). Moving a subscription **between tenants** wipes all RBAC assignments and requires re-doing them.

---

## 2.13 Azure Policy

**Azure Policy enforces standards and assesses compliance.** RBAC says *who* can do things; **Policy says *what* can be done.**

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Policy definition** | A JSON rule: `if` condition `then` effect. |
| **Initiative / Policy set** | A named group of policy definitions deployed together. |
| **Assignment** | Applying a definition or initiative at a scope (MG, sub, RG, resource). |
| **Scope** | Where the assignment applies; inherits downward. |
| **Exclusion** | A child scope removed from an assignment. |
| **Exemption** | A first-class resource that waives a specific resource from an assignment, with a reason (`Waiver` or `Mitigated`) and optional expiry. |
| **Effect** | What happens on a match: Deny, Audit, Append, Modify, DeployIfNotExists, AuditIfNotExists, Disabled, Manual, DenyAction. |
| **Compliance state** | Compliant / Non-compliant / Exempt / Conflicting / Not started / Not registered. |
| **Evaluation** | Runs on resource create/update (immediate), on assignment change (~30 min), and on a **24-hour** cycle. |
| **Remediation task** | A job that applies `Modify` / `DeployIfNotExists` to **existing** resources. |
| **Managed identity** | **Required** for `Modify` and `DeployIfNotExists` assignments. |
| **Parameters** | Make a definition reusable (e.g., a list of allowed locations). |
| **Aliases** | The property paths Policy can evaluate, e.g., `Microsoft.Storage/storageAccounts/supportsHttpsTrafficOnly`. |
| **Compliance scan** | On-demand trigger: `az policy state trigger-scan`. |

### 📌 Policy Effects — memorize this table

| Effect | What it does | Order of evaluation | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Disabled** | Turns the rule off | Evaluated first | Useful for testing |
| **Append** | **Adds** fields/tags to a request | Before request | Does not modify existing resources |
| **Modify** | **Adds/updates/removes** properties or tags | Before request | ✅ Works on existing via remediation; **needs managed identity** |
| **Deny** | **Blocks** the create/update request | After Append/Modify | The enforcement hammer |
| **Audit** | Allows, but flags as non-compliant | After the request | No enforcement |
| **AuditIfNotExists** | Audits when a **related** resource is missing | After the request | e.g., "VM has no diagnostic extension" |
| **DeployIfNotExists (DINE)** | **Deploys** a related resource if missing | After the request | ✅ Remediable; **needs managed identity** |
| **DenyAction** | Blocks a specific **action** (esp. `delete`) | With Deny | Newer effect |
| **Manual** | Sets compliance manually (attestations) | — | For non-technical controls |

**Order:** `Disabled → Append/Modify → Deny → Audit → AuditIfNotExists/DeployIfNotExists`

### 📌 The three biggest Policy traps

1. **Policy does NOT retroactively delete or fix non-compliant resources.** A new `Deny` policy leaves existing violators alone — it just marks them **non-compliant**. New/updated resources get blocked.
2. **`Modify` and `DeployIfNotExists` require a managed identity** with the right role (e.g., Contributor or a specific role) on the assignment scope. Portal offers to create it. Without it → remediation fails.
3. **Only `Modify` and `DeployIfNotExists` can be remediated.** `Deny` and `Audit` cannot remediate anything.

### Policy definition anatomy

```json
{
  "properties": {
    "displayName": "Require environment tag on resources",
    "policyType": "Custom",
    "mode": "Indexed",
    "description": "Denies resource creation without an 'environment' tag.",
    "parameters": {
      "tagName": {
        "type": "String",
        "metadata": { "displayName": "Tag Name" },
        "defaultValue": "environment"
      }
    },
    "policyRule": {
      "if": {
        "field": "[concat('tags[', parameters('tagName'), ']')]",
        "exists": "false"
      },
      "then": { "effect": "deny" }
    }
  }
}
```

| Property | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `mode: All` | Evaluates resource groups, subscriptions, **and all resource types** |
| `mode: Indexed` | Evaluates **only resource types that support tags and location**. 📌 **Use Indexed for tag and location policies** |
| `mode: Microsoft.Kubernetes.Data` | AKS via Gatekeeper/OPA |
| `mode: Microsoft.KeyVault.Data` | Key Vault objects |

⚠️ **Trap:** If you write a tag policy with `mode: All`, it will flag resource types that *cannot have tags* as non-compliant. Use **Indexed**.

### Common built-in policies to recognize

| Policy | Effect |
|---|---|
| Allowed locations | Deny |
| Allowed virtual machine size SKUs | Deny |
| Allowed resource types / Not allowed resource types | Deny |
| Require a tag on resources | Deny |
| Inherit a tag from the resource group | Modify |
| Add a tag to resources | Modify |
| Secure transfer to storage accounts should be enabled | Audit/Deny |
| Deploy Log Analytics agent to VMs | DeployIfNotExists |
| Configure diagnostic settings to Log Analytics | DeployIfNotExists |
| Audit VMs that do not use managed disks | Audit |
| MFA should be enabled on accounts with owner permissions | AuditIfNotExists |

### Initiatives you should recognize

| Initiative | Purpose |
|---|---|
| **Microsoft cloud security benchmark** | Default Defender for Cloud initiative |
| **NIST SP 800-53 Rev. 5** | US federal compliance |
| **ISO 27001:2013** | International security standard |
| **PCI DSS v4** | Payment card |
| **HIPAA / HITRUST** | Healthcare |
| **CIS Microsoft Azure Foundations Benchmark** | Hardening baseline |

### Commands

```bash
# Create a custom definition at MG scope
az policy definition create --name "require-env-tag" \
  --display-name "Require environment tag" \
  --rules ./rule.json --params ./params.json \
  --mode Indexed \
  --management-group "Corp"

# Assign
az policy assignment create --name "require-env-tag-prod" \
  --policy "require-env-tag" \
  --scope "/subscriptions/{subId}" \
  --params '{"tagName":{"value":"environment"}}'

# Assign with a managed identity (needed for Modify/DINE)
az policy assignment create --name "inherit-tag" \
  --policy "<policyDefinitionId>" \
  --scope "/subscriptions/{subId}" \
  --mi-system-assigned --location eastus \
  --role Contributor --identity-scope "/subscriptions/{subId}"

# Exclusion
az policy assignment create --name "allowed-locations" --policy "<id>" \
  --scope "/subscriptions/{subId}" \
  --not-scopes "/subscriptions/{subId}/resourceGroups/rg-sandbox"

# Compliance
az policy state list --resource-group rg-prod -o table
az policy state summarize --subscription <subId>
az policy state trigger-scan --resource-group rg-prod

# Remediation
az policy remediation create --name "remediate-tags" \
  --policy-assignment "inherit-tag" --resource-group rg-prod

# Exemption
az policy exemption create --name "legacy-waiver" \
  --policy-assignment "<assignmentId>" \
  --exemption-category Waiver \
  --expires-on "2026-12-31" \
  --scope "/subscriptions/{sub}/resourceGroups/rg-legacy"
```

```powershell
$def = New-AzPolicyDefinition -Name "require-env-tag" -Policy ./rule.json -Mode Indexed
New-AzPolicyAssignment -Name "require-env-tag-prod" -PolicyDefinition $def -Scope "/subscriptions/{subId}"
Get-AzPolicyState -ResourceGroupName rg-prod
Start-AzPolicyComplianceScan -ResourceGroupName rg-prod
Start-AzPolicyRemediation -Name "fix-tags" -PolicyAssignmentId $assignment.PolicyAssignmentId
```

### 📌 RBAC vs Policy — the comparison question

| | Azure RBAC | Azure Policy |
|---|---|---|
| Question answered | **Who** can act? | **What** can exist / be configured? |
| Focus | Identity | Resource properties |
| Default | Deny (nothing until granted) | Allow (everything until restricted) |
| Applies to | Security principals | All requests, **including Owners** |
| Can a subscription Owner bypass it? | N/A | **❌ No — Policy applies to everyone** |
| Evaluated | At request authorization | At request + continuously |

⚠️ **Trap:** "An Owner tries to create a VM in a blocked region." → **Policy denies it.** RBAC role has no bearing. Policy is not an identity control.

### Azure Blueprints (legacy — being deprecated in favor of Deployment Stacks / Template Specs)

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Blueprint** | A package of artifacts: role assignments, policy assignments, ARM templates, resource groups. |
| **Artifact** | One item in the package. |
| **Blueprint assignment** | Deploying the package to a subscription. |
| **Blueprint lock** | `Don't Lock` / `Read Only` / `Do Not Delete` — implemented as **deny assignments**. |
| Status | **Deprecated**; Microsoft recommends **Template Specs + Deployment Stacks**. |

📌 Blueprint locks are the practical way you *encounter* deny assignments as an admin.

---

## 2.14 Resource Locks

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Resource lock** | A management setting preventing accidental deletion or modification. |
| **CanNotDelete (Delete)** | Read and modify allowed; **delete blocked**. |
| **ReadOnly** | Read allowed; **create, update, and delete blocked**. Equivalent to Reader for everyone. |
| **Lock scope** | Subscription, resource group, or resource. |
| **Inheritance** | Locks inherit **downward**. The **most restrictive** lock wins. |
| **Who can manage locks** | Requires `Microsoft.Authorization/locks/*` — held by **Owner** and **User Access Administrator** only. |

### 📌 Lock facts

| Fact | Detail |
|---|---|
| Applies to | **Everyone**, including Owners and Global Admins |
| Bypass? | ❌ No. You must **remove the lock first**. |
| Inheritance | Parent → child; **most restrictive wins** |
| Affects data plane? | ⚠️ **Sometimes.** ReadOnly on a storage account **blocks listing keys** (it's a POST), which breaks apps. |
| Contributor can manage locks? | ❌ **No** — Contributor has `Microsoft.Authorization/*/Write` in NotActions |
| Deleting an RG with a locked resource | ❌ Fails |
| Does a lock stop cost? | ❌ No — a locked VM still bills |

### ⚠️ The famous ReadOnly side-effects

- **Storage account + ReadOnly** → `listKeys` is a POST operation → **blocked** → apps using keys fail, and the portal can't show blobs.
- **VM + ReadOnly** → cannot start/stop/restart (those are POST actions).
- **Cosmos DB + ReadOnly** → replicas can't be added.
- **App Service + ReadOnly** → many operations break.

💡 **Rule of thumb:** `CanNotDelete` is safe and common. `ReadOnly` is a footgun — use it sparingly.

### Commands

```bash
az lock create --name "no-delete-prod" --lock-type CanNotDelete --resource-group rg-prod
az lock create --name "ro-vm" --lock-type ReadOnly \
  --resource-group rg-prod --resource-name vm01 \
  --resource-type Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachines
az lock list --resource-group rg-prod -o table
az lock delete --name "no-delete-prod" --resource-group rg-prod
```

```powershell
New-AzResourceLock -LockName "no-delete-prod" -LockLevel CanNotDelete -ResourceGroupName rg-prod
Get-AzResourceLock -ResourceGroupName rg-prod
Remove-AzResourceLock -LockName "no-delete-prod" -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -Force
```

⚠️ **Trap:** "Contributor cannot delete the lock." → Correct. Only **Owner** or **User Access Administrator**.

---

## 2.15 Tags

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Tag** | A name/value pair attached to a resource, RG, or subscription for organization and cost reporting. |
| **Tag inheritance** | ❌ **NOT automatic.** Resources do **not** inherit RG tags by default. |
| **Inherit a tag from the resource group** | The **Azure Policy** (`Modify` effect) that makes inheritance happen. |
| **Cost allocation tag** | A tag surfaced in Cost Management for chargeback. |

### 📌 Tag limits — memorize

| Limit | Value |
|---|---|
| Tags per resource/RG/subscription | **50** |
| Tag **name** length | **512** characters (128 for storage accounts) |
| Tag **value** length | **256** characters |
