| Case sensitivity | Tag **names** are case-**insensitive**; tag **values** are case-**sensitive** |
| Inheritance | **None** — child resources do NOT inherit RG/subscription tags |
| Can all resources be tagged? | ❌ No — classic resources and some types cannot |
| Special characters not allowed in names | `< > % & \ ? /` |

⚠️ **THE tag trap:** *"Resources inherit tags from their resource group."* → **FALSE.** Use the built-in policy **"Inherit a tag from the resource group"** with the `Modify` effect, then run a **remediation task** to apply it to existing resources.

### Common tagging taxonomy

| Tag | Example values |
|---|---|
| `environment` | prod, dev, test, staging |
| `costCenter` | CC-1042 |
| `owner` | alice@contoso.com |
| `application` | payments-api |
| `dataClassification` | public, internal, confidential |
| `businessUnit` | finance |
| `expiryDate` | 2026-12-31 |

### Commands

```bash
# Replace ALL tags (destructive)
az tag create --resource-id <resourceId> --tags environment=prod costCenter=CC-1042

# Merge / update / delete specific tags (non-destructive)
az tag update --resource-id <resourceId> --operation Merge  --tags owner=alice
az tag update --resource-id <resourceId> --operation Delete --tags owner=alice
az tag update --resource-id <resourceId> --operation Replace --tags environment=prod

# Query by tag
az resource list --tag environment=prod -o table
```

```powershell
# ⚠️ Set-AzResource -Tag REPLACES all tags
Set-AzResource -ResourceId <id> -Tag @{ environment="prod"; costCenter="CC-1042" } -Force

# Merge safely
Update-AzTag -ResourceId <id> -Tag @{ owner="alice" } -Operation Merge
Update-AzTag -ResourceId <id> -Tag @{ owner="alice" } -Operation Delete

Get-AzResource -Tag @{ environment="prod" }
Get-AzTag
```

📌 **`Merge` vs `Replace`** is exam-relevant: `Replace` wipes existing tags.

---

## 2.16 Cost Management and Budgets

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Cost Management + Billing** | The portal blade for analyzing, forecasting, and controlling spend. |
| **Cost analysis** | Interactive charts: group by resource, RG, tag, service, location. |
| **Budget** | A spending threshold with **alerts**. ⚠️ **Budgets do NOT stop spending** — they only notify (unless you wire an Action Group to an automation runbook). |
| **Budget scope** | Management group, subscription, RG, or billing scope. |
| **Budget reset period** | Monthly / Quarterly / Annually. |
| **Alert condition** | Percentage of budget: **Actual** cost or **Forecasted** cost. |
| **Action group** | Optional hook to trigger automation (e.g., shut down VMs). |
| **Cost alert** | Notification when a threshold is hit. |
| **Credit alert** | For EA/sponsored — when credit runs low. |
| **Spending quota alert** | EA departmental quota alert. |
| **Pricing calculator** | Estimates cost of a *planned* deployment. |
| **TCO calculator** | Compares **on-prem vs Azure** total cost of ownership. |
| **Azure Advisor** | Recommendations across Cost, Security, Reliability, Performance, Operational Excellence. |
| **Reserved Instance (RI)** | 1- or 3-year commitment; up to **~72%** savings vs PAYG. |
| **Savings Plan for compute** | Hourly spend commitment (1/3 yr); more flexible than RI, up to ~65%. |
| **Azure Hybrid Benefit (AHB)** | Reuse on-prem Windows Server / SQL Server licenses with Software Assurance — up to **~40–85%** savings. |
| **Spot VM** | Unused capacity at deep discount (up to 90%); **can be evicted with 30 seconds notice**. |
| **Dev/Test pricing** | Discounted rates for non-production (requires eligible subscription). |
| **Auto-shutdown** | Schedule VM deallocation — a simple huge saver. |
| **Amortized vs Actual cost** | Amortized spreads a reservation purchase across its term; Actual shows the lump sum on purchase day. |

### 📌 The budget trap

> **A budget does NOT prevent spending. It only sends alerts.**

To actually stop spend, you must attach an **Action Group** that triggers an **Automation runbook** or **Logic App** to deallocate resources. This is the single most common Cost Management exam question.

### Cost-saving levers ranked

| Lever | Typical saving | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Delete unused resources (orphaned disks, unattached PIPs) | 100% | Advisor finds these |
| Right-size / resize VMs | 30–60% | Advisor recommends |
| Auto-shutdown dev VMs | ~65% (nights + weekends) | Free to configure |
| Spot VMs | up to 90% | Interruptible only |
| Reserved Instances (3-yr) | up to 72% | Commitment |
| Savings Plan (3-yr) | up to 65% | More flexible than RI |
| Azure Hybrid Benefit | up to 85% (with RI) | Needs Software Assurance |
| Right storage tier (Hot→Cool→Archive) | 50–90% | Use lifecycle rules |
| B-series burstable VMs | varies | For low-average-CPU workloads |

⚠️ **Trap: Deallocated vs Stopped VM.**
- **Stopped (from inside the OS)** → status "Stopped" → **you still pay for compute!**
- **Stopped (deallocated)** (via portal/CLI) → status "Stopped (deallocated)" → **no compute charge**, you still pay for **disks** and any **static PIP**.

### Commands

```bash
az consumption budget create --budget-name "monthly-prod" \
  --amount 5000 --time-grain Monthly \
  --start-date 2026-07-01 --end-date 2027-07-01 \
  --resource-group rg-prod

az consumption usage list --start-date 2026-07-01 --end-date 2026-07-31 -o table
az costmanagement query --type Usage --scope "/subscriptions/{subId}" --timeframe MonthToDate
```

```powershell
New-AzConsumptionBudget -Name "monthly-prod" -Amount 5000 -Category Cost `
  -TimeGrain Monthly -StartDate 2026-07-01 -EndDate 2027-07-01 `
  -ContactEmail "finops@contoso.com" -NotificationKey "Actual_GreaterThan_80_Percent" `
  -NotificationThreshold 80

Get-AzConsumptionUsageDetail -StartDate 2026-07-01 -EndDate 2026-07-31
```

---

## 2.17 Moving Resources

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Move operation** | Relocating a resource to a different RG or subscription. |
| **Validate move** | A preflight API check that reports blockers before you commit. |
| **Source/target lock** | Both source and destination RGs are **locked** during the move (~4 hours max). |
| **Move across regions** | ⚠️ Moving between RGs/subscriptions **does NOT change the region**. To change region you must **redeploy** or use **Azure Resource Mover / ASR**. |

### 📌 Rules

1. Both source and target must be **in the same Entra tenant** (for subscription moves).
2. Both must have the **resource provider registered**.
3. RBAC assignments do **NOT** move with the resource — they must be recreated.
4. The resource's **region never changes** during a move.
5. Some resources **cannot be moved at all**.
6. During the move, the source and target RGs are **locked** (no writes).

### 📌 Resources that CANNOT be moved (recognize these)

- Virtual Networks with **VNet peerings** (delete peering first)
- **VPN Gateways** and **ExpressRoute circuits** (in most cases)
- **Application Gateways**
- **Public IPs with a Basic SKU** (some scenarios) and **Standard SKU PIPs across subscriptions**
- **Recovery Services vaults with registered items**
- **Azure Bastion**
- **Managed disks with snapshots/images** attached in complex ways
- **AKS clusters** (some scenarios)
- Certificates in App Service
- **Availability sets** — must move **with** all the VMs inside them
- **NSGs / route tables** referenced by a subnet in a different RG being moved separately

### Commands

```bash
# Validate first — always
az resource invoke-action --action validateMoveResources \
  --ids "/subscriptions/{sub}/resourceGroups/rg-source" \
  --request-body '{ "resources": ["<resourceId>"], "targetResourceGroup": "/subscriptions/{sub}/resourceGroups/rg-target" }'

# Move to another RG
az resource move --destination-group rg-target --ids <resourceId1> <resourceId2>

# Move to another subscription
az resource move --destination-group rg-target \
  --destination-subscription-id <targetSubId> --ids <resourceId>
```

```powershell
Move-AzResource -DestinationResourceGroupName "rg-target" -ResourceId <id>
Move-AzResource -DestinationSubscriptionId <subId> -DestinationResourceGroupName "rg-target" -ResourceId <id>
```

⚠️ **Trap:** "Move a VM from East US to West Europe using Move-AzResource." → **Not possible.** Moves never change region. Use **Azure Resource Mover** or ASR, or redeploy.

---
---

# Part 3 — Domain 2: Implement and Manage Storage (15–20%)

## 3.1 Storage Account Fundamentals

**A storage account is a container that provides a globally unique namespace for your data.**

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Storage account** | The top-level resource providing a unique namespace, accessible over HTTP/HTTPS. |
| **Account name** | 3–24 characters, **lowercase letters and numbers only**, **globally unique across all of Azure**. |
| **Endpoint** | The URL for a service: `https://<account>.blob.core.windows.net`. |
| **Performance tier: Standard** | Backed by HDD (magnetic). General purpose, cheapest. |
| **Performance tier: Premium** | Backed by SSD. Low latency, high IOPS. Different account kinds per service. |
| **Account kind: StorageV2 (general-purpose v2)** | 📌 **The recommended default.** Supports blobs, files, queues, tables, all tiers, all redundancy. |
| **Account kind: StorageV1** | Legacy GPv1. No access tiers, no lifecycle management. |
| **Account kind: BlockBlobStorage** | Premium, block/append blobs only. High transaction rate, low latency. |
| **Account kind: FileStorage** | Premium, Azure Files only. |
| **Account kind: BlobStorage** | Legacy blob-only. |
| **Hierarchical namespace (HNS)** | Enables **Azure Data Lake Storage Gen2**: true directories, atomic renames, POSIX ACLs. |
| **Data Lake Storage Gen2 (ADLS Gen2)** | = StorageV2 + HNS enabled. |
| **Flat namespace** | Default blob behavior — "folders" are just name prefixes with `/`, not real directories. |
| **Container** | The blob-service grouping. Names: 3–63 chars, lowercase, numbers, hyphens. |
| **`$root` container** | Special container allowing blobs at the account root URL. |
| **`$logs` container** | Where classic storage analytics logging writes. |
| **Access tier** | Hot / Cool / Cold / Archive — cost/access tradeoff. |
| **Scalability target** | ~**5 PiB** max capacity per account; ~**20,000 IOPS** for standard; **500 TiB** default egress limits vary by region. |
| **Storage Explorer** | Free desktop app (and portal blade) for browsing/managing storage. |

### 📌 Which account kind?

| Need | Choose |
|---|---|
| Anything general | **StorageV2 (Standard)** |
| Data Lake / analytics | StorageV2 + **HNS enabled** |
| High transaction rate, small blobs, low latency | **Premium BlockBlobStorage** |
| High-performance SMB/NFS file shares | **Premium FileStorage** |
| Page blobs / unmanaged disks | Premium **Storage (page blobs)** |

⚠️ **Trap:** You **can** upgrade GPv1 → GPv2 in place (no downtime). You **cannot** downgrade GPv2 → GPv1 in the portal, and you **cannot** convert Standard ↔ Premium — that requires copying data to a new account.

⚠️ **Trap:** **Hierarchical namespace can only be enabled at creation time.** (Migration is possible via a special one-way upgrade path, but you cannot toggle it off.)

### Storage services in one account

| Service | Stores | Endpoint | Max object size |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Blob** | Unstructured objects | `.blob.core.windows.net` | Block blob ~190.7 TiB |
| **Files** | SMB/NFS file shares | `.file.core.windows.net` | Share up to 100 TiB (large file shares) |
| **Queue** | Messages for decoupling | `.queue.core.windows.net` | **64 KB** per message |
| **Table** | NoSQL key-attribute store | `.table.core.windows.net` | 1 MB per entity |
| **Disk** | Managed disks (separate resource) | n/a | 32 TiB |

📌 **Queue message max = 64 KB, max TTL = 7 days by default (can be −1 for infinite).** A classic recall question.

### Commands

```bash
az storage account create \
  --name stcontosoprod001 \
  --resource-group rg-storage \
  --location eastus \
  --sku Standard_LRS \
  --kind StorageV2 \
  --access-tier Hot \
  --min-tls-version TLS1_2 \
  --https-only true \
  --allow-blob-public-access false

az storage account list -o table
az storage account show --name stcontosoprod001 --resource-group rg-storage
az storage account check-name --name stcontosoprod001   # globally unique?
az storage account update --name stcontosoprod001 -g rg-storage --sku Standard_GRS
az storage account delete --name stcontosoprod001 -g rg-storage --yes
```

```powershell
New-AzStorageAccount -ResourceGroupName rg-storage -Name stcontosoprod001 `
  -Location eastus -SkuName Standard_LRS -Kind StorageV2 -AccessTier Hot `
  -EnableHttpsTrafficOnly $true -MinimumTlsVersion TLS1_2 -AllowBlobPublicAccess $false

Get-AzStorageAccount -ResourceGroupName rg-storage
Get-AzStorageAccountNameAvailability -Name stcontosoprod001
Set-AzStorageAccount -ResourceGroupName rg-storage -Name stcontosoprod001 -SkuName Standard_GRS
```

---

## 3.2 Redundancy Options

**This is the most predictably tested storage topic. Learn the table cold.**

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **LRS** (Locally Redundant Storage) | **3 copies** in a **single datacenter** in one region. Synchronous. |
| **ZRS** (Zone-Redundant Storage) | **3 copies** across **3 Availability Zones** in one region. Synchronous. |
| **GRS** (Geo-Redundant Storage) | LRS in primary (3 copies) + **asynchronous** replication to the paired region where it's LRS again = **6 copies total**. |
| **GZRS** (Geo-Zone-Redundant Storage) | ZRS in primary (3 copies across zones) + async to paired region as LRS = **6 copies**. The most durable. |
| **RA-GRS** | GRS + **read access** to the secondary endpoint. |
| **RA-GZRS** | GZRS + read access to the secondary. |
| **Primary endpoint** | `https://<acct>.blob.core.windows.net` |
| **Secondary endpoint** | `https://<acct>-secondary.blob.core.windows.net` — **read-only**, exists only with RA-GRS/RA-GZRS. |
| **Durability** | Probability the data survives, expressed as "nines". |
| **Failover** | Promoting the secondary to primary. **Customer-managed failover** is manual; account becomes LRS afterward. |
| **Last Sync Time** | Timestamp showing how current the secondary is. Data written after it may be **lost** on failover. |
| **RPO for geo-replication** | **< 15 minutes** (async, not guaranteed). |

### 📌 THE redundancy table

| Option | Copies | Spread | Sync? | Durability | Secondary readable? | Protects against |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **LRS** | 3 | 1 datacenter, 1 region | Sync | 11 nines (99.999999999%) | ❌ | Disk/rack/server failure |
| **ZRS** | 3 | 3 AZs, 1 region | Sync | 12 nines | ❌ | **Datacenter/zone failure** |
| **GRS** | 6 | 1 DC primary + 1 DC secondary region | Async to secondary | 16 nines | ❌ | **Regional outage** |
| **RA-GRS** | 6 | same as GRS | Async | 16 nines | ✅ **Read-only** | Regional outage + read scale |
| **GZRS** | 6 | 3 AZs primary + 1 DC secondary region | Sync in-region, async cross-region | 16 nines | ❌ | Zone **and** regional |
| **RA-GZRS** | 6 | same as GZRS | same | 16 nines | ✅ **Read-only** | Zone + region + read scale |

### 📌 The decision tree

```
Do you need protection from a REGIONAL outage?
├── NO
│   └── Do you need protection from a ZONE (datacenter) failure?
│       ├── NO  → LRS   (cheapest)
│       └── YES → ZRS
└── YES
    └── Do you also need zone protection in the primary?
        ├── NO  → GRS   (or RA-GRS if you need read access to secondary)
        └── YES → GZRS  (or RA-GZRS if you need read access to secondary)
```

### ⚠️ The four redundancy traps

1. **The secondary in GRS is NOT readable.** Only **RA-GRS** / **RA-GZRS** give you the `-secondary` endpoint. If a question says "read data during a regional outage without waiting for failover" → the answer is **RA-GRS** or **RA-GZRS**.
2. **Geo-replication is asynchronous.** RPO < 15 min. In a failover, recent writes **can be lost**. `Last Sync Time` tells you the exposure.
3. **After a customer-initiated failover, the account becomes LRS.** You must manually re-enable geo-redundancy, and re-replication costs money and time.
4. **The paired region is fixed.** You cannot choose it.

### 📌 What supports what

| Constraint | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Premium block blobs** | LRS and ZRS only — **no GRS** |
| **Premium file shares** | LRS and ZRS only — **no GRS** |
| **Premium page blobs** | **LRS only** |
| **Managed disks** | LRS or ZRS (ZRS on limited SKUs/regions) — **not GRS** |
| **Archive tier** | LRS, GRS, RA-GRS — **NOT ZRS/GZRS/RA-GZRS** |
| **NFS 3.0 / SFTP** | LRS, ZRS |
| **Standard GPv2** | All six options |

⚠️ **Trap: "Configure GRS for a premium file share."** → **Impossible.** Premium files support LRS/ZRS only. Use standard, or use Azure File Sync / Backup for geo-protection.

⚠️ **Trap: "Archive tier + ZRS."** → **Not supported.**

### Conversions allowed

| From → To | How |
|---|---|
| LRS ↔ GRS ↔ RA-GRS | Portal/CLI, instant (async re-replication) |
| ZRS ↔ GZRS ↔ RA-GZRS | Portal/CLI |
| **LRS → ZRS** | **Customer-initiated conversion** or **manual migration** (copy data). Not always instant. |
| GRS → GZRS | Requires a conversion request |
| Premium ↔ Standard | ❌ Must create a new account and copy |

```bash
az storage account update -n stacct -g rg --sku Standard_RAGRS
az storage account show -n stacct -g rg --query "statusOfPrimary, statusOfSecondary, geoReplicationStats.lastSyncTime"
az storage account failover -n stacct -g rg   # ⚠️ destructive; account becomes LRS
```

---

## 3.3 Blob Storage

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Blob** | Binary Large Object — any unstructured file. |
| **Container** | Groups blobs. Flat — cannot nest containers. |
| **Block blob** | 📌 **Default.** Made of blocks. Optimized for **upload and streaming** — documents, images, backups, video. Max ~**190.7 TiB**. |
| **Append blob** | Optimized for **append-only** writes — **logging**. Cannot modify/delete existing blocks. Max ~195 GiB. |
| **Page blob** | Random read/write in 512-byte pages. Backs **unmanaged VHDs / managed disks**. Max **8 TiB** (page blob), managed disks up to 32 TiB. |
| **Block** | A chunk of a block blob, max 4000 MiB; up to **50,000 blocks** per blob. |
| **Blob prefix** | `folder1/folder2/file.txt` — looks like a path but the namespace is flat (unless HNS). |
| **Virtual directory** | The illusion of folders created by prefixes. |
| **Metadata** | User-defined key/value pairs on a blob or container. |
| **Blob index tags** | Searchable key/value tags (max 10 per blob) enabling `Find Blobs by Tags`. |
| **Public access level** | `Private` (default) / `Blob` (anonymous read blobs only) / `Container` (anonymous read + **list** blobs). |
| **Anonymous access** | Public read without credentials. Governed by the account-level `allowBlobPublicAccess` flag. |
| **Static website** | Serve HTML from the special **`$web`** container. |
| **Blob leasing** | An exclusive write lock, 15–60 seconds or infinite. |
| **Object replication** | Async copy of block blobs between accounts (needs versioning + change feed). |
| **Change feed** | An ordered, durable log of all blob changes. |
| **Blob inventory** | Scheduled report of all blobs and their properties. |

### 📌 The three blob types — the classic question

| Type | Use for | Key trait |
|---|---|---|
| **Block** | Files, images, backups, video, most things | Default; parallel upload |
| **Append** | **Log files**, audit trails | Write **only at the end** |
| **Page** | **VHDs / disks**, random-access data | 512-byte page random I/O |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Which blob type for an application writing continuous log entries?" → **Append blob.**
⚠️ **Trap:** "Which blob type backs a virtual machine disk?" → **Page blob.**

### 📌 Public access levels

| Level | Anonymous can read blobs? | Anonymous can list container contents? |
|---|---|---|
| **Private (no anonymous access)** | ❌ | ❌ |
| **Blob** | ✅ (if they know the exact URL) | ❌ |
| **Container** | ✅ | ✅ |

📌 **Two-step gate:** the account-level toggle **`allowBlobPublicAccess`** must be **true** *before* container-level `Blob`/`Container` settings do anything. If the account toggle is off, everything is private regardless of the container setting.

⚠️ **Trap:** "Users can't access the public container even though it's set to Blob." → Check **`allowBlobPublicAccess`** on the account. Since ~2023 it defaults to **false** for new accounts.

### Static website hosting

| Fact | Detail |
|---|---|
| Container name | **`$web`** (auto-created, exact name) |
| Endpoint | `https://<account>.z13.web.core.windows.net` — a **separate** endpoint from `.blob.` |
| Index document | e.g., `index.html` |
| Error document path | e.g., `404.html` |
| Custom domain HTTPS | ❌ Not natively — put **Azure CDN** or **Front Door** in front |
| Server-side code | ❌ Static only |
| Auth | ❌ Anonymous read only |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Static website with a custom domain over HTTPS." → **Requires Azure CDN or Front Door.** Blob static websites cannot do custom-domain HTTPS on their own.

### Commands

```bash
# Container
az storage container create --name mycontainer \
  --account-name stacct --auth-mode login --public-access off

az storage container list --account-name stacct --auth-mode login -o table
az storage container set-permission --name mycontainer --public-access blob --account-name stacct

# Upload / download
az storage blob upload --account-name stacct --container-name mycontainer \
  --name report.pdf --file ./report.pdf --auth-mode login --tier Hot

az storage blob upload-batch --destination mycontainer --source ./localdir --account-name stacct

az storage blob download --account-name stacct -c mycontainer -n report.pdf -f ./report.pdf
az storage blob download-batch -d ./localdir -s mycontainer --account-name stacct

az storage blob list --account-name stacct -c mycontainer --auth-mode login -o table
az storage blob delete --account-name stacct -c mycontainer -n report.pdf

# Static website
az storage blob service-properties update --account-name stacct \
  --static-website --index-document index.html --404-document 404.html
```

```powershell
$ctx = (Get-AzStorageAccount -ResourceGroupName rg -Name stacct).Context
New-AzStorageContainer -Name mycontainer -Context $ctx -Permission Off
Set-AzStorageBlobContent -File ./report.pdf -Container mycontainer -Blob report.pdf -Context $ctx -StandardBlobTier Hot
Get-AzStorageBlob -Container mycontainer -Context $ctx
Get-AzStorageBlobContent -Container mycontainer -Blob report.pdf -Destination ./ -Context $ctx
Remove-AzStorageBlob -Container mycontainer -Blob report.pdf -Context $ctx
Enable-AzStorageStaticWebsite -Context $ctx -IndexDocument index.html -ErrorDocument404Path 404.html
```

📌 `--auth-mode login` uses your **Entra identity** instead of the account key. `--auth-mode key` (default in older CLI) uses keys.

---

## 3.4 Access Tiers and Lifecycle Management

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Hot** | Highest storage cost, **lowest access cost**. Frequently accessed data. |
| **Cool** | Lower storage cost, higher access cost. **30-day** minimum. Infrequent access. |
| **Cold** | Between Cool and Archive. **90-day** minimum. Rarely accessed but needs to be online. |
| **Archive** | **Lowest storage cost, highest access cost.** **180-day** minimum. **OFFLINE** — must rehydrate to read. |
| **Account default tier** | Hot or Cool — applies to blobs with no explicit tier. |
| **Blob-level tiering** | Setting the tier per blob (block blobs only). |
| **Early deletion penalty** | Deleting/moving a blob before its minimum period incurs a **pro-rated charge** for the remaining days. |
| **Rehydration** | Moving a blob from Archive back to Hot/Cool/Cold so it can be read. |
| **Rehydration priority: Standard** | Up to **15 hours**. |
| **Rehydration priority: High** | Under **1 hour** for blobs < 10 GB. Costs more. |
| **Lifecycle management** | Rule engine that automatically tiers or deletes blobs based on age. |
| **Rule** | JSON with filters (prefix, blob type, index tags) and actions. |
| **`daysAfterModificationGreaterThan`** | Age since last modified. |
| **`daysAfterLastAccessTimeGreaterThan`** | Age since last read — requires **last access time tracking** to be enabled. |
| **`daysAfterCreationGreaterThan`** | Used for snapshots and versions. |

### 📌 The tier table

| | Hot | Cool | Cold | Archive |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Storage cost | Highest | Lower | Lower still | **Lowest** |
| Access cost | **Lowest** | Higher | Higher | **Highest** |
| Minimum retention | none | **30 days** | **90 days** | **180 days** |
| Availability SLA | 99.9% (99.99% RA-GRS) | 99% (99.9% RA-GRS) | 99% | Offline |
| First-byte latency | ms | ms | ms | **hours** |
| Online? | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ **Offline** |
| Can set at account level? | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ **Blob-level only** |
| Supports ZRS/GZRS? | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ **No** |
| Blob types | Block | Block | Block | **Block + append (blob-level only)** |

### ⚠️ Archive traps

1. **Archive is offline.** You cannot read an archived blob. You must **rehydrate** first (Standard ≤15 h, High <1 h).
2. **Archive can only be set at the BLOB level**, never as an account default.
3. **You cannot rehydrate "in place" instantly** — either change the tier (takes hours) or **copy** the blob to a new online blob (the original stays archived).
4. **Archive + ZRS/GZRS is not supported.**
5. Minimum 180 days — deleting at day 30 charges you for 150 days.
6. **Archive is per-blob; it does not apply to page blobs** (so no archived VHDs).

### 📌 The tier movement matrix

| Move | Behavior |
|---|---|
| Hot → Cool → Cold → Archive | Instant metadata change; charged as a **write** to the destination tier |
| Archive → Hot/Cool/Cold | **Rehydration** — hours; charged as a **read** from Archive |
| Cool → Hot | Instant; charged as a read from Cool |

💡 Rule: **moving to a cooler tier costs a write; moving to a warmer tier costs a read.**

### Lifecycle management policy example

```json
{
  "rules": [
    {
      "enabled": true,
      "name": "tier-and-expire-logs",
      "type": "Lifecycle",
      "definition": {
        "filters": {
          "blobTypes": [ "blockBlob" ],
          "prefixMatch": [ "logs/" ],
          "blobIndexMatch": [ { "name": "project", "op": "==", "value": "alpha" } ]
        },
        "actions": {
          "baseBlob": {
            "tierToCool":    { "daysAfterModificationGreaterThan": 30 },
            "tierToCold":    { "daysAfterModificationGreaterThan": 90 },
            "tierToArchive": { "daysAfterModificationGreaterThan": 180 },
            "delete":        { "daysAfterModificationGreaterThan": 2555 }
          },
          "snapshot": {
            "delete": { "daysAfterCreationGreaterThan": 90 }
          },
          "version": {
            "tierToCool": { "daysAfterCreationGreaterThan": 30 },
            "delete":     { "daysAfterCreationGreaterThan": 365 }
          }
        }
      }
    }
  ]
}
```

### 📌 Lifecycle facts

| Fact | Detail |
|---|---|
| Max rules per policy | **100** |
| Available on | **GPv2, BlockBlobStorage, BlobStorage** — ❌ **not GPv1** |
| Evaluation cadence | Once per day; can take **up to 24–48 hours** to take effect after creating the policy |
| Filters available | `blobTypes` (required), `prefixMatch`, `blobIndexMatch` |
| Actions | `tierToCool`, `tierToCold`, `tierToArchive`, `delete`, `enableAutoTierToHotFromCool` |
| Applies to | baseBlob, snapshot, version |
| Cost | The lifecycle engine itself is free; you pay for the transactions it performs |

⚠️ **Trap:** "The lifecycle rule didn't run immediately." → Expected. It can take **up to 48 hours** for the first run.
⚠️ **Trap:** "Lifecycle management on a GPv1 account." → **Not supported.** Upgrade to GPv2.
⚠️ **Trap:** Using `daysAfterLastAccessTimeGreaterThan` requires **last access time tracking** to be explicitly enabled (it adds transaction cost).

```bash
az storage account management-policy create \
  --account-name stacct -g rg --policy @policy.json

az storage account blob-service-properties update \
  --account-name stacct -g rg --enable-last-access-tracking true

# Change a blob's tier
az storage blob set-tier --account-name stacct -c logs -n old.log --tier Archive
az storage blob set-tier --account-name stacct -c logs -n old.log --tier Hot --rehydrate-priority High
```

---

## 3.5 Blob Protection: Versioning, Snapshots, Soft Delete, Immutability

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Snapshot** | A **manual**, read-only, point-in-time copy of a blob. You create it explicitly. Identified by a `?snapshot=<timestamp>` query string. |
| **Version** | An **automatic** read-only copy created on **every write/delete** when versioning is enabled. Identified by a `versionId`. |
| **Current version** | The live blob. |
| **Previous version** | Any older automatically-retained copy. |
| **Blob soft delete** | Deleted blobs (and versions/snapshots) are retained and restorable for **1–365 days**. |
| **Container soft delete** | Deleted **containers** retained for **1–365 days**. |
| **Account soft delete** | Deleted **storage accounts** recoverable within **14 days** (only if no account with the same name has been created since). |
| **Point-in-time restore (PITR)** | Restores block blobs in a container to an earlier state. **Requires versioning + change feed + soft delete.** |
| **Immutable storage / WORM** | Write Once, Read Many. Blobs cannot be modified or deleted for a set period. |
| **Time-based retention policy** | Immutable for N days. |
| **Legal hold** | Immutable indefinitely until the named tag is cleared. |
| **Locked vs unlocked policy** | Unlocked = editable/deletable (for testing). **Locked = permanent** — you may only **increase** the retention period, up to **5 times**. |
| **Version-level immutability** | Immutability applied per blob version rather than per container. |
| **Change feed** | An append-only, ordered transaction log of blob changes; prerequisite for PITR and object replication. |

### 📌 Snapshot vs Version — the classic distinction

| | Snapshot | Version |
|---|---|---|
| Created by | **You, manually** | **Automatically on every write/delete** |
| Requires | Nothing | **Versioning enabled** |
| Identified by | `?snapshot=<datetime>` | `versionId` |
| Deleting the base blob | Must delete snapshots first (or use `include=snapshots`) | Versions survive; a delete marker is created |
| Cost | Only for **unique blocks** (differential) | Only for unique blocks |
| Use case | Controlled backup before a change | Automatic protection from overwrite/delete |
| Can be tiered by lifecycle | ✅ | ✅ |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Recover a blob that a user overwrote." → **Versioning** (soft delete alone does not protect against **overwrite** unless versioning is on — actually blob soft delete *does* keep a soft-deleted snapshot on overwrite, but versioning is the clean, recommended answer).

### 📌 Immutability rules

| Rule | Detail |
|---|---|
| Once **locked**, a time-based policy | ⚠️ **CANNOT be shortened or removed** |
| Extending a locked policy | Allowed, **max 5 times** |
| Deleting a container with locked immutable blobs | ❌ Blocked until retention expires |
| Deleting the **storage account** with locked immutable data | ❌ Blocked |
| Legal hold | Cleared by removing the tag; requires the appropriate data-plane permission |
| Applies to | Block blobs; container-level or version-level |
| Compliance targets | SEC 17a-4(f), CFTC, FINRA — this is why the exam frames it as "regulatory compliance" |

⚠️ **THE immutability trap:** "How do I delete data protected by a locked time-based retention policy?" → **You can't.** Not until the retention expires. Not even as Global Admin/Owner. That's the entire point of WORM.

### Enabling protection

```bash
# Blob soft delete (7 days)
az storage account blob-service-properties update -n stacct -g rg \
  --enable-delete-retention true --delete-retention-days 7

# Container soft delete
az storage account blob-service-properties update -n stacct -g rg \
  --enable-container-delete-retention true --container-delete-retention-days 7

# Versioning
az storage account blob-service-properties update -n stacct -g rg --enable-versioning true

# Change feed
az storage account blob-service-properties update -n stacct -g rg --enable-change-feed true

# Point-in-time restore (requires versioning + change feed + soft delete)
az storage account blob-service-properties update -n stacct -g rg \
  --enable-restore-policy true --restore-days 6

# ⚠️ restore-days must be LESS than delete-retention-days

# Restore
az storage blob restore --account-name stacct -g rg --time-to-restore "2026-07-10T10:00:00Z" \
  --blob-range "container1/" "container1/-"

# Snapshot
az storage blob snapshot --account-name stacct -c mycontainer -n report.pdf

# Immutability
az storage container immutability-policy create \
  --account-name stacct -c legal --period 365
az storage container immutability-policy lock \
  --account-name stacct -c legal --if-match <etag>
az storage container legal-hold set --account-name stacct -c legal --tags litigation2026
```

📌 **PITR prerequisite chain:** soft delete → versioning → change feed → **then** point-in-time restore. And `restore-days` must be **less than** `delete-retention-days`. This chain is exam-favorite material.

---

## 3.6 Azure Files

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Azure Files** | Fully managed file shares accessible via **SMB**, **NFS**, and **REST**. |
| **File share** | The SMB/NFS share inside a storage account. |
| **SMB 3.x** | The protocol used by Windows/Linux/macOS. Requires **port 445** outbound. |
| **NFS 4.1** | Linux-native protocol. **Premium FileStorage only**; requires **private endpoint** (no public access); **no AD auth** — uses host-based/network security. |
| **Standard file share** | HDD-backed, transaction-based pricing, in GPv2. |
| **Premium file share** | SSD-backed, **provisioned** capacity model, in FileStorage accounts. |
| **Large file shares** | Raises share limit from 5 TiB → **100 TiB**. Standard shares only; requires **LRS/ZRS**. |
| **Share snapshot** | Read-only point-in-time copy of the whole share. Up to **200** per share. |
| **Soft delete for shares** | 1–365 days recovery. |
| **Access tiers (standard)** | **Transaction optimized**, **Hot**, **Cool**. |
| **Identity-based auth** | Entra DS, **AD DS (on-prem)**, or **Entra Kerberos** — gives NTFS ACLs and per-user access. |
| **Storage account key auth** | Key acts as the share password. Everyone gets full access. |
| **NTFS ACL** | Windows file/folder permissions, honored by SMB when using identity-based auth. |
| **Share-level permission** | RBAC role: Storage File Data SMB Share **Reader / Contributor / Elevated Contributor**. |

### 📌 Port 445 — the #1 Azure Files troubleshooting answer

SMB requires **TCP port 445 outbound**. Most ISPs and many corporate firewalls **block it**.

| Symptom | Fix |
|---|---|
| Cannot mount SMB share from home/office | Port 445 blocked → use **VPN**, **ExpressRoute**, or a **private endpoint** with the traffic going over the tunnel |
| `Test-NetConnection <acct>.file.core.windows.net -Port 445` fails | Confirms the block |
| Works in Azure but not on-prem | Definitely a 445 issue |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Users cannot connect to the Azure file share from the corporate office." → **Port 445 is blocked.** The fix is *not* changing NSGs on a storage account (storage accounts don't have NSGs).

### 📌 Standard vs Premium file shares

| | Standard | Premium |
|---|---|---|
| Account kind | **StorageV2** | **FileStorage** |
| Media | HDD | SSD |
| Billing | Pay per GB used + transactions | **Provisioned** GB (pay for what you provision) |
| Max share size | 5 TiB (100 TiB with **large file shares**) | 100 TiB |
| Redundancy | LRS, ZRS, GRS, GZRS | **LRS, ZRS only** |
| Protocols | SMB, REST | SMB, **NFS**, REST |
| Latency | ms | sub-ms |
| IOPS | up to 20k (large shares) | up to 100k |
| Tiers | Transaction optimized / Hot / Cool | n/a (single tier) |

⚠️ **Trap:** **NFS requires Premium FileStorage** and **must** be accessed via a private endpoint (public endpoint not supported for NFS). NFS shares **cannot** use AD authentication and **cannot** be accessed from Windows via NFS in most exam framings.

### 📌 Identity-based authentication options

| Method | Where identities live | Use case |
|---|---|---|
| **On-premises AD DS** | Your DCs (synced to Entra via Entra Connect) | Hybrid orgs, most common |
| **Microsoft Entra Domain Services** | Managed domain in Azure | Cloud-only, need Kerberos |
| **Microsoft Entra Kerberos** | Entra ID directly | **Entra-joined / hybrid-joined Windows clients** — FSLogix profiles for AVD |
| **Storage account key** | The key | Simple, no per-user permissions ⚠️ |

📌 Access control is **two layers**:
1. **Share-level** = Azure RBAC role (Storage File Data SMB Share Reader/Contributor/Elevated Contributor).
2. **Directory/file-level** = **NTFS ACLs** set with `icacls` / Windows Explorer.
Both must permit the action.

### Mounting

```powershell
# Windows — key-based
$key = (Get-AzStorageAccountKey -ResourceGroupName rg -Name stacct)[0].Value
cmd /c "cmdkey /add:stacct.file.core.windows.net /user:localhost\stacct /pass:$key"
New-PSDrive -Name Z -PSProvider FileSystem -Root "\\stacct.file.core.windows.net\myshare" -Persist

# Test port 445
Test-NetConnection -ComputerName stacct.file.core.windows.net -Port 445
```

```bash
# Linux — SMB
sudo apt install cifs-utils
sudo mkdir -p /mnt/myshare
sudo mount -t cifs //stacct.file.core.windows.net/myshare /mnt/myshare \
  -o username=stacct,password=<key>,serverino,nosharesock,actimeo=30,mfsymlinks,vers=3.1.1

# Persist in /etc/fstab
//stacct.file.core.windows.net/myshare /mnt/myshare cifs credentials=/etc/smbcredentials/stacct.cred,dir_mode=0777,file_mode=0777,serverino,vers=3.1.1 0 0

# Linux — NFS (premium only, private endpoint required)
sudo mount -t nfs stacct.file.core.windows.net:/stacct/myshare /mnt/myshare -o vers=4,minorversion=1,sec=sys
```

```bash
az storage share-rm create --storage-account stacct -g rg --name myshare --quota 1024
az storage share-rm list --storage-account stacct -g rg -o table
az storage share snapshot --account-name stacct --name myshare
az storage account file-service-properties update -n stacct -g rg \
  --enable-delete-retention true --delete-retention-days 14
```

---

## 3.7 Azure File Sync

**Turns a Windows Server into a fast local cache of an Azure file share.**

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Storage Sync Service** | The top-level Azure resource for File Sync. |
| **Sync group** | Defines the sync topology — one cloud endpoint + one or more server endpoints. All endpoints in a group stay in sync. |
| **Cloud endpoint** | The **Azure file share**. ⚠️ **One cloud endpoint per sync group** — and a file share can only be in **one** sync group. |
| **Server endpoint** | A **path on a registered Windows Server** (a volume or folder). |
| **Registered server** | A Windows Server with the **Azure File Sync agent** installed and registered to the Storage Sync Service. |
| **Cloud tiering** | Files are **tiered to Azure**; a **reparse point** (pointer) stays on the server. Opening the file **recalls** it. |
| **Reparse point** | The NTFS stub left behind by cloud tiering; ~small size on disk. |
| **Volume free space policy** | Keep X% of the volume free by tiering the coldest files. |
| **Date policy** | Tier files not accessed in N days. |
| **Recall** | Downloading a tiered file back on access. |
| **Offline data transfer** | Seeding via Data Box. |
| **Multi-site sync** | Multiple branch servers sharing one cloud share. |

### 📌 File Sync facts

| Fact | Value |
|---|---|
| Cloud endpoints per sync group | **1** |
| Server endpoints per sync group | 100 |
| Sync groups per Storage Sync Service | 200 |
| Registered servers per Storage Sync Service | 100 |
| Server OS support | **Windows Server 2012 R2 – 2025** (Datacenter/Standard, **not** Windows client OS) |
| Filesystem | **NTFS only** — ReFS, FAT, and NFS are **not supported** |
| Volume | Cannot be the **system volume** in some agent versions; sysvol/boot excluded |
| Can a file share be in 2 sync groups? | ❌ **No** |
| Preserves ACLs? | ✅ **Yes** — NTFS ACLs, timestamps, and attributes are preserved |
| Sync frequency | Changes on the server detected ~immediately; cloud changes detected every **24 h** (change detection job) — a **manual** change detection can be triggered |
| Cloud tiering minimum | Files must be > **64 KB** to be tiered |

### 📌 The three big File Sync traps

1. **A file share can belong to only ONE sync group.** Trying to add it to a second fails.
2. **Cloud tiering leaves a reparse point** — free space on the server increases, but the file *appears* to still be there. Deleting the reparse point deletes the file everywhere.
3. **Changes made directly in the Azure file share** (portal/AzCopy) are only detected by the **24-hour change detection job**, unless you invoke it manually. Changes made **on the server** sync almost immediately.

⚠️ **Trap:** "I uploaded a file to the Azure file share via the portal but it hasn't appeared on the server." → Wait up to **24 hours**, or run `Invoke-AzStorageSyncChangeDetection`.

### Setup order 📌

```
1. Deploy the Storage Sync Service
2. Prepare the Windows Server (disable IE Enhanced Security, install PowerShell Az module)
3. Install the Azure File Sync agent
4. Register the server with the Storage Sync Service
5. Create a Sync Group (specify the storage account + file share = cloud endpoint)
6. Add a Server Endpoint (specify the local path)
7. (Optional) Enable cloud tiering with a volume free space and/or date policy
```

```powershell
Install-Module Az.StorageSync
New-AzStorageSyncService -ResourceGroupName rg -Name "sync-contoso" -Location eastus
New-AzStorageSyncGroup -ResourceGroupName rg -StorageSyncServiceName "sync-contoso" -Name "sg-files"

New-AzStorageSyncCloudEndpoint -ResourceGroupName rg -StorageSyncServiceName "sync-contoso" `
  -SyncGroupName "sg-files" -Name "cloud-ep" `
  -StorageAccountResourceId <saId> -AzureFileShareName "myshare"

New-AzStorageSyncServerEndpoint -ResourceGroupName rg -StorageSyncServiceName "sync-contoso" `
  -SyncGroupName "sg-files" -Name "server-ep" -ServerResourceId <serverId> `
  -ServerLocalPath "D:\Shares" -CloudTiering -VolumeFreeSpacePercent 20

# Force cloud change detection
Invoke-AzStorageSyncChangeDetection -ResourceGroupName rg -StorageSyncServiceName "sync-contoso" `
  -SyncGroupName "sg-files" -CloudEndpointName "cloud-ep" -Path "Folder1"
```

---

## 3.8 Storage Security

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Access key** | One of **two** 512-bit keys granting **full control** of the account. `key1` and `key2`. |
| **Key rotation** | Regenerate key1, update apps, regenerate key2 — the two keys exist to enable zero-downtime rotation. |
| **Connection string** | `DefaultEndpointsProtocol=https;AccountName=x;AccountKey=y;EndpointSuffix=core.windows.net` |
| **Entra ID (RBAC) auth** | Token-based data-plane auth using data roles. 📌 **Most secure — recommended.** |
| **SAS** | Shared Access Signature — a signed URL with limited scope/time. |
| **Encryption at rest** | **Always on**, cannot be disabled. **256-bit AES**. |
| **SSE (Storage Service Encryption)** | The at-rest encryption feature. |
| **MMK / Microsoft-managed keys** | Default — Microsoft handles everything. |
| **CMK / Customer-managed keys** | Your key in **Azure Key Vault** or Managed HSM. You control rotation and revocation. |
| **Customer-provided keys (CPK)** | Key supplied on each blob request header. Blob only. |
| **Infrastructure encryption** | An optional **second layer** of encryption (double encryption). **Must be enabled at account creation.** |
| **Encryption scope** | Different keys for different containers/blobs within one account. |
| **Encryption in transit** | **HTTPS**; enforced by `supportsHttpsTrafficOnly` (Secure transfer required). |
| **Minimum TLS version** | Enforce TLS 1.2 (default for new accounts). |
| **Key Vault** | Managed secret/key/certificate store. |
| **Managed identity** | An identity for an Azure resource — use it instead of storing keys. |
| **Storage account key access** | Can be **disabled** (`allowSharedKeyAccess=false`) to force Entra auth. |

### 📌 Encryption defaults

| Property | Default | Can change? |
|---|---|---|
| Encryption at rest | **Always on, AES-256** | ❌ Cannot be disabled |
| Key type | Microsoft-managed | ✅ Switch to CMK anytime |
| **Infrastructure encryption** | **Off** | ⚠️ **Only at creation time** |
| Secure transfer required (HTTPS) | **On** | ✅ (but don't) |
| Minimum TLS | 1.2 | ✅ |
| Allow blob public access | **Off** (new accounts) | ✅ |
| Allow shared key access | On | ✅ Set to false to force Entra |

⚠️ **THE encryption trap:** **Infrastructure encryption cannot be enabled after the account is created.** You must recreate the account.

### 📌 CMK requirements

| Requirement | Detail |
|---|---|
| Key store | Azure **Key Vault** (or Managed HSM), same region as the storage account (or use cross-tenant) |
| **Soft delete** on the vault | ✅ **Required** |
| **Purge protection** on the vault | ✅ **Required** |
| Identity | A **managed identity** on the storage account, granted access to the vault |
| Vault permission model | Access policy: `get`, `wrapKey`, `unwrapKey`. RBAC: **Key Vault Crypto Service Encryption User** |
| Key type | RSA or RSA-HSM, 2048/3072/4096 |
| Auto-rotation | Supported (specify key without version) |

⚠️ **Trap:** "CMK fails to configure." → 99% of the time: **soft delete and/or purge protection are not enabled on the Key Vault**.

### Key Vault essentials (AZ-104 needs the basics)

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Vault** | Container for secrets, keys, certificates. |
| **Secret** | Password/connection string. |
| **Key** | Cryptographic key (RSA/EC), usable for wrap/unwrap, sign/verify. |
| **Certificate** | X.509 certificate + its private key. |
| **SKU: Standard** | Software-protected keys. |
| **SKU: Premium** | **HSM-backed** keys (FIPS 140-2 Level 2). |
| **Managed HSM** | Fully managed, single-tenant HSM pool (FIPS 140-2 Level 3). |
| **Access policy** | Legacy per-vault permission model. |
| **RBAC permission model** | Modern; use Azure RBAC roles (Key Vault Administrator, Secrets User, etc.). 📌 Recommended. |
| **Soft delete** | **Always on**, 7–90 days retention (default 90). Cannot be disabled. |
| **Purge protection** | Prevents permanent deletion during the retention window. ⚠️ **Once enabled, cannot be disabled.** |
| **Firewall / private endpoint** | Restrict network access to the vault. |

⚠️ **Trap:** **Purge protection is irreversible.** Once on, it stays on for the life of the vault.
⚠️ **Trap:** Soft delete on Key Vault is **mandatory** — you cannot turn it off (since 2020).

```bash
# Rotate keys
az storage account keys list -n stacct -g rg -o table
az storage account keys renew -n stacct -g rg --key primary

# Force Entra-only auth
az storage account update -n stacct -g rg --allow-shared-key-access false

# CMK
az keyvault create -n kv-contoso -g rg --enable-purge-protection true --enable-soft-delete true
az keyvault key create --vault-name kv-contoso -n storage-key --protection software
az storage account update -n stacct -g rg --assign-identity
az keyvault set-policy -n kv-contoso --object-id <saPrincipalId> --key-permissions get wrapKey unwrapKey
az storage account update -n stacct -g rg --encryption-key-source Microsoft.Keyvault \
  --encryption-key-vault "https://kv-contoso.vault.azure.net" --encryption-key-name storage-key

# Infrastructure encryption — CREATION ONLY
az storage account create -n stacct2 -g rg --require-infrastructure-encryption true --sku Standard_LRS --kind StorageV2
```

---

## 3.9 Shared Access Signatures (SAS)

**A SAS is a signed URL granting scoped, time-limited access without sharing the account key.**

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Service SAS** | Access to **one service** (blob, file, queue, or table) in the account. Signed with the **account key**. |
| **Account SAS** | Access to **one or more services** at the account level, including **service-level operations** (e.g., Get/Set Service Properties, List Containers). Signed with the **account key**. |
| **User delegation SAS** | 📌 **The most secure.** Signed with an **Entra ID credential**, not the account key. **Blob and Data Lake only.** Max validity **7 days**. |
| **Stored access policy** | A named policy on a **container/share/queue/table** that defines permissions and expiry. A SAS can reference it. 📌 **The only way to REVOKE a SAS without rotating the key.** |
| **Ad hoc SAS** | A SAS with permissions/expiry baked into the URL. **Cannot be revoked individually.** |
| **SAS token** | The query-string portion: `?sv=...&ss=b&srt=co&sp=rl&se=...&sig=...` |
| **`sv`** | Storage service version |
| **`ss`** | Signed services (b/f/q/t) — account SAS only |
| **`srt`** | Signed resource types: **s**ervice / **c**ontainer / **o**bject — account SAS only |
| **`sp`** | Signed permissions: `racwdlup` etc. |
| **`st` / `se`** | Start time / expiry time (UTC) |
| **`sip`** | Allowed IP range |
| **`spr`** | Allowed protocol (`https` or `https,http`) |
| **`sig`** | The HMAC-SHA256 signature |
| **`sr`** | Signed resource (b=blob, c=container) — service SAS |
| **`si`** | Signed identifier — reference to a **stored access policy** |
| **`skoid`/`sktid`/`ske`** | User-delegation key fields (user delegation SAS only) |

### 📌 SAS permission letters

| Letter | Permission |
|---|---|
| `r` | Read |
| `a` | Add |
| `c` | Create |
| `w` | Write |
| `d` | Delete |
| `x` | Delete version |
| `l` | List |
| `t` | Tags |
| `f` | Filter by tags |
| `m` | Move |
| `e` | Execute |
| `o` | Set immutability policy |
| `p` | Permanent delete |
| `u` | Update (queues) |
| `i` | Process (queues) |
| `y` | Permanent delete |

💡 Mnemonic for the common set: **`racwdl`** = Read, Add, Create, Write, Delete, List.

### 📌 The three SAS types

| | Service SAS | Account SAS | **User delegation SAS** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Signed with | Account key | Account key | **Entra credentials** |
| Scope | One service | Multiple services + account ops | Blob/ADLS only |
| Max lifetime | Unlimited (policy-limited) | Unlimited | **7 days** |
| Revoke by | Rotate key / delete stored access policy | Rotate key | **Revoke the user delegation key** or the user's RBAC |
| Security | Medium | Lowest | **Highest** ✅ |
| Needs the key on the server? | Yes | Yes | **No** |

### 📌 The revocation question — asked constantly

> **How do you revoke a SAS?**

| SAS kind | Revocation method |
|---|---|
| Ad hoc SAS (no stored access policy) | ⚠️ **Rotate the account key** — this invalidates **ALL** SAS signed with that key |
| SAS tied to a **stored access policy** | ✅ **Delete or modify the stored access policy** — surgical, only that SAS is killed |
| User delegation SAS | ✅ `Revoke user delegation keys`, or remove the user's RBAC |

📌 **This is why stored access policies exist.** If the question says "revoke access to a specific SAS without affecting other clients" → **stored access policy**.

### 📌 Stored access policy facts

| Fact | Value |
|---|---|
| Max policies per container/share/queue/table | **5** |
| Where defined | On the **container** (not the account, not the blob) |
| What it can define | Permissions, start time, expiry time |
| SAS reference field | `si=<policyId>` |
| Change takes effect | Within **30 seconds** |
| Delete the policy | Immediately invalidates any SAS referencing it |

### 📌 SAS best practices

1. **Always use HTTPS** (`spr=https`).
2. **Short expiry** — minutes/hours, not months.
3. **Least privilege** — `r` not `racwdl`.
4. **Restrict by IP** (`sip`) where possible.
5. **Prefer user delegation SAS** over key-signed SAS.
6. **Use stored access policies** so you can revoke.
7. **Set a SAS expiration policy** on the account to enforce max validity.
8. Set the **start time slightly in the past** (~15 min) to absorb clock skew.

⚠️ **Trap:** A SAS is **not affected by RBAC** — if the SAS is valid, it works, even if the user has no Azure roles. **Exception:** a **user delegation SAS** *is* bounded by the signing user's RBAC — the SAS can never grant more than the user has.

⚠️ **Trap:** A SAS **does not** bypass the **storage firewall**. If the account's public access is restricted to a VNet, a valid SAS from outside that VNet is still blocked. Network rules are evaluated **before** authorization.

### Commands

```bash
# Account SAS
az storage account generate-sas \
  --account-name stacct \
  --services b --resource-types sco \
  --permissions rl \
  --expiry 2026-07-16T00:00:00Z \
  --https-only --account-key <key>

# Service SAS for a blob
az storage blob generate-sas \
  --account-name stacct -c mycontainer -n report.pdf \
  --permissions r --expiry 2026-07-16T00:00:00Z --https-only --full-uri

# User delegation SAS (Entra-signed — no key!)
az storage blob generate-sas \
  --account-name stacct -c mycontainer -n report.pdf \
  --permissions r --expiry 2026-07-16T00:00:00Z \
  --auth-mode login --as-user --full-uri

# Stored access policy
az storage container policy create --container-name mycontainer \
  --name readonly-policy --permissions rl \
  --expiry 2026-12-31T23:59:00Z --account-name stacct

az storage blob generate-sas --account-name stacct -c mycontainer -n report.pdf \
  --policy-name readonly-policy --full-uri

# Revoke by deleting the policy
az storage container policy delete --container-name mycontainer --name readonly-policy --account-name stacct

# Enforce max SAS lifetime on the account
az storage account update -n stacct -g rg --sas-expiration-period 1.00:00:00
```

```powershell
$ctx = New-AzStorageContext -StorageAccountName stacct -UseConnectedAccount
New-AzStorageBlobSASToken -Container mycontainer -Blob report.pdf -Permission r `
  -ExpiryTime (Get-Date).AddHours(1) -Context $ctx -FullUri

New-AzStorageContainerStoredAccessPolicy -Container mycontainer -Policy "readonly-policy" `
  -Permission rl -ExpiryTime (Get-Date).AddDays(30) -Context $ctx
Remove-AzStorageContainerStoredAccessPolicy -Container mycontainer -Policy "readonly-policy" -Context $ctx
```

---

## 3.10 Networking for Storage

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Public endpoint** | The default internet-facing URL of the storage account. |
| **Storage firewall** | IP and VNet rules controlling who may reach the public endpoint. |
| **Default action: Allow** | Open to all networks (default on creation). |
| **Default action: Deny** | Blocked except for explicit rules and exceptions. |
| **Service endpoint** | Extends the **VNet identity** to the Azure service over the **Azure backbone**. The storage account still has a **public IP**, but it can filter on `Microsoft.Storage` from a specific subnet. Free. |
| **Private endpoint** | A **NIC with a private IP inside your VNet** mapped to the storage account. Traffic never touches the internet. Costs money. |
| **Private Link** | The service behind private endpoints. |
| **Private DNS zone** | `privatelink.blob.core.windows.net` — resolves the account name to the private IP. |
| **Trusted Microsoft services** | An exception allowing services like Backup, Monitor, Event Grid, Site Recovery to reach the account despite Deny. |
| **Resource instance rule** | A more granular exception allowing a **specific** Azure resource (by resource ID) or managed identity. |
| **Cross-tenant/Cross-region** | Private endpoints work across VNets, regions, and peered networks. |

### 📌 Service endpoint vs Private endpoint — the classic comparison

| | Service endpoint | **Private endpoint** |
|---|---|---|
| Gives storage a private IP? | ❌ **No** — still a public IP, filtered | ✅ **Yes** — NIC in your subnet |
| Traffic path | Azure backbone (source IP becomes the private VNet IP) | Azure backbone via Private Link |
| Cost | **Free** | **Paid** (per endpoint per hour + data) |
| Scope | Whole **service** in a region | **One specific resource** (even one sub-resource: blob vs file) |
| Access from on-prem (VPN/ER) | ❌ **No** | ✅ **Yes** |
| Access from **peered VNets** | ❌ No (must configure per-VNet) | ✅ Yes |
| DNS change needed | ❌ No | ✅ **Yes — private DNS zone** |
| Granularity | Subnet → service | Subnet → **specific resource** |
| Data exfiltration risk | ⚠️ Higher (can reach *any* account in the service) | Lower (only that account) |

📌 **If the question involves on-premises access, peered VNets, or reaching a single specific account → Private Endpoint.**
📌 **If the question involves "free," "simple," or "lock down to a subnet" → Service Endpoint.**

### 📌 Private endpoint DNS — the #1 private endpoint trap

Without the private DNS zone, `stacct.blob.core.windows.net` still resolves to the **public IP**, and the firewall blocks you. You **must**:

1. Create the private DNS zone: `privatelink.blob.core.windows.net`
2. Link it to the VNet (**virtual network link**)
3. Create an **A record** mapping `stacct` → the private IP
4. Azure adds a **CNAME** from `stacct.blob.core.windows.net` → `stacct.privatelink.blob.core.windows.net`

⚠️ **Trap:** "The private endpoint is created but the app still can't connect." → **DNS.** Run `nslookup stacct.blob.core.windows.net` — if it returns a public IP, the private DNS zone is missing or not linked.

### 📌 Private DNS zone names — memorize

| Service | Zone |
|---|---|
| Blob | `privatelink.blob.core.windows.net` |
| File | `privatelink.file.core.windows.net` |
| Queue | `privatelink.queue.core.windows.net` |
| Table | `privatelink.table.core.windows.net` |
| Web (static) | `privatelink.web.core.windows.net` |
| DFS (ADLS Gen2) | `privatelink.dfs.core.windows.net` |
| Key Vault | `privatelink.vaultcore.azure.net` |
| SQL Database | `privatelink.database.windows.net` |
| App Service | `privatelink.azurewebsites.net` |
| Cosmos DB (SQL) | `privatelink.documents.azure.com` |
| ACR | `privatelink.azurecr.io` |
| AKS | `privatelink.<region>.azmk8s.io` |

📌 **Each storage service needs its OWN private endpoint and its OWN DNS zone.** A blob private endpoint does not give you file access.

⚠️ **Trap:** "One private endpoint for a storage account gives access to blob, file, and queue." → **FALSE.** One per **sub-resource**.

### Commands

```bash
# Firewall
az storage account update -n stacct -g rg --default-action Deny
az storage account network-rule add -n stacct -g rg --ip-address 203.0.113.10
az storage account network-rule add -n stacct -g rg --vnet-name vnet1 --subnet subnet1
az storage account update -n stacct -g rg --bypass AzureServices Logging Metrics
az storage account network-rule list -n stacct -g rg

# Service endpoint (on the SUBNET, not the storage account)
az network vnet subnet update -g rg --vnet-name vnet1 -n subnet1 \
  --service-endpoints Microsoft.Storage

# Private endpoint
az network private-endpoint create \
  --name pe-stacct-blob -g rg --vnet-name vnet1 --subnet subnet1 \
  --private-connection-resource-id <storageAccountId> \
  --group-id blob --connection-name pe-conn

# Private DNS
az network private-dns zone create -g rg -n "privatelink.blob.core.windows.net"
az network private-dns link vnet create -g rg -n link-vnet1 \
  -z "privatelink.blob.core.windows.net" -v vnet1 -e false

az network private-endpoint dns-zone-group create \
  -g rg --endpoint-name pe-stacct-blob -n zonegroup \
  --private-dns-zone "privatelink.blob.core.windows.net" --zone-name blob

# Disable public access entirely
az storage account update -n stacct -g rg --public-network-access Disabled
```

⚠️ **Trap:** Adding a **service endpoint** is done on the **subnet**, not on the storage account. Adding the **VNet rule** is done on the **storage account**. You need **both**.

---

## 3.11 Data Movement Tools

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **AzCopy** | Command-line tool for high-performance copy/sync to/from/between storage accounts. Uses **server-to-server** copy when both ends are Azure (no local bandwidth used). |
| **Azure Storage Explorer** | Free GUI (desktop + portal). Uses AzCopy underneath. |
| **Azure Import/Export** | Ship your own **disks** to a datacenter for bulk import/export. |
| **Azure Data Box** | Microsoft ships **you** an appliance. Disk (8 TB), Box (100 TB), Heavy (1 PB). |
| **Data Box Gateway** | A **virtual appliance** on-prem that presents SMB/NFS and continuously uploads to Azure. |
| **Azure Data Factory (ADF)** | Cloud ETL/orchestration service for scheduled, large-scale data movement. |
| **Azure Migrate** | Assessment + migration hub for servers, DBs, web apps. |
| **`azcopy sync`** | One-way sync (like rsync) — only transfers changed files. |
| **`azcopy copy`** | One-way copy; `--recursive` for folders. |

### 📌 The bulk-transfer decision table

| Scenario | Tool |
|---|---|
| A few GB, scripted | **AzCopy** |
| Ad hoc, GUI, browse | **Storage Explorer** |
| Copy between two storage accounts | **AzCopy** (server-to-server) or **Object Replication** |
| **40 TB+, poor/expensive network** | **Azure Data Box** |
| Petabytes | **Data Box Heavy** |
| You already own the disks | **Import/Export** |
| Continuous on-prem → cloud tiering | **Data Box Gateway** or **Azure File Sync** |
| Scheduled, transformed, orchestrated pipelines | **Azure Data Factory** |

💡 **Rule of thumb:** if transferring over the network would take **more than a week**, use Data Box.

### 📌 Data Box family

| Product | Capacity (usable) | Form |
|---|---|---|
| **Data Box Disk** | 8 TB per disk, up to **40 TB** (5 disks) | SSDs in the mail |
| **Data Box** | **80 TB usable** (100 TB raw) | Ruggedized appliance |
| **Data Box Heavy** | **~770 TB usable** (1 PB raw) | Rack on wheels |

### AzCopy commands

```bash
# Login (Entra) — preferred
azcopy login
azcopy login --identity                 # managed identity
azcopy login --service-principal --application-id <id> --tenant-id <tid>

# Upload
azcopy copy "C:\data\*" "https://stacct.blob.core.windows.net/mycontainer/" --recursive

# Download
azcopy copy "https://stacct.blob.core.windows.net/mycontainer/*" "C:\data\" --recursive

# Account to account (server-to-server; no local bandwidth)
azcopy copy "https://src.blob.core.windows.net/c1?<SAS>" \
            "https://dst.blob.core.windows.net/c2?<SAS>" --recursive

# Sync (only changed files)
azcopy sync "C:\data" "https://stacct.blob.core.windows.net/mycontainer" \
  --recursive --delete-destination=true

# Useful flags
--block-size-mb 100
--cap-mbps 500
--overwrite ifSourceNewer
--include-pattern "*.log"
--exclude-path "temp;cache"
--put-md5
--blob-type BlockBlob
--block-blob-tier Cool

# Jobs
azcopy jobs list
azcopy jobs resume <jobId>
azcopy jobs show <jobId>
```

⚠️ **Trap:** `azcopy sync --delete-destination=true` will **delete** files at the destination that no longer exist at the source. Dangerous.
⚠️ **Trap:** AzCopy needs either `azcopy login` (Entra) **or** a SAS in the URL. It does **not** use the account key directly.

# Part 4 — Domain 3: Deploy and Manage Azure Compute Resources (20–25%)

## 4.1 Virtual Machines

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Virtual machine (VM)** | IaaS compute — you control the OS and everything above it. |
| **VM image** | The template the OS disk is created from: Marketplace, Azure Compute Gallery, or a custom/managed image. |
| **Generation 1 (Gen1)** | BIOS boot, legacy. Broadest compatibility. |
| **Generation 2 (Gen2)** | **UEFI** boot. Required for: **Trusted Launch**, **Confidential VMs**, OS disks > 2 TB, Secure Boot, vTPM. 📌 Recommended default. |
| **VM agent (WAAgent)** | Guest agent enabling extensions, password reset, and heartbeat. Required for most features. |
| **OS disk** | The bootable disk, mounted as `C:` (Windows) or `/dev/sda1` (Linux). Max 4 TiB (Gen1: 2 TiB). |
| **Data disk** | Additional persistent disks. Count depends on VM size. |
| **Temporary disk** | 📌 **`D:` on Windows, `/dev/sdb` (`/mnt`) on Linux.** Local SSD on the host. **DATA IS LOST** on deallocate, resize, host maintenance, or host failure. **Not backed up. Not encrypted by the platform at rest in all cases.** |
| **NIC** | Network interface — attaches the VM to a subnet. |
| **Public IP (PIP)** | Optional internet-routable address. |
| **NSG** | Firewall rules; can be on the NIC and/or the subnet. |
| **Boot diagnostics** | Serial console output + screenshot for troubleshooting boot failures. |
| **Serial console** | Text console access to the VM (needs boot diagnostics with a storage account or managed storage). |
| **Provisioning state** | ARM's view: Creating / Succeeded / Failed. |
| **Power state** | Running / Stopped / **Stopped (deallocated)** / Starting / Deallocating. |
| **Trusted Launch** | Secure Boot + vTPM + boot integrity monitoring. Gen2 only. 📌 Default for new VMs. |
| **Confidential VM** | Encrypts memory in use (AMD SEV-SNP / Intel TDX). |
| **Ephemeral OS disk** | OS disk stored on the **local host** — free, fast, **stateless**. Lost on deallocate. Great for VMSS/AKS. |
| **Generalized image** | Sysprep'd (Windows) / waagent-deprovisioned (Linux) — no machine-specific data. Reusable as a template. |
| **Specialized image** | Not generalized — a clone of a specific machine, keeps hostname/SIDs/user accounts. |

### 📌 Power states and billing — asked in nearly every exam

| State | Compute billed? | Disks billed? | Static PIP billed? | RAM/temp disk kept? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Running** | ✅ Yes | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| **Stopped** (shutdown *inside the OS*) | ⚠️ **YES — still billed!** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (host reserved) |
| **Stopped (deallocated)** (portal / `az vm deallocate`) | ❌ **No** | ✅ **Yes** | ✅ **Yes** | ❌ **Temp disk data LOST** |
| **Deleted** | ❌ | ❌ (if disks deleted) | — | — |

⚠️ **THE VM billing trap:** Shutting a VM down from **inside the guest OS** leaves it in the **"Stopped"** state — **you keep paying for compute**. Only **"Stopped (deallocated)"** stops the compute bill. `az vm stop` also leaves it allocated in some versions — use **`az vm deallocate`**.

⚠️ **Second trap:** Even deallocated, you still pay for **managed disks** and **static public IPs**.

### 📌 What changes on deallocation?

| Item | Behavior |
|---|---|
| **Temporary disk (D: / /mnt)** | ⚠️ **Wiped** |
| **Dynamic private IP** | May change (released and re-leased) |
| **Static private IP** | Preserved |
| **Dynamic public IP** | ⚠️ **Released — you get a new one on start** |
| **Static public IP** | Preserved |
| OS and data disks | Preserved |
| VM ID, NIC, NSG | Preserved |

⚠️ **Trap:** "The VM's public IP changed after a restart." → It was a **Dynamic** PIP and the VM was **deallocated** (not just rebooted). A simple *restart* does not change it; a deallocate does. Fix: **Static** PIP.

### VM creation checklist

| Required | Notes |
|---|---|
| Subscription + resource group | |
| VM name | 1–15 chars for Windows (NetBIOS limit!), 1–64 for Linux |
| Region | Must match/allow the VNet |
| Availability option | None / Availability Set / Availability Zone / VMSS |
| Image | Marketplace / gallery / custom |
| Size | See 4.2 |
| Admin account | Username + password or **SSH key** |
| Inbound ports | ⚠️ Opening RDP/SSH to the internet is a security anti-pattern — use **Bastion** |
| Disks | OS disk type, data disks |
| Networking | VNet, subnet, PIP, NSG |
| Management | Boot diagnostics, auto-shutdown, patching |

📌 **Windows VM computer name max = 15 characters.** A frequent gotcha in "why did the deployment fail" questions.

### Commands

```bash
# Create a Linux VM with SSH key
az vm create \
  --resource-group rg-prod --name vm-web-01 \
  --image Ubuntu2204 --size Standard_D2s_v5 \
  --admin-username azureuser --generate-ssh-keys \
  --vnet-name vnet1 --subnet subnet1 \
  --public-ip-sku Standard --nsg-rule SSH \
  --zone 1 --os-disk-size-gb 64 --storage-sku Premium_LRS

# Create a Windows VM
az vm create -g rg-prod -n vm-win-01 \
  --image Win2022Datacenter --size Standard_D2s_v5 \
  --admin-username azureadmin --admin-password 'ComplexP@ss123!' \
  --availability-set as-web

# Lifecycle
az vm start      -g rg-prod -n vm-web-01
az vm restart    -g rg-prod -n vm-web-01
az vm stop       -g rg-prod -n vm-web-01     # ⚠️ may leave it allocated
az vm deallocate -g rg-prod -n vm-web-01     # ✅ stops compute billing
az vm delete     -g rg-prod -n vm-web-01 --yes

# Resize
az vm list-vm-resize-options -g rg-prod -n vm-web-01 -o table
az vm resize -g rg-prod -n vm-web-01 --size Standard_D4s_v5

# Info
az vm list -d -o table                       # -d shows power state
az vm get-instance-view -g rg-prod -n vm-web-01 --query instanceView.statuses
az vm list-sizes --location eastus -o table
az vm list-skus --location eastus --zone --size Standard_D -o table

# Redeploy (moves to a new host — fixes weird issues)
az vm redeploy -g rg-prod -n vm-web-01

# Reset password / SSH key
az vm user update -g rg-prod -n vm-web-01 -u azureuser -p 'NewP@ssw0rd!'

# Boot diagnostics
az vm boot-diagnostics enable -g rg-prod -n vm-web-01
az vm boot-diagnostics get-boot-log -g rg-prod -n vm-web-01
```

```powershell
New-AzVm -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -Name vm-web-01 -Location eastus `
  -Image "Win2022Datacenter" -Size "Standard_D2s_v5" `
  -VirtualNetworkName vnet1 -SubnetName subnet1 -Zone 1 `
  -Credential (Get-Credential)

Start-AzVM    -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -Name vm-web-01
Restart-AzVM  -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -Name vm-web-01
Stop-AzVM     -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -Name vm-web-01 -Force            # deallocates
Stop-AzVM     -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -Name vm-web-01 -StayProvisioned  # ⚠️ keeps billing
Remove-AzVM   -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -Name vm-web-01 -Force

Get-AzVM -Status | Select Name, PowerState
$vm = Get-AzVM -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -Name vm-web-01
$vm.HardwareProfile.VmSize = "Standard_D4s_v5"
Update-AzVM -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -VM $vm
```

📌 **`Stop-AzVM -Force`** deallocates. **`Stop-AzVM -StayProvisioned`** keeps the compute bill running.

---

## 4.2 VM Sizing and Families

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **VM size / SKU** | The named hardware profile: vCPUs, RAM, disk limits, NICs, IOPS caps. |
| **Series / family** | A letter grouping: B, D, E, F, etc. |
| **Version** | The generation suffix: `v3`, `v4`, `v5`, `v6`. Newer = better price/perf. |
| **`s` suffix** | 📌 **Premium Storage capable** (e.g., `Standard_D2s_v5`). Required for Premium SSD and for VM-level SLA of 99.9%. |
| **`a` suffix** | AMD processor. |
| **`d` suffix** | Has a local **temp disk**. |
| **`p` suffix** | ARM64 (Ampere Altra). |
| **`m` suffix** | Extra memory. |
| **`i` suffix** | Isolated (dedicated hardware). |
| **`l` suffix** | Low memory relative to vCPU. |
| **vCPU quota** | Per-region, per-family cap. Deployment fails with "Operation could not be completed as it results in exceeding approved quota." |
| **Accelerated networking** | SR-IOV — bypasses the host virtual switch. Lower latency, higher PPS. Needs 2+ vCPUs and a supported size/OS. |
| **Dedicated host** | A physical server dedicated to your subscription. For compliance/licensing. |
| **Constrained vCPU** | Reduce billable vCPUs while keeping RAM/IO — saves per-core software licensing (e.g., SQL). |

### 📌 VM families — know the letters

| Series | Type | Use for | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **B** | **Burstable** | Dev/test, low-traffic web, small DBs | Earns **CPU credits** when idle, burns them when busy. Cheapest. ⚠️ Throttled once credits run out. |
| **D / Dv5 / Dsv5** | **General purpose** | 📌 **The default choice.** Web servers, app servers, small-medium DBs | Balanced CPU:RAM ≈ **1:4** |
| **E / Ev5 / Esv5** | **Memory optimized** | SQL Server, SAP HANA, in-memory caches, analytics | CPU:RAM ≈ **1:8** |
| **F / Fsv2** | **Compute optimized** | Batch processing, gaming servers, web with high CPU, network appliances | CPU:RAM ≈ **1:2** |
| **L / Lsv3** | **Storage optimized** | NoSQL (Cassandra/MongoDB), big data, data warehousing | High local **NVMe** throughput |
| **M / Mv2** | **Massive memory** | SAP HANA, huge in-memory DBs | Up to ~**12 TB RAM** |
| **N (NC/ND/NV)** | **GPU** | NC = **compute/CUDA/AI training**; ND = **deep learning**; NV = **visualization/VDI/rendering** | |
| **H / HB / HC** | **HPC** | CFD, molecular dynamics, weather modeling | InfiniBand RDMA |
| **A** | Entry level | Very light dev/test | Legacy |
| **DC** | Confidential | Confidential computing (SGX/SEV-SNP) | |

### 📌 The mapping question — memorize this

| Requirement | Family |
|---|---|
| "Lowest cost dev/test, intermittent CPU" | **B (burstable)** |
| "General purpose web server, balanced" | **D** |
| "SQL Server needing lots of RAM" | **E** or **M** |
| "High CPU, low memory batch job" | **F** |
| "Big data / NoSQL needing local disk throughput" | **L** |
| "SAP HANA with 4 TB of RAM" | **M** |
| "Machine learning training" | **NC / ND (GPU)** |
| "Remote desktop with 3D graphics" | **NV (GPU)** |
| "MPI / InfiniBand cluster" | **H / HB** |

### Size name decoder

```
Standard_D8ds_v5
   │      ││││  └── Version 5
   │      │││└───── s = Premium Storage capable
   │      ││└────── d = local temp Disk
   │      │└─────── 8 = vCPU count
   │      └──────── D = General purpose family
   └─────────────── Standard tier
```

### 📌 Resizing rules

| Rule | Detail |
|---|---|
| Same **hardware cluster** | Resize possible **without deallocating** (VM just reboots) |
| Different cluster | Must **deallocate** the VM first |
| In an **availability set** | ⚠️ All VMs in the set may need to be deallocated if the target size isn't available in the current cluster |
| Not all sizes available in all regions/zones | Check `az vm list-skus --location eastus --zone` |
| Resizing wipes the **temp disk** | ✅ Always |
| Data on OS/data disks | Preserved |

⚠️ **Trap:** "The desired VM size isn't in the resize list." → The current hardware cluster doesn't support it. **Deallocate the VM** and retry — the list expands.

### Accelerated networking

| Fact | Detail |
|---|---|
| What | SR-IOV — the NIC talks straight to the VM, bypassing the host vSwitch |
| Benefit | Lower latency, lower jitter, less CPU, higher packets/sec |
| Requirements | **2+ vCPUs** (4+ for some), supported size (D/DS v2+, F/Fs, E, etc.), supported OS |
| Cost | **Free** |
| Enable/disable | Can toggle on an existing VM, but the VM must be **deallocated** |
| Not supported on | B-series ⚠️ |

```bash
az network nic update -g rg -n vm-web-01VMNic --accelerated-networking true
```

⚠️ **Trap:** B-series does **not** support accelerated networking.

---

## 4.3 Azure Disks

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Managed disk** | 📌 Azure manages the underlying storage account. **The default and recommended.** |
| **Unmanaged disk** | Legacy — a VHD page blob you place in your own storage account. **Deprecated.** |
| **VHD** | The Virtual Hard Disk file format. |
| **Ultra Disk** | Highest performance. **Independently configurable** IOPS/throughput without resizing. No caching. Zone-pinned. Not available for OS disks. |
| **Premium SSD v2** | Newer premium tier — configurable IOPS/throughput, cheaper baseline than Ultra. **No caching. No OS disk support. LRS/ZRS only.** |
| **Premium SSD** | SSD-backed, size-tiered performance (P1–P80). Required for the **99.9% single-VM SLA**. |
| **Standard SSD** | SSD, lower/consistent performance. Dev/test, light production. |
| **Standard HDD** | Cheapest, magnetic. Backup, non-critical. |
| **Disk caching** | Host-level cache: **None / ReadOnly / ReadWrite**. |
| **Bursting** | Temporary IOPS boost. **Credit-based** (free, P20 and below) or **on-demand** (paid, P30+). |
| **Disk encryption: SSE** | Server-side encryption at rest. **Always on.** Platform or customer key. |
| **Azure Disk Encryption (ADE)** | **In-guest** encryption using **BitLocker** (Windows) or **dm-crypt** (Linux). Keys in Key Vault. |
| **Encryption at host** | Encrypts data on the **host VM** including the **temp disk and cache**. Must be enabled per-region on the subscription. |
| **Disk snapshot** | Read-only, full point-in-time copy of a managed disk. A standalone resource. |
| **Managed image** | A generalized image resource built from a VM. |
| **Shared disks** | A managed disk attached to **multiple VMs** simultaneously (`maxShares`), for clustering. Premium SSD/Ultra only. |
| **Disk bursting** | See above. |
| **Host caching** | See disk caching. |

### 📌 The four disk types

| | Ultra Disk | Premium SSD v2 | Premium SSD | Standard SSD | Standard HDD |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Media | NVMe SSD | SSD | SSD | SSD | HDD |
| Max IOPS/disk | **160,000** | 80,000 | 20,000 | 6,000 | 2,000 |
| Max throughput | **4,000 MB/s** | 1,200 MB/s | 900 MB/s | 750 MB/s | 500 MB/s |
| Max size | 64 TiB | 64 TiB | 32 TiB | 32 TiB | 32 TiB |
| **OS disk?** | ❌ **No** | ❌ **No** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| **Caching?** | ❌ **No** | ❌ **No** | ✅ | ✅ (ReadOnly/None) | ✅ |
| Perf independent of size? | ✅ | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |
| Redundancy | LRS, ZRS | LRS, ZRS | LRS, ZRS | LRS, ZRS | LRS |
| **Snapshots?** | ⚠️ Limited (incremental only) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| **Availability set?** | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| Use for | SAP HANA, top-tier SQL, NoSQL | High-perf general | Production | Web servers, light prod | Backup, dev/test |

### ⚠️ The four Ultra Disk traps

1. **Ultra Disk cannot be an OS disk** — data disks only.
2. **Ultra Disk does not support host caching** — must be `None`.
3. **Ultra Disk cannot be used in an availability set**; it requires **Availability Zones** and must be in the same zone as the VM.
4. **Ultra Disk requires the VM size to support it** (`az vm list-skus --query "[?capabilities[?name=='UltraSSDAvailable']]"`) and `--ultra-ssd-enabled true` on the VM.

📌 **Premium SSD v2** shares traps 1 and 2 (no OS disk, no caching) — but it *does* support ZRS and is simpler/cheaper than Ultra.

### 📌 Disk caching — the exam table

| Cache setting | Read | Write | Use for |
|---|---|---|---|
| **None** | Not cached | Straight to disk | 📌 **Write-heavy / write-only workloads**, **log disks**, Ultra/PSSDv2 |
| **ReadOnly** | Cached on host | Straight to disk | 📌 **Read-heavy** workloads, **SQL data files**, most **OS disks** |
| **ReadWrite** | Cached | Cached (lazy write) | 📌 **Default for OS disks**. ⚠️ **Never for SQL data/log disks** — risk of data loss / corruption |

📌 **The SQL Server disk caching answer:**
- OS disk → ReadWrite (default)
- **SQL data files** → **ReadOnly**
- **SQL log files** → **None**
- **TempDB** → the local **temp disk (D:)** or ReadOnly

⚠️ **Trap:** "Which caching for a transaction log disk?" → **None.**

### 📌 Premium SSD tiers (recognize the pattern)

| Tier | Size | Baseline IOPS | Baseline MB/s |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | 4 GiB | 120 | 25 |
| P4 | 32 GiB | 120 | 25 |
| P6 | 64 GiB | 240 | 50 |
| P10 | 128 GiB | 500 | 100 |
| P15 | 256 GiB | 1,100 | 125 |
| P20 | 512 GiB | 2,300 | 150 |
| P30 | **1 TiB** | **5,000** | 200 |
| P40 | 2 TiB | 7,500 | 250 |
| P50 | 4 TiB | 7,500 | 250 |
| P60 | 8 TiB | 16,000 | 500 |
| P70 | 16 TiB | 18,000 | 750 |
| P80 | **32 TiB** | **20,000** | **900** |

📌 **Key insight:** Premium SSD performance is **tied to size**. Need more IOPS? **Buy a bigger disk** — or stripe multiple disks. This is why "the disk is slow but it's only 30% full" has the answer "**increase the disk size**" or "**stripe several disks**."

📌 **Credit-based bursting** is **free** and automatic on **P20 and below** (up to 3,500 IOPS / 170 MB/s). **On-demand bursting** is paid and available on **P30+**.

### 📌 Encryption comparison

| | SSE (default) | Azure Disk Encryption (ADE) | Encryption at host |
|---|---|---|---|
