| Where | Storage service | **Inside the guest OS** | **On the host** |
| Technology | Platform AES-256 | **BitLocker / dm-crypt** | Platform |
| Always on? | ✅ Yes | ❌ Opt-in | ❌ Opt-in |
| Encrypts **temp disk**? | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ |
| Encrypts **caches**? | ❌ | Partially | ✅ |
| Requires Key Vault? | Only for CMK | ✅ **Yes** | Only for CMK |
| Guest agent required? | ❌ | ✅ **Yes** | ❌ |
| Performance impact | None | Some | None |
| Works with Ultra Disk? | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ |
| Works with ephemeral OS disk? | n/a | ❌ | ✅ |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Encrypt the **temporary disk**." → **ADE** or **Encryption at host**. SSE alone does **not** cover the temp disk.
⚠️ **Trap:** ADE requires the Key Vault to be in the **same region** and have **"Enabled for disk encryption"** turned on.

### Commands

```bash
# Create and attach a data disk
az vm disk attach -g rg-prod --vm-name vm-web-01 \
  --name disk-data-01 --new --size-gb 256 --sku Premium_LRS --caching ReadOnly

az vm disk attach -g rg-prod --vm-name vm-web-01 --name existing-disk   # attach existing
az vm disk detach -g rg-prod --vm-name vm-web-01 --name disk-data-01

# Resize a disk (VM must be DEALLOCATED)
az vm deallocate -g rg-prod -n vm-web-01
az disk update -g rg-prod -n disk-data-01 --size-gb 512
az vm start -g rg-prod -n vm-web-01

# Change disk SKU
az disk update -g rg-prod -n disk-data-01 --sku StandardSSD_LRS

# Snapshot
az snapshot create -g rg-prod -n snap-disk-01 --source disk-data-01
az snapshot create -g rg-prod -n snap-inc --source disk-data-01 --incremental true

# Create a disk from a snapshot
az disk create -g rg-prod -n disk-restored --source snap-disk-01

# Ultra Disk
az vm create ... --ultra-ssd-enabled true --zone 1
az vm disk attach -g rg --vm-name vm01 -n ultra-disk --new --size-gb 1024 \
  --sku UltraSSD_LRS --caching None

# Encryption at host (must register the feature first)
az feature register --namespace Microsoft.Compute --name EncryptionAtHost
az vm update -g rg -n vm-web-01 --set securityProfile.encryptionAtHost=true

# ADE
az keyvault create -n kv-ade -g rg --enabled-for-disk-encryption true
az vm encryption enable -g rg -n vm-web-01 --disk-encryption-keyvault kv-ade --volume-type All
az vm encryption show -g rg -n vm-web-01
```

⚠️ **Trap:** **You cannot shrink a managed disk.** Only grow. To shrink you must create a new smaller disk and copy the data.
⚠️ **Trap:** To resize a disk, the VM must be **deallocated** (except for some cases with Premium SSD v2/Ultra live-resize).

---

## 4.4 High Availability for VMs

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Fault domain (FD)** | Servers sharing a power source and network switch. Failure of one FD takes down its VMs. |
| **Update domain (UD)** | Group of VMs rebooted together during **planned** platform maintenance. Only one UD is rebooted at a time, with **30 minutes** between groups. |
| **Availability set** | A logical grouping spreading VMs across FDs and UDs **within one datacenter**. Free. |
| **Availability Zone** | Physically separate datacenter within a region. |
| **Proximity placement group (PPG)** | Forces VMs into the **same datacenter/rack area** for **ultra-low latency**. ⚠️ Reduces availability. |
| **Virtual machine scale set (VMSS)** | Identical VMs managed as one unit, with autoscaling. |
| **Flexible orchestration** | 📌 **Modern default** VMSS mode — VMs are individual, can be different sizes, **spread across FDs and/or zones**. |
| **Uniform orchestration** | Legacy VMSS mode — identical VMs from one model. |
| **Managed instance** | n/a for VMs. |

### 📌 Availability set limits

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| **Fault domains** | **2 or 3** (max **3** in most regions; 2 in some) |
| **Update domains** | **5 by default**, configurable up to **20** |
| **VMs per availability set** | 200 |
| **SLA** | **99.95%** (with **2+** VMs) |
| Cost of the set itself | **Free** |
| Can you add an existing VM to a set? | ❌ **No** — must be set **at creation**; you must delete and recreate the VM (keeping the disks) |
| Can you change a VM's availability set? | ❌ **No** |
| Combine with zones? | ❌ **No — a VM is either in an availability set OR in a zone, never both** |
| Load balancer SKU | Basic LB works with availability sets; Standard LB works with both |

⚠️ **THE availability set trap #1:** **You cannot add an existing VM to an availability set.** You must **delete the VM** (keep the disks) and **recreate** it in the set.
⚠️ **THE availability set trap #2:** **A VM cannot be in both an availability set and an availability zone.**
⚠️ **Trap #3:** Availability sets protect against **rack** failure, **not** datacenter failure. If the whole datacenter goes down, all VMs in the set go down.

### 📌 Availability set vs Availability Zone

| | Availability Set | Availability Zone |
|---|---|---|
| Protects against | Rack / hardware / planned maintenance | **Whole-datacenter failure** |
| Scope | One datacenter | Multiple datacenters in a region |
| SLA | **99.95%** | **99.99%** |
| Latency between members | Lowest | <2 ms (slightly higher) |
| Cost | Free | Free (but **inter-zone data transfer is billed**) |
| Available everywhere? | ✅ | ❌ Only in AZ-enabled regions |
| Can combine? | ❌ Mutually exclusive | ❌ |
| Configure when? | **At VM creation only** | **At VM creation only** |

⚠️ **Trap:** **Inter-zone bandwidth is charged.** Intra-zone/intra-availability-set traffic is free. This appears in cost-optimization questions.

### 📌 Choosing the availability option

| Requirement | Answer |
|---|---|
| 99.95% SLA, single datacenter is fine | **Availability Set** (2+ VMs) |
| 99.99% SLA, survive a datacenter loss | **Availability Zones** (2+ zones) |
| Autoscale identical web servers | **VMSS** |
| 99.9% SLA with **one** VM | **Single VM with Premium SSD/Ultra for all disks** |
| Survive a **region** loss | **Azure Site Recovery** or a multi-region active/active design |
| Ultra-low latency between VMs (HPC, trading) | **Proximity Placement Group** |

### Fault/update domain distribution example

With 3 FDs and 5 UDs, VMs are distributed round-robin:

| VM | FD | UD |
|---|---|---|
| VM1 | 0 | 0 |
| VM2 | 1 | 1 |
| VM3 | 2 | 2 |
| VM4 | 0 | 3 |
| VM5 | 1 | 4 |
| VM6 | 2 | 0 |

📌 The platform reboots **one UD at a time** with **30 minutes** between them.

### Proximity Placement Groups

| Fact | Detail |
|---|---|
| Purpose | Co-locate VMs for **lowest network latency** |
| Trade-off | ⚠️ **Reduces resilience** — everything is close together |
| Anchor | Deploy the **most restrictive** VM size first; it anchors the PPG to a datacenter |
| Combine with | Availability sets (yes), Zones (yes but pins to one zone), VMSS (yes) |
| Allocation failures | ⚠️ More likely — the datacenter may not have the size you need |
| Use case | SAP, HPC, latency-sensitive multi-tier apps |

⚠️ **Trap:** "PPG improves availability." → **FALSE.** It improves **latency** and *reduces* availability.

### Commands

```bash
az vm availability-set create -g rg-prod -n as-web \
  --platform-fault-domain-count 3 --platform-update-domain-count 5

az vm create -g rg-prod -n vm-web-01 --availability-set as-web --image Ubuntu2204 ...
az vm create -g rg-prod -n vm-web-02 --availability-set as-web --image Ubuntu2204 ...

az vm availability-set list -g rg-prod -o table
az vm availability-set list-sizes -g rg-prod -n as-web -o table

# Zones
az vm create -g rg-prod -n vm-web-01 --zone 1 --image Ubuntu2204 ...
az vm create -g rg-prod -n vm-web-02 --zone 2 --image Ubuntu2204 ...

# Check zone support for a size
az vm list-skus --location eastus --size Standard_D2s_v5 --zone -o table

# PPG
az ppg create -g rg-prod -n ppg-sap --type Standard
az vm create -g rg-prod -n vm-app-01 --ppg ppg-sap --image ...
```

```powershell
New-AzAvailabilitySet -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -Name as-web -Location eastus `
  -PlatformFaultDomainCount 3 -PlatformUpdateDomainCount 5 -Sku Aligned

New-AzProximityPlacementGroup -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -Name ppg-sap -Location eastus
```

📌 **`-Sku Aligned`** = managed disks (required for modern deployments). `Classic` = unmanaged.

---

## 4.5 Virtual Machine Scale Sets (VMSS)

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **VMSS** | A group of load-balanced, identical VMs that can autoscale. |
| **Uniform orchestration** | All instances come from one model; identical; managed as a set. Legacy but still used. |
| **Flexible orchestration** | 📌 **Recommended default.** Instances are **standard VM resources**, can be heterogeneous, support **fault domains + zones**, and work with the standard VM APIs. |
| **Instance count** | Current number of VMs. |
| **Capacity** | Min / max instance bounds. |
| **Scaling policy** | Manual or **autoscale**. |
| **Autoscale rule** | Metric + threshold + duration + action. |
| **Scale-out** | Add instances. |
| **Scale-in** | Remove instances. |
| **Cool-down period** | 📌 Time to wait after a scale action before the next evaluation. **Default 5 minutes.** Prevents flapping. |
| **Scale-in policy** | Which instance to remove: **Default** (balance across zones/FDs, then highest ID), **NewestVM**, **OldestVM**. |
| **Upgrade policy: Manual** | You must manually apply the model change to each instance. **Default.** |
| **Upgrade policy: Automatic** | All instances updated at once — ⚠️ **causes downtime**. |
| **Upgrade policy: Rolling** | Batch-by-batch update with health checks. 📌 **Safest.** |
| **Automatic OS image upgrade** | Platform keeps instances on the latest image. |
| **Application health extension** | Reports instance health so rolling upgrades / auto-repair work. |
| **Automatic instance repair** | Deletes and recreates unhealthy instances. Requires health probe/extension + a **grace period**. |
| **Overprovisioning** | VMSS creates extra VMs and deletes the slowest — improves deployment success. Uniform only. |
| **Spot instances in VMSS** | Cheap, evictable capacity. |
| **Flexible + zones** | Spread across zones with `--zones 1 2 3`. |

### 📌 Uniform vs Flexible

| | Uniform | **Flexible** |
|---|---|---|
| Instance type | VMSS VM (special type) | **Real Azure VMs** |
| Heterogeneous sizes | ❌ | ✅ |
| Explicit fault domain placement | ❌ | ✅ (`--platform-fault-domain-count`) |
| Zones + FDs together | Limited | ✅ |
| Standard `az vm` commands | ❌ | ✅ |
| Add an existing VM | ❌ | ✅ |
| Overprovisioning | ✅ | ❌ |
| Max instances | 1,000 (600 with custom image) | **1,000** |
| Autoscale | ✅ | ✅ |
| Recommendation | Legacy | 📌 **Default for new work** |

### 📌 Autoscale mechanics

| Term | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Default cool-down** | **5 minutes** |
| Evaluation frequency | Every **1 minute** |
| **Scale-out** rule | Usually `> 70%` CPU for 10 min → +1 |
| **Scale-in** rule | Usually `< 30%` CPU for 10 min → −1 |
| **Scale out first** | 📌 If any scale-out rule fires, it **wins** over scale-in rules |
| Multiple profiles | Recurrence (schedule) + fixed date + default |
| Metric sources | Host metrics (CPU, network, disk) — no agent; **Guest metrics** (memory, disk queue) need the **Azure Monitor Agent** |
| Minimum | Must be ≥ 0 |
| Flapping prevention | The cool-down period + estimating the post-scale metric |

### ⚠️ The autoscale traps

1. **Memory is NOT a host metric.** To autoscale on memory you need the **Azure Monitor Agent** collecting guest metrics. CPU, network, and disk **are** host metrics (no agent).
2. **Scale-out wins over scale-in.** If both conditions are met, it scales out.
3. **Cool-down default is 5 minutes.** Questions about "it scaled too aggressively" → increase the cool-down or the duration window.
4. **Always define both scale-out AND scale-in rules.** A profile with only a scale-out rule will grow and never shrink.
5. **Make thresholds asymmetric** to prevent flapping (out at 70%, in at 30% — not 70/69).

### Autoscale JSON structure

```json
{
  "profiles": [{
    "name": "Business hours",
    "capacity": { "minimum": "2", "maximum": "10", "default": "2" },
    "rules": [
      {
        "metricTrigger": {
          "metricName": "Percentage CPU",
          "metricResourceUri": "<vmssId>",
          "timeGrain": "PT1M",
          "statistic": "Average",
          "timeWindow": "PT10M",
          "timeAggregation": "Average",
          "operator": "GreaterThan",
          "threshold": 70
        },
        "scaleAction": {
          "direction": "Increase",
          "type": "ChangeCount",
          "value": "1",
          "cooldown": "PT5M"
        }
      },
      {
        "metricTrigger": {
          "metricName": "Percentage CPU",
          "operator": "LessThan",
          "threshold": 30,
          "timeWindow": "PT10M"
        },
        "scaleAction": {
          "direction": "Decrease",
          "type": "ChangeCount",
          "value": "1",
          "cooldown": "PT5M"
        }
      }
    ],
    "recurrence": {
      "frequency": "Week",
      "schedule": { "timeZone": "Eastern Standard Time", "days": ["Monday"], "hours": [8], "minutes": [0] }
    }
  }]
}
```

### Commands

```bash
# Create a Flexible VMSS across 3 zones
az vmss create -g rg-prod -n vmss-web \
  --orchestration-mode Flexible \
  --image Ubuntu2204 --instance-count 2 \
  --vm-sku Standard_D2s_v5 \
  --zones 1 2 3 \
  --upgrade-policy-mode Rolling \
  --admin-username azureuser --generate-ssh-keys \
  --lb lb-web --backend-pool-name bepool

# Manual scale
az vmss scale -g rg-prod -n vmss-web --new-capacity 5

# Autoscale
az monitor autoscale create -g rg-prod --resource vmss-web \
  --resource-type Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachineScaleSets \
  --name autoscale-web --min-count 2 --max-count 10 --count 2

az monitor autoscale rule create -g rg-prod --autoscale-name autoscale-web \
  --condition "Percentage CPU > 70 avg 10m" --scale out 1 --cooldown 5

az monitor autoscale rule create -g rg-prod --autoscale-name autoscale-web \
  --condition "Percentage CPU < 30 avg 10m" --scale in 1 --cooldown 5

# Scheduled profile
az monitor autoscale profile create -g rg-prod --autoscale-name autoscale-web \
  --name "weekday-morning" --min-count 5 --max-count 20 --count 5 \
  --recurrence week mon tue wed thu fri --timezone "Eastern Standard Time" --start 08:00 --end 18:00

# Update the model and apply
az vmss update -g rg-prod -n vmss-web --set virtualMachineProfile.storageProfile.imageReference.version=latest
az vmss update-instances -g rg-prod -n vmss-web --instance-ids "*"

# Instance ops
az vmss list-instances -g rg-prod -n vmss-web -o table
az vmss restart -g rg-prod -n vmss-web --instance-ids 0 1
az vmss reimage -g rg-prod -n vmss-web --instance-ids 0
az vmss delete-instances -g rg-prod -n vmss-web --instance-ids 2

# Automatic repairs
az vmss update -g rg-prod -n vmss-web \
  --set automaticRepairsPolicy.enabled=true automaticRepairsPolicy.gracePeriod=PT30M
```

```powershell
New-AzVmss -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -VMScaleSetName vmss-web -Location eastus `
  -OrchestrationMode Flexible -InstanceCount 2 -ImageName "Ubuntu2204" `
  -Credential (Get-Credential)

Update-AzVmss -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -VMScaleSetName vmss-web -SkuCapacity 5
Add-AzVmssExtension ...
Update-AzVmssInstance -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -VMScaleSetName vmss-web -InstanceId 0
```

---

## 4.6 VM Extensions and Custom Data

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **VM extension** | A small agent-run application that provides post-deployment configuration/automation. Requires the **VM Agent**. |
| **Custom Script Extension** | Downloads and runs a script (Bash/PowerShell) on the VM. |
| **`cloud-init`** | 📌 **Linux-only** industry-standard first-boot configuration; passed via `--custom-data`. Runs **during** provisioning. |
| **Custom data** | Arbitrary data passed to the VM at creation; on Linux consumed by cloud-init, on Windows written to `%SystemDrive%\AzureData\CustomData.bin`. |
| **User data** | Like custom data but **retrievable at any time** from the Instance Metadata Service and **persists** across reboots. |
| **DSC extension** | PowerShell Desired State Configuration. |
| **Azure Monitor Agent (AMA)** | 📌 Current agent for metrics/logs. Replaces the legacy Log Analytics agent (MMA/OMS, retired Aug 2024). |
| **Dependency agent** | Feeds VM Insights service map. |
| **Network Watcher agent** | Enables connection monitor / packet capture. |
| **Azure Disk Encryption extension** | Drives BitLocker/dm-crypt. |
| **VMAccess extension** | Reset password / SSH key / RDP config. |
| **Antimalware extension** | Microsoft Antimalware for Windows. |
| **Instance Metadata Service (IMDS)** | `http://169.254.169.254/metadata/instance?api-version=2021-02-01` — non-routable endpoint returning VM metadata + managed identity tokens. |

### 📌 Custom Script Extension vs cloud-init

| | Custom Script Extension | cloud-init |
|---|---|---|
| OS | **Windows + Linux** | 📌 **Linux only** |
| When it runs | **After** provisioning completes | **During** provisioning |
| Reported in provisioning status? | Yes (as an extension) | Yes (blocks "Succeeded" until done) |
| Can it be added later? | ✅ Yes, to a running VM | ❌ Creation only |
| Script source | Blob storage, GitHub, inline | Inline YAML `#cloud-config` |
| Idempotent | You must handle it | Handled by modules |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Configure a Windows VM at first boot." → **Custom Script Extension** (cloud-init is Linux-only).

### cloud-init example

```yaml
#cloud-config
package_upgrade: true
packages:
  - nginx
  - htop
write_files:
  - path: /var/www/html/index.html
    content: |
      <h1>Hello from cloud-init</h1>
runcmd:
  - systemctl enable nginx
  - systemctl restart nginx
```

```bash
az vm create -g rg -n vm-web --image Ubuntu2204 --custom-data cloud-init.yaml \
  --admin-username azureuser --generate-ssh-keys
```

### Custom Script Extension

```bash
# Linux
az vm extension set -g rg --vm-name vm-web \
  --name CustomScript --publisher Microsoft.Azure.Extensions --version 2.1 \
  --settings '{"fileUris":["https://stacct.blob.core.windows.net/scripts/setup.sh"]}' \
  --protected-settings '{"commandToExecute":"bash setup.sh","storageAccountName":"stacct","storageAccountKey":"<key>"}'

# Windows
az vm extension set -g rg --vm-name vm-win \
  --name CustomScriptExtension --publisher Microsoft.Compute --version 1.10 \
  --settings '{"fileUris":["https://stacct.blob.core.windows.net/scripts/setup.ps1"]}' \
  --protected-settings '{"commandToExecute":"powershell -ExecutionPolicy Unrestricted -File setup.ps1"}'

# Inline
az vm extension set -g rg --vm-name vm-web -n CustomScript \
  --publisher Microsoft.Azure.Extensions \
  --protected-settings '{"commandToExecute":"apt-get update && apt-get install -y nginx"}'

# Run command (no extension needed — great for troubleshooting)
az vm run-command invoke -g rg -n vm-web --command-id RunShellScript --scripts "systemctl status nginx"
az vm run-command invoke -g rg -n vm-win --command-id RunPowerShellScript --scripts "Get-Service W3SVC"

# List / remove extensions
az vm extension list -g rg --vm-name vm-web -o table
az vm extension delete -g rg --vm-name vm-web -n CustomScript
az vm extension image list --location eastus -o table
```

📌 **`protected-settings`** is encrypted and never returned by the API — put **secrets, keys, and commands with credentials** there.
📌 **Run Command** is the answer for "run a one-off script without installing anything" and "the VM is unreachable over the network but I need to run a command."

### Managed Identities

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **System-assigned** | Tied to **one** resource's lifecycle. Created and deleted with the resource. 1:1. |
| **User-assigned** | A standalone Azure resource. Can be attached to **many** resources. Survives resource deletion. 1:many. |
| Where the token comes from | **IMDS** (`169.254.169.254`) |
| Cost | Free |
| Credential management | **None** — Azure rotates automatically |

| | System-assigned | User-assigned |
|---|---|---|
| Lifecycle | Bound to the resource | **Independent** |
| Sharing | ❌ One resource only | ✅ Many resources |
| Deleted when the resource is deleted | ✅ | ❌ |
| RBAC pre-assignment before resource exists | ❌ | ✅ |
| Use for | Simple, single resource | Fleets, VMSS, pre-provisioned access |

```bash
az vm identity assign -g rg -n vm-web                              # system-assigned
az identity create -g rg -n id-app01                               # user-assigned
az vm identity assign -g rg -n vm-web --identities <userAssignedId>

# Grant it access
az role assignment create --assignee <principalId> --role "Storage Blob Data Reader" --scope <saId>

# From inside the VM — get a token
curl -H "Metadata:true" \
 "http://169.254.169.254/metadata/identity/oauth2/token?api-version=2018-02-01&resource=https://storage.azure.com/"
```

⚠️ **Trap:** "An app on a VM needs to read a Key Vault secret without storing credentials." → **Managed identity** + Key Vault RBAC/access policy. Not a service principal with a secret.

---

## 4.7 Images, Galleries, and Capture

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Generalized image** | Machine-specific info removed (**Sysprep** on Windows, `waagent -deprovision+user` on Linux). Used as a **template** for many new VMs. |
| **Specialized image** | Not generalized. A **clone** — keeps hostname, SIDs, user accounts, and the machine's identity. |
| **Managed image** | A standalone image resource in a resource group. Legacy-ish; regional. |
| **Azure Compute Gallery** (formerly Shared Image Gallery) | 📌 The modern way: versioning, **replication to many regions**, **zone-redundant** storage, RBAC sharing, scaling. |
| **Image definition** | The logical grouping: publisher / offer / SKU, OS type, generalized vs specialized, Gen1/Gen2. |
| **Image version** | An actual deployable version, e.g., `1.0.0`. Contains the disk data. |
| **Replica count** | How many copies exist per target region — affects deployment speed at scale. 📌 **1 replica per 20 concurrent VM deployments** is the rule of thumb. |
| **Capture** | Creating an image from an existing VM (⚠️ the VM becomes unusable if generalized). |
| **VM Image Builder** | Managed service (based on Packer) that builds images from a template. |
| **Marketplace image** | Publisher-provided image; URN format `Publisher:Offer:SKU:Version`. |
| **Plan information** | Required for some Marketplace images with licensing terms. |

### 📌 Generalized vs Specialized

| | Generalized | Specialized |
|---|---|---|
| Sysprep / deprovision run? | ✅ **Yes** | ❌ No |
| Prompts for hostname/admin at deploy? | ✅ Yes | ❌ No — reuses the originals |
| Use case | **Golden image** for many VMs | Clone a specific server, dev boxes, quick restore |
| Original VM after capture | ⚠️ **Unusable** (must be deleted) | Still usable |
| Duplicate SIDs risk | ❌ No | ⚠️ Yes if used repeatedly |
| Set at | Image **definition** level | Image definition level |

⚠️ **THE image trap:** After you **Sysprep and capture** a VM as generalized, the **original VM is no longer usable** and must be deleted. If a question mentions keeping the source VM, the answer is **specialized** — or take a **snapshot** instead.

### The generalize + capture workflow 📌

**Windows:**
```
1. RDP to the VM
2. Run: C:\Windows\System32\Sysprep\sysprep.exe
   - System Cleanup Action: "Enter System Out-of-Box Experience (OOBE)"
   - Check "Generalize"
   - Shutdown Options: "Shutdown"
3. az vm deallocate -g rg -n vm-source
4. az vm generalize -g rg -n vm-source
5. az image create / az sig image-version create
```

**Linux:**
```
1. SSH to the VM
2. sudo waagent -deprovision+user -force
3. exit
4. az vm deallocate -g rg -n vm-source
5. az vm generalize -g rg -n vm-source
6. az image create / az sig image-version create
```

📌 **Order matters and is tested:** **Sysprep/deprovision → deallocate → generalize → capture.**

### Azure Compute Gallery structure

```
Azure Compute Gallery  (gal_contoso)
  └── Image Definition  (win2022-web)
        │  publisher: contoso / offer: windows / sku: web-server
        │  osType: Windows / osState: Generalized / hyperVGeneration: V2
        └── Image Version  (1.0.0)
              ├── Source: managed image / VM / snapshot / another version
              └── Target regions: eastus (3 replicas, ZRS), westeurope (2 replicas)
```

### 📌 Gallery vs Managed image

| | Managed image | **Azure Compute Gallery** |
|---|---|---|
| Versioning | ❌ | ✅ |
| Multi-region replication | ❌ (manual copy) | ✅ **Automatic** |
| Zone-redundant storage | ❌ | ✅ |
| Share via RBAC | Limited | ✅ Fine-grained + **community/direct shared galleries** |
| Scale | ~20 concurrent VMs | **1,000s** (with replicas) |
| Deployment speed at scale | Slow | Fast |
| Recommendation | Legacy | 📌 **Use this** |

### Commands

```bash
# Capture to a managed image
az vm deallocate -g rg -n vm-source
az vm generalize -g rg -n vm-source
az image create -g rg -n img-web-v1 --source vm-source --hyper-v-generation V2

# Compute Gallery
az sig create -g rg --gallery-name gal_contoso

az sig image-definition create -g rg --gallery-name gal_contoso \
  --gallery-image-definition win2022-web \
  --publisher contoso --offer windows --sku web-server \
  --os-type Windows --os-state Generalized --hyper-v-generation V2

az sig image-version create -g rg --gallery-name gal_contoso \
  --gallery-image-definition win2022-web --gallery-image-version 1.0.0 \
  --managed-image /subscriptions/.../images/img-web-v1 \
  --target-regions "eastus=3=Standard_ZRS" "westeurope=2" \
  --replica-count 2

# Deploy from the gallery
az vm create -g rg -n vm-new --image /subscriptions/.../galleries/gal_contoso/images/win2022-web/versions/1.0.0

# Marketplace images
az vm image list --output table
az vm image list --publisher Canonical --all -o table
az vm image list-publishers --location eastus -o table
az vm image list-offers --location eastus --publisher Canonical -o table
az vm image list-skus --location eastus --publisher Canonical --offer 0001-com-ubuntu-server-jammy -o table
az vm image show --location eastus --urn Canonical:0001-com-ubuntu-server-jammy:22_04-lts-gen2:latest
az vm image terms accept --urn <publisher:offer:sku:version>
```

---

## 4.8 ARM Templates and Bicep

> Covered structurally in §1.4. This section adds the compute-specific and exam-specific pieces.

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Linked template** | A separate template file referenced by URI. Must be **accessible over HTTP(S)** — typically blob storage + SAS. |
| **Nested template** | A template embedded inline within the parent's `resources`. |
| **Bicep module** | The Bicep equivalent of linked templates. Local file path — no URI or SAS needed. |
| **`copy` element / loops** | Deploy N copies of a resource. |
| **`condition`** | Deploy a resource only if an expression is true. |
| **Parameter file** | `azuredeploy.parameters.json` supplying values. |
| **Template spec** | Store a template as an Azure resource; share via RBAC; versioned. |
| **`what-if`** | Preview the changes a deployment would make. |
| **Deployment stack** | Manages a set of resources as a unit with deny settings and cleanup. |
| **Secure parameter** | `"type": "securestring"` — value never logged. |
| **Key Vault reference** | Pull a secret into a parameter at deploy time. |

### Loops

```json
"copy": {
  "name": "vmLoop",
  "count": "[parameters('vmCount')]",
  "mode": "Parallel",
  "batchSize": 2
},
"name": "[concat('vm-', copyIndex(1))]"
```

```bicep
resource vms 'Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachines@2023-09-01' = [for i in range(0, vmCount): {
  name: 'vm-${i+1}'
  location: location
  properties: { ... }
}]
```

### Conditions

```json
{
  "condition": "[equals(parameters('environment'), 'prod')]",
  "type": "Microsoft.Network/publicIPAddresses",
  "name": "pip-prod"
}
```

```bicep
resource pip 'Microsoft.Network/publicIPAddresses@2023-05-01' = if (environment == 'prod') {
  name: 'pip-prod'
  location: location
}
```

### Key Vault reference in a parameter file

```json
{
  "parameters": {
    "adminPassword": {
      "reference": {
        "keyVault": { "id": "/subscriptions/.../vaults/kv-contoso" },
        "secretName": "vmAdminPassword"
      }
    }
  }
}
```
📌 The Key Vault must have **"Enabled for template deployment"** turned on.

### Template Specs

```bash
az ts create -g rg -n webapp-spec -v 1.0.0 -f main.bicep
az ts list -g rg -o table
az deployment group create -g rg-target \
  --template-spec "/subscriptions/.../templateSpecs/webapp-spec/versions/1.0.0"
```
📌 Template specs are shared with **RBAC (Reader is enough to deploy)** — no need to hand out the file or a SAS URL.

### Deploying at different scopes

```bash
az deployment group create   -g rg     -f main.bicep    # resource group scope
az deployment sub create     -l eastus -f sub.bicep     # subscription scope (creates RGs, policies)
az deployment mg create      -m mgName -l eastus -f mg.bicep
az deployment tenant create  -l eastus -f tenant.bicep
```

⚠️ **Trap:** To **create a resource group** from a template, you must deploy at **subscription scope**, not resource group scope.

### Commands recap

```bash
az bicep install
az bicep upgrade
az bicep build --file main.bicep              # bicep → JSON
az bicep decompile --file main.json           # JSON → bicep (best effort)
az deployment group what-if -g rg -f main.bicep
az deployment group list -g rg -o table
az deployment group show -g rg -n <deploymentName>
az deployment operation group list -g rg -n <deploymentName>   # find WHY it failed
```

📌 **`az deployment operation group list`** is the answer for "how do I find the specific error in a failed multi-resource deployment."

---

## 4.9 Containers: ACI and ACR

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Container** | A lightweight, isolated process bundle sharing the host OS kernel. |
| **Image** | The immutable, layered filesystem template a container runs from. |
| **Registry** | Where images are stored and pulled from. |
| **Docker Hub** | The public default registry. |
| **Azure Container Registry (ACR)** | Private, managed Docker registry in Azure. |
| **Azure Container Instances (ACI)** | 📌 **Serverless containers.** Run a container without managing VMs or orchestrators. Per-second billing. |
| **Container group** | 📌 **The ACI scheduling unit.** Multiple containers sharing a lifecycle, network (**and IP/ports**), and storage volumes — scheduled on the **same host**. Analogous to a Kubernetes **pod**. |
| **Restart policy** | `Always` (default, for services), `OnFailure` (for retryable jobs), `Never` (run once). |
| **Azure Container Apps** | Serverless container platform with **KEDA** scaling, Dapr, revisions, ingress — between ACI and AKS. |
| **Azure Web App for Containers** | App Service running a container. |
| **Manifest** | The metadata describing an image's layers and config. |
| **Tag** | A mutable pointer to an image, e.g., `myapp:v1`, `myapp:latest`. |
| **Repository** | A named collection of images/tags in a registry. |

### 📌 ACR SKUs

| | Basic | Standard | Premium |
|---|---|---|---|
| Included storage | 10 GiB | 100 GiB | **500 GiB** |
| Webhooks | 2 | 10 | 500 |
| **Geo-replication** | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| **Content trust / image signing** | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| **Private endpoint / Private Link** | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| **Customer-managed keys (CMK)** | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| **Repository-scoped tokens** | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| Firewall / VNet rules | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| **Zone redundancy** | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| Throughput | Lowest | Medium | Highest |
| Use for | Learning, dev | Most production | Global, secure, high-scale |

📌 **The Premium-only list is a guaranteed exam question:** **geo-replication, private link/endpoint, content trust, CMK, scoped tokens, zone redundancy.**

⚠️ **Trap:** "Replicate the registry to multiple regions." → **Premium.**
⚠️ **Trap:** "Restrict registry access to a VNet / use a private endpoint." → **Premium.**

### ACR authentication

| Method | Use for | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Entra ID individual identity** | Humans | `az acr login` |
| **Service principal** | CI/CD, external systems | AcrPush / AcrPull role |
| **Managed identity** | Azure resources (AKS, ACI, App Service) | 📌 **Best practice** |
| **Admin account** | Quick tests only | ⚠️ **Disabled by default; single user shared by everyone — do not use in production** |
| **Repository-scoped token** | Fine-grained per-repo access | **Premium only** |

⚠️ **Trap:** The ACR **admin account is disabled by default** and Microsoft explicitly recommends against it. If a question says "the most secure way for an AKS cluster to pull images" → **managed identity** (via `az aks update --attach-acr`), not the admin account.

### 📌 ACR Tasks

| Task type | Trigger |
|---|---|
| **Quick task** (`az acr build`) | On demand — build in the cloud, no local Docker |
| **Automatically triggered task** | **Source code commit** (GitHub/Azure Repos), **base image update**, or a **schedule** |
| **Multi-step task** | YAML-defined build/test/push pipeline |

📌 **Base image update trigger** is the answer for "automatically rebuild my images when the OS base image is patched."

### ACI facts 📌

| Fact | Detail |
|---|---|
| Billing | **Per second**, for vCPU and memory |
| Startup | **Seconds** |
| Max resources | ~4 vCPU / 16 GB (varies by region/OS); GPU SKUs available |
| **Persistent storage** | Mount an **Azure Files** share as a volume |
| Networking | Public IP + **FQDN with a DNS name label**, or deployed **into a VNet** (⚠️ VNet-deployed ACI **cannot have a public IP**) |
| OS | Linux and **Windows** (Windows has fewer features — no VNet in most cases) |
| Container group | Containers share the **IP, port namespace, and lifecycle** |
| **Restart policy** | Always / OnFailure / Never |
| Autoscaling | ❌ **None** — ACI does not autoscale. Use **Container Apps** or **AKS**. |
| Health probes | Liveness + readiness supported |
| Secrets | **Secure environment variables** and **secret volumes** |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Run a batch job that should not restart when it completes successfully." → **Restart policy = `OnFailure`** (or `Never`). The **default is `Always`**, which would restart it forever.
⚠️ **Trap:** "ACI needs to autoscale based on queue length." → ACI **can't**. Use **Azure Container Apps (KEDA)** or AKS.
⚠️ **Trap:** "Persist data written by an ACI container." → Mount an **Azure Files** volume. The container filesystem is ephemeral.

### Commands

```bash
# ACR
az acr create -g rg -n acrcontoso --sku Premium --admin-enabled false
az acr login -n acrcontoso
az acr build -r acrcontoso -t myapp:v1 .            # build in the cloud, no local Docker
az acr repository list -n acrcontoso -o table
az acr repository show-tags -n acrcontoso --repository myapp -o table
az acr repository delete -n acrcontoso --image myapp:v1
az acr replication create -r acrcontoso -l westeurope     # Premium only
az acr task create -r acrcontoso -n build-on-commit \
  --image myapp:{{.Run.ID}} --context https://github.com/contoso/app.git \
  --file Dockerfile --git-access-token <pat>

# Import from Docker Hub
az acr import -n acrcontoso --source docker.io/library/nginx:latest --image nginx:latest

# ACI from ACR using a managed identity
az container create -g rg -n aci-web \
  --image acrcontoso.azurecr.io/myapp:v1 \
  --registry-login-server acrcontoso.azurecr.io \
  --assign-identity <userAssignedIdentityId> \
  --acr-identity <userAssignedIdentityId> \
  --cpu 1 --memory 1.5 \
  --ports 80 --dns-name-label contoso-web --restart-policy OnFailure \
  --environment-variables ENV=prod \
  --secure-environment-variables DB_PASS=<secret>

# Mount Azure Files
az container create -g rg -n aci-data --image nginx \
  --azure-file-volume-account-name stacct \
  --azure-file-volume-account-key <key> \
  --azure-file-volume-share-name myshare \
  --azure-file-volume-mount-path /data

# ACI in a VNet (no public IP possible)
az container create -g rg -n aci-private --image nginx \
  --vnet vnet1 --subnet aci-subnet

az container logs -g rg -n aci-web
az container attach -g rg -n aci-web
az container exec -g rg -n aci-web --exec-command "/bin/bash"
az container show -g rg -n aci-web --query ipAddress.fqdn
az container restart -g rg -n aci-web
az container delete -g rg -n aci-web --yes
```

---

## 4.10 Azure Kubernetes Service (AKS)

> AZ-104 needs **conceptual** AKS knowledge, not deep Kubernetes ops.

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Kubernetes** | Open-source container orchestrator: scheduling, scaling, self-healing, service discovery. |
| **AKS** | Managed Kubernetes. 📌 **The control plane is free and managed by Microsoft; you pay only for the agent nodes** (and their disks/LB). |
| **Control plane** | API server, scheduler, etcd, controller manager — Microsoft-managed, not visible to you. |
| **Node** | A worker VM running your containers. Lives in a node pool. |
| **Node pool** | A group of identical nodes. **System** node pool (runs CoreDNS, metrics-server; must be Linux) vs **User** node pool (your workloads). |
| **Pod** | The smallest deployable unit — one or more containers sharing an IP and storage. |
| **Deployment** | Declarative management of ReplicaSets/Pods; handles rolling updates. |
| **ReplicaSet** | Maintains N pod replicas. |
| **Service** | Stable networking for pods: `ClusterIP` (internal), `NodePort`, **`LoadBalancer`** (provisions an Azure Load Balancer). |
| **Ingress** | HTTP(S) routing (host/path). Backed by an ingress controller (NGINX, **Application Gateway Ingress Controller (AGIC)**, or the managed **Application Routing add-on**). |
| **Namespace** | Logical partition of a cluster. |
| **ConfigMap / Secret** | Non-secret / secret configuration injected into pods. |
| **PersistentVolume (PV) / Claim (PVC)** | Storage abstraction. Azure Disks (RWO) or **Azure Files (RWX)**. |
| **Cluster autoscaler** | Adds/removes **nodes** based on pending pods. |
| **Horizontal Pod Autoscaler (HPA)** | Adds/removes **pods** based on metrics. |
| **KEDA** | Event-driven pod autoscaling (queue length, etc.). |
| **kubenet** | Basic networking. Pods get IPs from a **separate, logical** address space; NAT'd through the node. Fewer VNet IPs used. |
| **Azure CNI** | Pods get **real VNet IPs**. ⚠️ Requires a **large subnet**. Better performance and direct pod addressability. |
| **Azure CNI Overlay** | Pods get IPs from an overlay CIDR — VNet IP savings of kubenet with CNI performance. 📌 Modern default. |
| **Virtual nodes** | Burst pods to **ACI** — serverless scale-out. Requires **Azure CNI**. |
| **AKS + ACR integration** | `az aks update --attach-acr` grants the kubelet identity **AcrPull**. |
| **Workload identity** | Modern way for pods to get Entra tokens (replaces pod-managed identity). |

### 📌 kubenet vs Azure CNI — a guaranteed question

| | kubenet | **Azure CNI** |
|---|---|---|
| Pod IP source | Logical/overlay space | 📌 **The VNet subnet** |
| VNet IPs consumed | Only for **nodes** | **Nodes + every pod** |
| Subnet size needed | Small | ⚠️ **Large** |
| Max pods per node (default) | 110 | **30** (max 250) |
| Pods directly reachable from the VNet | ❌ (NAT) | ✅ |
| **Virtual nodes (ACI)** | ❌ **Not supported** | ✅ **Supported** |
| Windows node pools | ❌ | ✅ |
| Performance | Extra hop (UDR) | Native |
| Complexity | Lower | IP planning required |

📌 **Azure CNI IP math:**
```
IPs needed = (number of nodes + 1 for upgrade surge) × (max pods per node + 1)
```
Example: 3 nodes, 30 max pods → (3+1) × (30+1) = **124 IPs** → a `/25` (128) minimum, realistically `/24`.

⚠️ **Trap:** "AKS deployment fails with insufficient IP addresses." → Azure CNI + too-small subnet. Enlarge the subnet, reduce max-pods, or use **CNI Overlay**/kubenet.
⚠️ **Trap:** "Enable virtual nodes." → **Requires Azure CNI.** kubenet won't work.

### 📌 AKS storage

| Need | Use |
|---|---|
| Single pod, high performance (databases) | **Azure Disk** (`ReadWriteOnce`) |
| **Multiple pods sharing the same volume** | 📌 **Azure Files** (`ReadWriteMany`) |
| High-performance shared NFS | Azure NetApp Files / Azure Files Premium NFS |
| Ephemeral | `emptyDir` |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Multiple pods on different nodes must write to the same volume." → **Azure Files (RWX)**. An Azure **Disk cannot** be `ReadWriteMany`.

### 📌 AKS upgrade

- **Control plane and node pools are upgraded separately.**
- You can **only upgrade one minor version at a time** (e.g., 1.28 → 1.29 → 1.30; not 1.28 → 1.30).
- **Node surge** (`--max-surge`, default 1 / 33% for some) adds temporary nodes to keep capacity during the rolling upgrade.
- **Cordon and drain** moves pods off a node before upgrading it.
- **PodDisruptionBudgets** can block a drain if too few replicas would remain.

```bash
az aks get-upgrades -g rg -n aks-prod -o table
az aks upgrade -g rg -n aks-prod --kubernetes-version 1.29.2
az aks nodepool upgrade -g rg --cluster-name aks-prod -n userpool --kubernetes-version 1.29.2
```

### Commands

```bash
az aks create -g rg -n aks-prod \
  --node-count 3 --node-vm-size Standard_D2s_v5 \
  --network-plugin azure --network-plugin-mode overlay \
  --enable-managed-identity \
  --enable-cluster-autoscaler --min-count 3 --max-count 10 \
  --zones 1 2 3 \
  --attach-acr acrcontoso \
  --enable-addons monitoring \
  --generate-ssh-keys

az aks get-credentials -g rg -n aks-prod
kubectl get nodes
kubectl get pods -A

az aks nodepool add -g rg --cluster-name aks-prod -n gpupool \
  --node-count 1 --node-vm-size Standard_NC6s_v3 --node-taints sku=gpu:NoSchedule

az aks scale -g rg -n aks-prod --node-count 5
az aks update -g rg -n aks-prod --enable-cluster-autoscaler --min-count 2 --max-count 20
az aks update -g rg -n aks-prod --attach-acr acrcontoso
az aks stop  -g rg -n aks-prod     # stops billing for nodes
az aks start -g rg -n aks-prod
```

📌 **`az aks stop`** deallocates the node pool — the answer for "reduce dev cluster costs overnight."

---

## 4.11 Azure App Service

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **App Service** | PaaS for hosting web apps, REST APIs, and mobile backends. |
| **App Service plan (ASP)** | 📌 **The VMs your apps run on.** Defines region, OS, size, instance count, and **what you pay for**. |
| **Web App** | The application resource hosted in a plan. |
| **Function App** | Serverless functions; can run on a Consumption, Premium, or App Service plan. |
| **Logic App** | Workflow automation with connectors. |
| **App Service Environment (ASE)** | 📌 **Single-tenant, fully isolated** App Service deployed **into your VNet**. For Isolated tier. |
| **Deployment slot** | 📌 A **live app with its own hostname** in the same plan — for staging, testing, and **swaps**. |
| **Swap** | Exchange the content and config of two slots. **Warms up** the target first → **zero downtime**. |
| **Auto swap** | Automatically swap to production after a successful deploy to staging. |
| **Slot setting / "Deployment slot setting"** | 📌 An app setting or connection string marked as **sticky** — it **stays with the slot** and does NOT swap. |
| **Scale up** | Change the **plan tier/SKU** (vertical). |
| **Scale out** | Change the **instance count** (horizontal). |
| **Autoscale** | Rule-based instance count changes (Standard+). |
| **Always On** | Keeps the app loaded (prevents unload after 20 min idle). Basic tier and above. |
| **VNet integration** | 📌 **Outbound** — lets the app reach resources in a VNet. |
| **Private endpoint** | 📌 **Inbound** — gives the app a private IP for incoming traffic. |
| **Hybrid connections** | Reach a specific on-prem host:port via Relay, no VPN. |
| **Custom domain** | Your own DNS name, verified with a **TXT** record (`asuid.<name>`) plus a **CNAME** or **A** record. |
| **TLS/SSL binding** | SNI SSL (free-ish, most common) or IP-based SSL (needs a dedicated IP). |
| **App Service Managed Certificate** | **Free** TLS cert from Microsoft. ⚠️ Does **not** support naked/apex domains in all cases, no wildcard, auto-renews. |
| **Kudu / SCM site** | The advanced tools site: `https://<app>.scm.azurewebsites.net`. |
| **WEBSITE_RUN_FROM_PACKAGE** | Run the app from a zip — atomic, read-only deployments. |
| **Deployment Center** | Configure CI/CD from GitHub, Azure Repos, Bitbucket, containers. |

### 📌 App Service plan tiers

| Tier | Compute | Custom domain | TLS | Scale out | Slots | VNet integration | Autoscale | Backups | SLA |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Free (F1)** | **Shared** | ❌ | ❌ | 1 | 0 | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | **None** |
| **Shared (D1)** | **Shared** | ✅ | ❌ | 1 | 0 | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ | **None** |
| **Basic (B1–B3)** | Dedicated | ✅ | ✅ | **Manual, 3** | **0** ⚠️ | ✅ | ❌ | ❌ | 99.95% |
| **Standard (S1–S3)** | Dedicated | ✅ | ✅ | **Auto, 10** | **5** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ Daily | 99.95% |
| **Premium (P1v3–P3v3)** | Dedicated | ✅ | ✅ | **Auto, 30** | **20** | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | 99.95% |
| **Isolated (I1v2+) / ASE** | **Dedicated + your VNet** | ✅ | ✅ | **Auto, 100** | **20** | ✅ (native) | ✅ | ✅ | 99.95% |

### 📌 The App Service tier traps

1. **Free and Shared have NO SLA** and run on **shared** compute with **CPU quotas** (F1: 60 CPU-min/day).
2. **Free does not support custom domains.** Shared (D1) does.
3. ⚠️ **Basic has NO deployment slots and NO autoscale.** 📌 **Deployment slots start at STANDARD.** This is asked constantly.
4. **Standard = 5 slots. Premium = 20 slots.**
5. **Isolated/ASE** is the only tier that gets full **network isolation in your own VNet**.
6. **You pay for the App Service PLAN, not the apps.** 10 apps in one plan = one bill. But they share CPU/RAM.
7. **Always On** requires Basic+ — Free/Shared apps unload after **20 minutes** of inactivity.

### 📌 Deployment slots — how swap works

```
1. You deploy new code to the "staging" slot
2. You click Swap
3. Azure applies the PRODUCTION slot's config to the staging instances
4. Azure WARMS UP staging (sends a request to "/" and waits for a response)
5. Once warm, Azure swaps the ROUTING — staging becomes production
6. The old production code now sits in the staging slot (instant rollback: swap again!)
```

📌 **The warm-up is why swaps are zero-downtime.** Restarting the app directly is not.
📌 **Rollback = swap back.** Instant.

### 📌 What swaps and what doesn't

| **Swaps with the slot** (follows the code) | **Stays with the slot** (sticky) |
|---|---|
| App settings (unless marked as slot settings) | ⚠️ **Settings marked "Deployment slot setting"** |
| Connection strings (unless marked) | ⚠️ **Connection strings marked "Deployment slot setting"** |
| Handler mappings | **Publishing endpoints** |
| Public certificates | **Custom domain names** |
| WebJobs content | **Non-public certificates and TLS bindings** |
| Framework version, 32/64-bit, web sockets | **Scale settings** |
| Path-based CORS (some) | **WebJobs schedulers** |
| Hybrid connections* | **IP restrictions** |
| Service endpoints* | **Always On** |
| Azure CDN* | **Diagnostic log settings** |
| | **CORS** |
| | **VNet integration** |
| | **Managed identities** |
| | **Private endpoints** |

📌 **The memorable rule:** *Content and app settings swap. Network, scale, identity, and anything you marked "slot setting" stay put.*

⚠️ **THE slot trap:** "The staging slot must point to the test database and production to the prod database." → Mark the connection string as a **"Deployment slot setting"** so it does **not** swap.

### 📌 Scaling

| | Scale up | Scale out |
|---|---|---|
| Changes | The **plan tier/SKU** | The **instance count** |
| Direction | Vertical | Horizontal |
| Effect | More CPU/RAM/disk per instance, unlocks features | More copies behind the built-in load balancer |
| Where | App Service plan → **Scale up** | App Service plan → **Scale out** |
| Autoscale available | n/a | **Standard+** |
| Applies to | Every app in the plan | Every app in the plan |

📌 **Scaling applies to the PLAN — every app in it scales together.** If one app is noisy, **move it to its own plan**.

### 📌 VNet integration vs Private endpoint

| | **Regional VNet integration** | **Private endpoint** |
|---|---|---|
| Direction | 📌 **OUTBOUND** (app → VNet) | 📌 **INBOUND** (VNet → app) |
| Gives the app a private IP? | ❌ (it gets an IP from the delegated subnet for outbound) | ✅ |
| Subnet | Must be **delegated** to `Microsoft.Web/serverFarms`, **empty**, min `/28` (recommended `/26`) | Any subnet |
| Tier required | Basic+ (Standard+ recommended) | **PremiumV2/V3, Isolated** (and Functions Premium) |
| Removes the public endpoint? | ❌ | ✅ (when public access is disabled) |
| Use case | App must reach a VM/SQL/storage in the VNet | App must only be reachable from the VNet/on-prem |

⚠️ **Trap:** "The web app must connect to a SQL Server on a VM in a VNet." → **VNet integration** (outbound).
⚠️ **Trap:** "The web app must only be reachable from the corporate network." → **Private endpoint** (inbound) + disable public access.
⚠️ **Trap:** The integration subnet must be **empty and delegated**. It cannot be shared with other resources.

### Custom domain + certificate

```
1. Add a TXT record:  asuid.www.contoso.com  →  <custom domain verification ID>
2. Add a CNAME:       www.contoso.com        →  myapp.azurewebsites.net
   (or an A record to the app's inbound IP for an apex/naked domain)
3. App Service → Custom domains → Add
4. App Service → TLS/SSL settings → Private Key Certificates → Create App Service Managed Certificate
5. Bind it (SNI SSL)
6. Enable "HTTPS Only"
```

⚠️ **Trap:** For a **naked/apex domain** (`contoso.com`, no `www`) you need an **A record** (CNAME is not allowed at the apex by DNS rules) **plus** the TXT verification record — or use Azure DNS **alias records**.

### Commands

```bash
# Plan and app
az appservice plan create -g rg -n plan-web --sku S1 --is-linux --number-of-workers 2
az webapp create -g rg -p plan-web -n app-contoso --runtime "NODE:20-lts"

# Scale
az appservice plan update -g rg -n plan-web --sku P1V3          # scale UP
az appservice plan update -g rg -n plan-web --number-of-workers 5  # scale OUT (manual)

az monitor autoscale create -g rg --resource plan-web \
  --resource-type Microsoft.Web/serverfarms --name autoscale-web \
  --min-count 2 --max-count 10 --count 2
az monitor autoscale rule create -g rg --autoscale-name autoscale-web \
  --condition "CpuPercentage > 70 avg 10m" --scale out 1

# Slots
az webapp deployment slot create -g rg -n app-contoso --slot staging
az webapp deployment slot swap   -g rg -n app-contoso --slot staging --target-slot production
az webapp deployment slot auto-swap -g rg -n app-contoso --slot staging --auto-swap-slot production
az webapp deployment slot list -g rg -n app-contoso -o table

# Sticky (slot) settings
az webapp config appsettings set -g rg -n app-contoso --slot staging --settings DB_NAME=testdb
az webapp config appsettings set -g rg -n app-contoso --slot-settings DB_NAME=proddb   # sticky!
az webapp config connection-string set -g rg -n app-contoso -t SQLAzure \
  --slot-settings DefaultConnection="<conn>"

# Networking
az webapp vnet-integration add -g rg -n app-contoso --vnet vnet1 --subnet app-subnet
az network private-endpoint create -g rg -n pe-app --vnet-name vnet1 --subnet subnet1 \
  --private-connection-resource-id <appId> --group-id sites --connection-name pe-conn

# Custom domain + free cert
az webapp config hostname add -g rg --webapp-name app-contoso --hostname www.contoso.com
az webapp config ssl create -g rg --name app-contoso --hostname www.contoso.com   # managed cert
az webapp config ssl bind -g rg -n app-contoso --certificate-thumbprint <tp> --ssl-type SNI
az webapp update -g rg -n app-contoso --https-only true

# Config
az webapp config set -g rg -n app-contoso --always-on true --min-tls-version 1.2 --ftps-state Disabled
az webapp identity assign -g rg -n app-contoso
az webapp log tail -g rg -n app-contoso
az webapp restart -g rg -n app-contoso
```

---
---

# Part 5 — Domain 4: Implement and Manage Virtual Networking (15–20%)

## 5.1 Virtual Networks and Subnets

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Virtual network (VNet)** | Your private, isolated network in Azure. The fundamental building block. Scoped to **one region** and **one subscription**. |
| **Address space** | The CIDR block(s) assigned to the VNet, e.g., `10.0.0.0/16`. A VNet can have **multiple** address spaces. |
| **Subnet** | A subdivision of the VNet address space. Resources attach to subnets. |
| **CIDR** (Classless Inter-Domain Routing) | Notation for an address range: `10.0.0.0/24` = 256 addresses. |
| **Isolation boundary** | VNets are isolated from each other by default — no communication without peering, VPN, or ExpressRoute. |
| **Regional scope** | 📌 **A VNet cannot span regions.** Connect regional VNets with **global peering**. |
| **Subscription scope** | 📌 A VNet lives in **one subscription**, but can be peered to VNets in other subscriptions/tenants. |
| **Reserved addresses** | 📌 Azure reserves **5 IPs per subnet**. |
| **Delegated subnet** | A subnet handed to a specific Azure service (e.g., `Microsoft.Web/serverFarms`, `Microsoft.Sql/managedInstances`). Only that service can use it. |
| **Dedicated subnet** | A subnet reserved by name for a service: `GatewaySubnet`, `AzureFirewallSubnet`, `AzureBastionSubnet`, `RouteServerSubnet`. |
| **DDoS Protection** | Basic (free, always on, platform-level) vs **Network Protection / Standard** (paid, per-VNet, tuned policies, cost protection, rapid response team). |
| **Azure-provided DNS** | Default recursive resolver at **`168.63.129.16`**. |
| **`168.63.129.16`** | 📌 The magic virtual public IP: DNS, DHCP lease, health probe source, and communication channel with the platform. **Never block it.** |

### 📌 The 5 reserved IPs per subnet — memorize

For subnet `10.0.1.0/24`:

| Address | Reserved for |
|---|---|
| `10.0.1.0` | **Network address** |
| `10.0.1.1` | **Default gateway** |
| `10.0.1.2` | Azure DNS mapping |
| `10.0.1.3` | Azure DNS mapping |
| `10.0.1.255` | **Broadcast address** |

**Usable = 256 − 5 = 251.**

### 📌 The CIDR table — memorize

| CIDR | Total IPs | **Usable in Azure** | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| /29 | 8 | **3** | 📌 **Smallest allowed subnet** |
| /28 | 16 | 11 | Minimum for VNet integration |
| /27 | 32 | 27 | Minimum for **AzureBastionSubnet** (/26 recommended) |
| /26 | 64 | 59 | **AzureFirewallSubnet** minimum |
| /25 | 128 | 123 | |
| /24 | 256 | **251** | 📌 The typical subnet |
| /23 | 512 | 507 | |
| /22 | 1,024 | 1,019 | |
| /21 | 2,048 | 2,043 | |
| /20 | 4,096 | 4,091 | |
| /16 | 65,536 | 65,531 | 📌 The typical VNet |
| /8 | 16,777,216 | — | 📌 **Largest allowed VNet** |

⚠️ **Trap:** "The smallest subnet is /28." → **FALSE. It's /29** (3 usable). The smallest **recommended** is /28, but /29 is the hard minimum.
⚠️ **Trap:** "How many VMs fit in a /29?" → **3**, not 8, not 6.

### 📌 Private address ranges (RFC 1918)

| Range | CIDR | Size |
|---|---|---|
| `10.0.0.0` – `10.255.255.255` | `10.0.0.0/8` | 16.7M |
| `172.16.0.0` – `172.31.255.255` | `172.16.0.0/12` | 1M |
| `192.168.0.0` – `192.168.255.255` | `192.168.0.0/16` | 65K |

📌 You **can** use public IP ranges inside a VNet, but Azure will treat them as private and you lose the ability to reach the real internet hosts at those addresses.

⚠️ **Restricted ranges Azure will NOT let you use:**
- `224.0.0.0/4` (multicast)
- `255.255.255.255/32` (broadcast)
- `127.0.0.0/8` (loopback)
- `169.254.0.0/16` (link-local)
- `168.63.129.16/32` (Azure internal DNS)

### 📌 Dedicated subnet names (exact spelling matters)

| Subnet name | For | Minimum size |
|---|---|---|
| **`GatewaySubnet`** | VPN Gateway / ExpressRoute Gateway | `/29` (⚠️ **`/27` recommended**, `/26` for coexistence) |
| **`AzureFirewallSubnet`** | Azure Firewall | **`/26`** |
| **`AzureFirewallManagementSubnet`** | Firewall forced-tunneling mgmt | `/26` |
| **`AzureBastionSubnet`** | Azure Bastion | **`/26`** (was `/27` minimum) |
| **`RouteServerSubnet`** | Azure Route Server | `/27` |
| **`AzureRouteServerSubnet`** | (alias) | `/27` |

⚠️ **THE naming trap:** These names are **case-sensitive and exact**. `GatewaySubnet` — not `Gateway-Subnet`, not `gatewaysubnet`. Deployment fails otherwise.
⚠️ **Trap:** You **cannot** attach an NSG to `GatewaySubnet` in most configurations (it breaks the gateway), and `AzureBastionSubnet` has very specific NSG requirements.

### 📌 VNet rules

| Rule | Detail |
|---|---|
| Regional | One VNet = one region. Cannot span. |
| Subscription | One VNet = one subscription. |
| Address spaces per VNet | Multiple allowed |
| Subnets per VNet | 3,000 |
| VNets per subscription per region | 1,000 (soft) |
| **Overlapping address spaces** | ⚠️ **Cannot peer** VNets with overlapping ranges |
| Resizing the address space | ✅ Possible (can add/remove/resize prefixes) — but you **cannot shrink** a range that has subnets in it |
| Resizing a **subnet** | ✅ Possible, but only if **no resources** are in the affected range |
| Deleting a subnet | Must be **empty** first |
| Private IP assignment | **Dynamic** (default, DHCP from the subnet) or **Static** |
| DHCP lease | Effectively infinite; the IP is held by the NIC |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Change a VM's private IP from dynamic to static." → Do it on the **NIC's IP configuration**, not inside the guest OS. ⚠️ **Never set a static IP inside the guest OS** — Azure's DHCP won't know, and the VM will lose connectivity on a lease renewal.

### Commands

```bash
az network vnet create -g rg -n vnet1 \
  --address-prefixes 10.0.0.0/16 10.1.0.0/16 \
  --subnet-name web --subnet-prefixes 10.0.1.0/24 \
  --location eastus

az network vnet subnet create -g rg --vnet-name vnet1 -n app --address-prefixes 10.0.2.0/24
az network vnet subnet create -g rg --vnet-name vnet1 -n GatewaySubnet --address-prefixes 10.0.255.0/27
az network vnet subnet create -g rg --vnet-name vnet1 -n AzureFirewallSubnet --address-prefixes 10.0.254.0/26
az network vnet subnet create -g rg --vnet-name vnet1 -n AzureBastionSubnet --address-prefixes 10.0.253.0/26

# Delegate a subnet
az network vnet subnet update -g rg --vnet-name vnet1 -n app-integration \
  --delegations Microsoft.Web/serverFarms

# Add an address space
az network vnet update -g rg -n vnet1 --address-prefixes 10.0.0.0/16 10.2.0.0/16

az network vnet list -o table
az network vnet show -g rg -n vnet1
az network vnet subnet list -g rg --vnet-name vnet1 -o table
az network vnet list-available-ips -g rg -n vnet1   # (via subnet)
az network vnet subnet delete -g rg --vnet-name vnet1 -n app
```

```powershell
$web = New-AzVirtualNetworkSubnetConfig -Name web -AddressPrefix 10.0.1.0/24
$app = New-AzVirtualNetworkSubnetConfig -Name app -AddressPrefix 10.0.2.0/24
New-AzVirtualNetwork -ResourceGroupName rg -Name vnet1 -Location eastus `
  -AddressPrefix 10.0.0.0/16 -Subnet $web,$app

$vnet = Get-AzVirtualNetwork -ResourceGroupName rg -Name vnet1
Add-AzVirtualNetworkSubnetConfig -Name db -AddressPrefix 10.0.3.0/24 -VirtualNetwork $vnet
$vnet | Set-AzVirtualNetwork
```

---

## 5.2 IP Addressing

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Private IP** | An address from the VNet's address space, assigned to a NIC. Used for internal communication. |
| **Public IP (PIP)** | An internet-routable address. A **separate Azure resource**. |
| **Dynamic allocation** | The address is assigned when the resource starts and **released on deallocation**. |
| **Static allocation** | The address is reserved and **persists** through deallocation. |
| **Basic SKU PIP** | Legacy. Dynamic or static. **Open by default** (no NSG required). ⚠️ **Retired September 30, 2025.** |
| **Standard SKU PIP** | 📌 **Always static.** **Secure by default — closed to all inbound unless an NSG allows it.** Zone-redundant or zonal. Required for Standard Load Balancer. |
| **Zone-redundant PIP** | A Standard PIP available across all zones in the region. |
| **Zonal PIP** | A Standard PIP pinned to a single zone. |
| **Public IP prefix** | A **contiguous block** of Standard public IPs reserved for you — predictable ranges for partner firewall allow-lists. |
| **NIC** | Network interface card resource — links a VM to a subnet. |
| **IP configuration** | The private IP (+ optional public IP) on a NIC. A NIC can have **multiple** IP configurations. |
| **Primary NIC** | 📌 The NIC that carries the **default route**. A multi-NIC VM has exactly one primary. |
| **Secondary IP config** | Additional private/public IPs on the same NIC — for hosting multiple SSL sites, etc. |
| **DNS name label** | Creates `<label>.<region>.cloudapp.azure.com` for a PIP. |
| **Instance-level public IP (ILPIP)** | A PIP directly on a VM's NIC. |
| **Outbound SNAT** | How VMs without a PIP reach the internet. |
| **Default outbound access** | ⚠️ **Being retired (Sept 30, 2025)** — new VNets require an **explicit** outbound method (NAT Gateway, LB outbound rule, or a PIP). |

### 📌 Basic vs Standard Public IP — a guaranteed question

| | **Basic** (retired) | **Standard** |
|---|---|---|
| Allocation | Dynamic **or** Static | 📌 **Always Static** |
| **Default security** | ⚠️ **OPEN** — allows inbound by default | 📌 **CLOSED** — an **NSG is required** to permit inbound |
| Availability Zones | ❌ Not zone-aware | ✅ Zonal or zone-redundant |
| SLA | None | **99.99%** |
| Load Balancer compat | Basic LB only | Standard LB only |
| Outbound SNAT | Implicit | Explicit (LB rule / NAT Gateway) |
| Cost | Free/cheap | Charged |
| Status | ⚠️ **Retired 30 Sept 2025** | 📌 Current |

⚠️ **THE PIP trap:** A **Standard** public IP is **secure by default** — traffic is **denied** unless an NSG explicitly allows it. Attach a Standard PIP to a VM with no NSG and **nothing can reach it**. A Basic PIP is **open by default**.

⚠️ **Trap:** "You cannot mix SKUs." → Correct. A **Basic** PIP cannot be used with a **Standard** Load Balancer, and vice versa. All resources in a load-balanced set must match SKU.

⚠️ **Trap:** "The VM's public IP changed." → Dynamic PIP + **deallocation**. Make it **Static**.

### 📌 Multiple NICs and IPs

| Fact | Detail |
|---|---|
| NICs per VM | Depends on the **VM size** (e.g., D2s_v5 = 2, D8s_v5 = 4). Check `az vm list-sizes`. |
| Adding/removing a NIC | ⚠️ The VM must be **deallocated (stopped)** |
| All NICs on a VM | Must be in the **same VNet** (different subnets allowed) and the **same region/subscription** |
| Primary NIC | Carries the default route; only one |
| IP configs per NIC | Up to **256** |
| Public IPs per NIC | One per IP configuration |
| ⚠️ Guest OS config | For secondary NICs/IPs you often must configure the OS manually (routes, addresses) |

```bash
az network public-ip create -g rg -n pip-web --sku Standard --allocation-method Static \
  --zone 1 2 3 --dns-name contoso-web --version IPv4

az network public-ip prefix create -g rg -n pipprefix --length 28 --location eastus

az network nic create -g rg -n nic-web-01 --vnet-name vnet1 --subnet web \
  --public-ip-address pip-web --network-security-group nsg-web \
  --private-ip-address 10.0.1.10 --accelerated-networking true

# Static private IP
az network nic ip-config update -g rg --nic-name nic-web-01 -n ipconfig1 \
  --private-ip-address 10.0.1.10 --set privateIpAllocationMethod=Static

# Secondary IP config
az network nic ip-config create -g rg --nic-name nic-web-01 -n ipconfig2 \
  --private-ip-address 10.0.1.11

# Attach a NIC (VM must be deallocated)
az vm deallocate -g rg -n vm-web-01
az vm nic add -g rg --vm-name vm-web-01 --nics nic-web-02
az vm start -g rg -n vm-web-01

az network public-ip list -o table
az network nic list -g rg -o table
az network nic show-effective-route-table -g rg -n nic-web-01 -o table
```

---

## 5.3 Network Security Groups (NSGs)

**An NSG is a stateful packet filter (a 5-tuple firewall) applied to subnets and/or NICs.**

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **NSG** | A list of allow/deny rules for inbound and outbound traffic. |
| **Security rule** | Priority + direction + source + destination + port + protocol + action. |
| **Priority** | **100–4096**. 📌 **Lower number = evaluated first. First match wins; evaluation stops.** |
| **Direction** | Inbound or Outbound — evaluated as **separate rule sets**. |
| **Source / Destination** | IP/CIDR, **service tag**, **ASG**, or `Any`/`*`. |
| **Port range** | Single (`80`), range (`1000-2000`), list (`80,443`), or `*`. |
| **Protocol** | TCP, UDP, ICMP, ESP, AH, or Any. |
| **Action** | Allow or Deny. |
| **Stateful** | 📌 **Return traffic is automatically allowed.** If you allow inbound 443, you do **not** need an outbound rule for the response. |
| **Default rules** | 6 built-in rules (3 inbound, 3 outbound) with priorities **65000–65500**. **Cannot be deleted**, but can be **overridden** by a lower-priority (higher-precedence) rule. |
| **Service tag** | A Microsoft-managed label for a set of IP prefixes: `Internet`, `VirtualNetwork`, `AzureLoadBalancer`, `Storage`, `Sql`, `AzureCloud`, `AzureActiveDirectory`, `AzureMonitor`, `AzureBastion`, etc. **Auto-updated by Microsoft.** |
| **ASG (Application Security Group)** | A named logical group of NICs usable as a source/destination. |
| **Effective security rules** | The computed union of subnet NSG + NIC NSG for a given NIC. 📌 **The troubleshooting tool.** |
| **NSG flow logs** | Records allowed/denied flows to a storage account; analyzed with **Traffic Analytics**. |
| **Augmented rules** | Multiple IPs/ports/tags/ASGs in a single rule. |

### 📌 The 6 default rules — memorize

**Inbound:**

| Priority | Name | Source | Destination | Port | Action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **65000** | `AllowVnetInBound` | `VirtualNetwork` | `VirtualNetwork` | Any | **Allow** |
| **65001** | `AllowAzureLoadBalancerInBound` | `AzureLoadBalancer` | Any | Any | **Allow** |
| **65500** | `DenyAllInBound` | Any | Any | Any | **Deny** |

**Outbound:**

| Priority | Name | Source | Destination | Port | Action |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **65000** | `AllowVnetOutBound` | `VirtualNetwork` | `VirtualNetwork` | Any | **Allow** |
| **65001** | `AllowInternetOutBound` | Any | `Internet` | Any | **Allow** |
| **65500** | `DenyAllOutBound` | Any | Any | Any | **Deny** |

📌 **The consequences:**
- **All VNet-internal traffic is allowed by default** (65000 inbound + outbound).
- **All outbound internet traffic is allowed by default** (65001).
- **All inbound internet traffic is denied by default** (65500).
- The `AzureLoadBalancer` tag must stay allowed or **health probes fail**.

⚠️ **Trap:** "Deny all outbound internet but allow Azure Storage." → Create a **Deny** rule for `Internet` at (say) priority 200, and an **Allow** rule for the `Storage` service tag at priority **100** (lower number wins).
⚠️ **Trap:** "Blocking `AzureLoadBalancer` inbound." → **Health probes break → the backend is marked unhealthy → the LB stops sending traffic.**

### 📌 NSG evaluation order — THE most tested networking concept

```
INBOUND traffic to a VM:
  1. SUBNET NSG evaluated FIRST
        ↓ (if allowed)
  2. NIC NSG evaluated SECOND
        ↓ (if allowed)
  3. Packet reaches the VM

OUTBOUND traffic from a VM:
  1. NIC NSG evaluated FIRST
        ↓ (if allowed)
  2. SUBNET NSG evaluated SECOND
        ↓ (if allowed)
  3. Packet leaves
```

📌 **The mnemonic: traffic passes through the NSGs in the order it physically encounters them.**
- Coming **in** from outside → hits the subnet edge first, then the NIC.
- Going **out** from the VM → hits the NIC first, then the subnet edge.

📌 **BOTH NSGs must allow the traffic.** If either denies, the packet is dropped. It's an **AND**, not an OR.

⚠️ **THE NSG trap:** "The subnet NSG allows port 80 but the NIC NSG doesn't have a rule for it." → **BLOCKED.** The NIC NSG's default `DenyAllInBound` (65500) drops it. Both must allow.

⚠️ **Trap:** Within **one** NSG, evaluation is **first-match-wins by priority**. A Deny at 100 beats an Allow at 200. There is no "deny always wins" rule inside a single NSG — **priority decides**.

### 📌 NSG rules and limits

| Fact | Value |
|---|---|
| Rules per NSG | **1,000** (default; raisable) |
| Priority range | **100–4096** (user rules) |
| NSGs per subscription per region | 5,000 |
| NSGs per NIC | **1** |
| NSGs per subnet | **1** |
| Can one NSG be attached to many subnets/NICs? | ✅ **Yes** |
| Stateful? | ✅ **Yes** |
| Does it filter intra-subnet traffic? | ⚠️ A **subnet** NSG **does** filter VM-to-VM traffic within the same subnet |
| Does it apply to `168.63.129.16`? | ⚠️ Outbound to the platform DNS/probe IP should always be allowed |
| Can you delete default rules? | ❌ No — only override |
| ICMP | Supported as a protocol; ⚠️ "Ping fails" is usually an NSG or in-guest firewall issue |

### 📌 Service tags you must know

| Tag | Represents |
|---|---|
| **`VirtualNetwork`** | All VNet address space + peered VNets + on-prem via gateway |
| **`Internet`** | Everything outside the VNet and Azure |
| **`AzureLoadBalancer`** | 📌 The health probe source (`168.63.129.16`) |
| **`AzureCloud`** | All Azure public IPs (regional: `AzureCloud.EastUS`) |
| **`Storage`** | Azure Storage (regional: `Storage.EastUS`) |
| **`Sql`** | Azure SQL / Synapse |
| **`AzureActiveDirectory`** | Entra ID endpoints |
| **`AzureMonitor`** | Log Analytics, App Insights ingestion |
| **`AzureKeyVault`** | Key Vault |
| **`AzureBastion`** | Bastion service |
| **`AzureContainerRegistry`** | ACR |
| **`AzureTrafficManager`** | Traffic Manager probes |
| **`GatewayManager`** | VPN/App Gateway management traffic |
| **`AzureResourceManager`** | ARM control plane |
| **`VirtualNetworkServiceEndpoint`** | (implicit with service endpoints) |

📌 **Why service tags matter:** Microsoft maintains the IP list. You write `Storage` instead of hunting down hundreds of prefixes that change monthly.

⚠️ **Trap:** `VirtualNetwork` includes **peered VNets and on-prem networks reachable via the gateway** — not just the local VNet. That's wider than people expect.

### Commands

```bash
az network nsg create -g rg -n nsg-web

# Allow HTTPS from the internet
az network nsg rule create -g rg --nsg-name nsg-web -n AllowHTTPS \
  --priority 100 --direction Inbound --access Allow --protocol Tcp \
  --source-address-prefixes Internet --source-port-ranges '*' \
  --destination-address-prefixes '*' --destination-port-ranges 443

# Allow SSH only from the corporate range
az network nsg rule create -g rg --nsg-name nsg-web -n AllowSSHCorp \
  --priority 110 --direction Inbound --access Allow --protocol Tcp \
  --source-address-prefixes 203.0.113.0/24 --destination-port-ranges 22

# Allow Storage outbound, deny the rest of the internet
az network nsg rule create -g rg --nsg-name nsg-web -n AllowStorage \
  --priority 100 --direction Outbound --access Allow --protocol Tcp \
  --destination-address-prefixes Storage.EastUS --destination-port-ranges 443

az network nsg rule create -g rg --nsg-name nsg-web -n DenyInternet \
  --priority 200 --direction Outbound --access Deny --protocol '*' \
  --destination-address-prefixes Internet --destination-port-ranges '*'

# Associate
az network vnet subnet update -g rg --vnet-name vnet1 -n web --network-security-group nsg-web
az network nic update -g rg -n nic-web-01 --network-security-group nsg-nic

# ⚠️ TROUBLESHOOTING — the effective rules
az network nic list-effective-nsg -g rg -n nic-web-01
az network nic show-effective-route-table -g rg -n nic-web-01 -o table

az network nsg rule list -g rg --nsg-name nsg-web --include-default -o table
az network list-service-tags --location eastus
```

```powershell
$rule = New-AzNetworkSecurityRuleConfig -Name AllowHTTPS -Priority 100 `
  -Direction Inbound -Access Allow -Protocol Tcp `
  -SourceAddressPrefix Internet -SourcePortRange * `
  -DestinationAddressPrefix * -DestinationPortRange 443

New-AzNetworkSecurityGroup -ResourceGroupName rg -Name nsg-web -Location eastus -SecurityRules $rule

Get-AzEffectiveNetworkSecurityGroup -NetworkInterfaceName nic-web-01 -ResourceGroupName rg
Get-AzEffectiveRouteTable -NetworkInterfaceName nic-web-01 -ResourceGroupName rg
```

📌 **`list-effective-nsg` / `Get-AzEffectiveNetworkSecurityGroup`** is *the* answer to "how do I troubleshoot why traffic is blocked."

---

## 5.4 Application Security Groups (ASGs)

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **ASG** | A **named logical grouping of NICs** you can use as the source or destination in an NSG rule — instead of IP addresses. |
| **Membership** | You attach a NIC's **IP configuration** to an ASG. |
| **Benefit** | Rules describe **application roles** ("web", "db") rather than fragile IP ranges. Add a new VM to the ASG and it inherits the rules automatically. |

### 📌 ASG rules

| Rule | Detail |
|---|---|
| Scope | All NICs in an ASG must be in the **same VNet** |
| Rule usage | If **both** source and destination are ASGs, they must be in the **same VNet** |
| ASGs per IP configuration | 20 |
| IP configurations per ASG | 4,000 |
| ASGs per NSG rule | 10 (source) + 10 (destination) |
| Can a NIC be in multiple ASGs? | ✅ Yes |
| Does an ASG replace an NSG? | ❌ **No** — an ASG is *used inside* NSG rules |

### Example: three-tier app

```bash
az network asg create -g rg -n asg-web
az network asg create -g rg -n asg-app
az network asg create -g rg -n asg-db

# Attach NICs
az network nic ip-config update -g rg --nic-name nic-web-01 -n ipconfig1 --application-security-groups asg-web
az network nic ip-config update -g rg --nic-name nic-app-01 -n ipconfig1 --application-security-groups asg-app
az network nic ip-config update -g rg --nic-name nic-db-01  -n ipconfig1 --application-security-groups asg-db

# Internet → web on 443
az network nsg rule create -g rg --nsg-name nsg-app -n Allow-Internet-Web \
  --priority 100 --direction Inbound --access Allow --protocol Tcp \
  --source-address-prefixes Internet \
  --destination-asgs asg-web --destination-port-ranges 443

# web → app on 8080
az network nsg rule create -g rg --nsg-name nsg-app -n Allow-Web-App \
  --priority 110 --direction Inbound --access Allow --protocol Tcp \
  --source-asgs asg-web --destination-asgs asg-app --destination-port-ranges 8080

# app → db on 1433
az network nsg rule create -g rg --nsg-name nsg-app -n Allow-App-DB \
  --priority 120 --direction Inbound --access Allow --protocol Tcp \
  --source-asgs asg-app --destination-asgs asg-db --destination-port-ranges 1433

# Deny web → db directly
az network nsg rule create -g rg --nsg-name nsg-app -n Deny-Web-DB \
  --priority 130 --direction Inbound --access Deny --protocol '*' \
  --source-asgs asg-web --destination-asgs asg-db --destination-port-ranges '*'
```

💡 **The value:** add a fourth web server → put its NIC in `asg-web` → **it automatically gets every web rule.** No IP list maintenance.

⚠️ **Trap:** ASGs work only **within a single VNet**. Cross-VNet rules need IP prefixes or service tags.

---

## 5.5 VNet Peering

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **VNet peering** | Connects two VNets so resources communicate over the **Microsoft backbone** using **private IPs** — as if in one network. |
| **Regional peering** | Both VNets in the **same region**. |
| **Global peering** | VNets in **different regions**. |
| **Non-transitive** | 📌 **THE key property.** A↔B and B↔C does **NOT** give A↔C. |
| **Bidirectional link** | 📌 Peering must be configured on **BOTH sides**. One-sided = `Initiated` state, not `Connected`. |
| **Allow virtual network access** | Permits communication (default: on). |
| **Allow forwarded traffic** | 📌 Accepts traffic **not originating** in the peer VNet (i.e., forwarded by an NVA/firewall). **Required for hub-spoke with an NVA.** |
| **Allow gateway transit** | 📌 Set on the **HUB** — lets spokes use the hub's VPN/ER gateway. |
| **Use remote gateways** | 📌 Set on the **SPOKE** — uses the hub's gateway. |
| **Peering state** | `Initiated` → (other side configured) → **`Connected`** → `Disconnected`. |
| **Hub-and-spoke** | The standard topology: a central hub with shared services (firewall, gateway, DNS) and peered spokes. |
| **Azure Virtual WAN** | Managed hub-and-spoke with automatic any-to-any transitivity. |

### 📌 Peering facts

| Fact | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Transitive?** | ❌ **NO** |
| Configure on both sides? | ✅ **YES** |
| Overlapping address spaces? | ❌ **Not allowed** |
| Cross-subscription? | ✅ Yes |
| Cross-tenant? | ✅ Yes (needs `Network Contributor` on both, via resource ID) |
| Cross-region (global)? | ✅ Yes |
| Latency | Same as intra-VNet (backbone) |
| Bandwidth | No gateway bottleneck; limited by VM size |
| Encryption | Traffic stays on the Microsoft backbone (private); can add MACsec |
| Cost | ⚠️ **Charged for ingress AND egress**, both directions. Global peering costs more. |
| Peerings per VNet | 500 |
| Can you peer to an on-prem network? | ❌ That's VPN/ExpressRoute |
| NSGs still apply? | ✅ Yes |
| Adding an address space after peering | ⚠️ Requires re-syncing the peering (`az network vnet peering sync`) |

### ⚠️ THE peering trap — non-transitivity

```
VNet-A  ←peer→  VNet-Hub  ←peer→  VNet-B

Can VNet-A talk to VNet-B?   ❌ NO.
```

**Three solutions:**

| Solution | How |
|---|---|
| **1. Direct peering A ↔ B** | Simplest for a few VNets; **N²** peerings at scale |
| **2. NVA / Azure Firewall in the hub** | Spokes route to the hub firewall via **UDR**; hub peerings need **"Allow forwarded traffic"** |
| **3. Azure Virtual WAN** | Managed hub — any-to-any transitivity built in |
| **4. Gateway transit** | Only for reaching **on-prem**, not spoke-to-spoke |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Enable gateway transit so spoke A can reach spoke B." → **Wrong.** Gateway transit only shares the **VPN/ER gateway** for on-prem connectivity. Spoke-to-spoke needs **UDR + firewall** or direct peering.

### 📌 Gateway transit — who sets what

```
HUB VNet   (has the VPN Gateway)
   Peering hub→spoke:  "Allow gateway transit" = ✅ ON

SPOKE VNet (no gateway)
   Peering spoke→hub:  "Use remote gateways" = ✅ ON
```

⚠️ **Rules:**
- The spoke **must NOT have its own gateway** to use `Use remote gateways`.
- Only **one** peering per spoke can use remote gateways.
- Both flags must be set on the **correct side** — a classic drag-and-drop question.

### 📌 Hub-and-spoke with a firewall

```
On-prem ──VPN──▶ [GatewaySubnet]
                       │
                  ┌────▼─────────────────┐
                  │   HUB VNet 10.0.0.0/16│
                  │  Azure Firewall       │  ← AzureFirewallSubnet 10.0.254.0/26
                  │  Bastion, DNS         │
                  └──┬──────────────┬─────┘
        peering ─────┘              └───── peering
     (AllowForwardedTraffic ✅)   (AllowForwardedTraffic ✅)
          │                              │
   ┌──────▼───────┐              ┌───────▼──────┐
   │ SPOKE-A      │              │  SPOKE-B     │
   │ 10.1.0.0/16  │              │  10.2.0.0/16 │
   │ UDR: 0.0.0.0/0 → FW         │  UDR: 0.0.0.0/0 → FW
   │     10.2.0.0/16 → FW        │     10.1.0.0/16 → FW
   └──────────────┘              └──────────────┘
```

📌 **Required for spoke-to-spoke via the hub firewall:**
1. **UDR** on each spoke subnet: next hop = **Virtual appliance** = the firewall's private IP.
2. **"Allow forwarded traffic"** = ON on **both hub↔spoke peerings**.
3. Firewall **network rules** permitting the traffic.
4. (Optional) IP forwarding on the NVA NIC if using a custom appliance.

### Commands

```bash
# Peer A → B
az network vnet peering create -g rg -n A-to-B --vnet-name vnet-A \
  --remote-vnet <vnetB-resourceId> \
  --allow-vnet-access --allow-forwarded-traffic --allow-gateway-transit

# Peer B → A  (MUST do both sides)
az network vnet peering create -g rg -n B-to-A --vnet-name vnet-B \
  --remote-vnet <vnetA-resourceId> \
  --allow-vnet-access --allow-forwarded-traffic --use-remote-gateways

az network vnet peering list -g rg --vnet-name vnet-A -o table
az network vnet peering show -g rg --vnet-name vnet-A -n A-to-B --query peeringState
az network vnet peering sync -g rg --vnet-name vnet-A -n A-to-B
```
