## 5.13 VPN Gateway and ExpressRoute

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Virtual network gateway** | The Azure resource providing VPN or ExpressRoute connectivity. Lives in **`GatewaySubnet`**. |
| **Gateway type: Vpn** | For site-to-site / point-to-site VPN. |
| **Gateway type: ExpressRoute** | For an ExpressRoute circuit. |
| **VPN type: Route-based** | 📌 **The default and recommended.** Uses IPsec tunnel interfaces + routing. Required for P2S, coexistence, multi-site, and active-active. |
| **VPN type: Policy-based** | Legacy. Static, matches traffic to a policy. **Basic SKU only**, **one tunnel**, **no P2S**, no BGP. |
| **Site-to-Site (S2S)** | 📌 Connects an **on-prem network** to a VNet over IPsec/IKE across the internet. Needs an on-prem **VPN device with a public IP**. |
| **Point-to-Site (P2S)** | 📌 Connects an **individual client device** to a VNet. No VPN device needed. |
| **VNet-to-VNet** | Connects two VNets via gateways (⚠️ **peering is usually better**). |
| **Local network gateway (LNG)** | 📌 The Azure resource **representing your on-prem site**: its public IP and its address prefixes. |
| **Connection** | The resource linking the VNet gateway to the LNG, with the **shared key (PSK)**. |
| **BGP** | Dynamic route exchange over the tunnel. Requires route-based VPN. |
| **ASN** | Autonomous System Number for BGP. Azure default: **65515**. |
| **Active-active** | Two gateway instances, two public IPs, two tunnels. **99.95%** SLA. |
| **Active-standby** | Default. One active instance; failover takes **10 s (planned)** to **~90 s (unplanned)**. |
| **Gateway SKU** | Basic, VpnGw1–5, VpnGw1AZ–5AZ (zone-redundant). |
| **ExpressRoute** | 📌 A **private, dedicated** connection to Microsoft via a connectivity provider. **Does NOT traverse the public internet.** |
| **ExpressRoute circuit** | The logical connection; has a **service key (s-key)**. |
| **Peering: Private** | Access to VNets (IaaS/PaaS via private endpoints). |
| **Peering: Microsoft** | Access to Microsoft 365, Dynamics, and Azure public services (public IPs). |
| **Peering: Public** | ⚠️ **Deprecated** — replaced by Microsoft peering. |
| **ExpressRoute Direct** | 10/100 Gbps direct port pair into Microsoft's network. |
| **ExpressRoute Global Reach** | 📌 Connect **on-prem site A to on-prem site B** through the Microsoft backbone. |
| **ExpressRoute FastPath** | Bypasses the ER gateway for data-plane traffic — lower latency. |
| **Bandwidth** | 50 Mbps – 10 Gbps (100 Gbps with ER Direct). |
| **Metered vs Unlimited** | Data plans; unlimited costs more per month. |
| **VPN as ER backup** | 📌 A S2S VPN can back up an ExpressRoute circuit (coexistence). |

### 📌 VPN Gateway SKUs

| SKU | S2S tunnels | P2S (SSTP/IKEv2/OpenVPN) | Aggregate throughput | BGP | **Zone-redundant** | Active-active |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Basic** | 10 | 128 / ❌ / ❌ | 100 Mbps | ❌ | ❌ | ❌ |
| **VpnGw1** | 30 | 128 / 250 / 250 | 650 Mbps | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ |
| **VpnGw2** | 30 | 128 / 500 / 500 | 1 Gbps | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ |
| **VpnGw3** | 30 | 128 / 1000 / 1000 | 1.25 Gbps | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ |
| **VpnGw4** | 100 | 128 / 5000 / 5000 | 5 Gbps | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ |
| **VpnGw5** | 100 | 128 / 10000 / 10000 | 10 Gbps | ✅ | ❌ | ✅ |
| **VpnGw1AZ–5AZ** | same | same | same | ✅ | ✅ **Yes** | ✅ |

### ⚠️ The Basic SKU traps

📌 **Basic VPN Gateway does NOT support:**
- **BGP**
- **Active-active**
- **IKEv2 / OpenVPN** for P2S (SSTP only)
- **Coexistence** with ExpressRoute
- **Zone redundancy**
- **RADIUS** authentication
- It's **deprecated** for new deployments.

⚠️ **Trap:** "Configure BGP on a Basic gateway." → **Not supported.** Resize to VpnGw1+.
⚠️ **Trap:** "Change the gateway SKU from Basic to VpnGw1." → 📌 You **cannot resize** to/from Basic. You must **delete and recreate** the gateway (new public IP, downtime). You *can* resize between VpnGw1↔VpnGw2↔VpnGw3 etc.

### 📌 Gateway deployment facts

| Fact | Detail |
|---|---|
| Subnet | 📌 **Must be named `GatewaySubnet`** exactly |
| Subnet size | `/29` minimum; 📌 **`/27` recommended**; `/26` for ExpressRoute coexistence |
| **Deployment time** | ⚠️ **~30–45 minutes** |
| Gateways per VNet | **1 VPN + 1 ExpressRoute** (coexistence) |
| Public IP | Required (Standard, static for AZ SKUs) |
| NSG on GatewaySubnet | ⚠️ **Not recommended / can break it** |
| UDR on GatewaySubnet | Very limited; use with care |
| SLA | **99.9%** (single) / **99.95%** (active-active or zone-redundant) |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Why is the deployment taking so long?" → **VPN gateways take up to 45 minutes.** Expected.

### 📌 Site-to-Site VPN — the build order

```
1. Create the VNet
2. Create the GatewaySubnet (/27 recommended)
3. Create the Public IP (Standard, Static)
4. Create the Virtual Network Gateway  ← ⏱ 30–45 minutes
5. Create the Local Network Gateway  (on-prem public IP + on-prem address prefixes)
6. Configure the ON-PREM VPN device (Azure gives you a config script)
7. Create the Connection (VNet GW ↔ LNG) with the shared key (PSK)
8. Verify — connection status should be "Connected"
```

📌 **This exact order is a common drag-and-drop question.**

⚠️ **Trap:** "The tunnel is up but on-prem can't reach Azure." → The **Local Network Gateway's address prefixes** are wrong/incomplete. The LNG tells Azure which prefixes live on-prem.
⚠️ **Trap:** "Address space overlap." → On-prem and the VNet must **not overlap**.

### 📌 Point-to-Site authentication

| Method | Protocols | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Azure certificate** | SSTP, IKEv2, OpenVPN | 📌 Upload the **root cert public key** to the gateway; issue **client certs** from it. |
| **Microsoft Entra ID** | 📌 **OpenVPN only** | Modern; supports Conditional Access/MFA |
| **RADIUS** | SSTP, IKEv2, OpenVPN | On-prem AD via NPS; ⚠️ **not supported on Basic** |

| Protocol | Client OS | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **SSTP** | 📌 **Windows only** | TCP 443 — traverses firewalls well. Max **128** connections. |
| **IKEv2** | Windows, macOS, Linux | UDP 500/4500 — may be blocked by firewalls |
| **OpenVPN** | 📌 **All platforms** (Win, Mac, Linux, iOS, Android) | TCP 443. 📌 **Required for Entra ID auth.** |

⚠️ **Trap:** "P2S with Entra ID authentication." → 📌 **Must use OpenVPN**. Certificate auth works with all three; Entra requires OpenVPN.
⚠️ **Trap:** "Mac users can't connect." → SSTP is **Windows-only**. Use IKEv2 or OpenVPN.
⚠️ **Trap:** The P2S **client address pool must NOT overlap** with the VNet or on-prem ranges.

### 📌 VPN vs ExpressRoute

| | **S2S VPN** | **ExpressRoute** |
|---|---|---|
| Path | 📌 **Public internet** (encrypted) | 📌 **Private circuit** — never touches the internet |
| Bandwidth | Up to **10 Gbps** (VpnGw5) | **50 Mbps – 100 Gbps** |
| Latency | Variable | 📌 **Low and predictable** |
| SLA | 99.9% / 99.95% | **99.95%** |
| Cost | Low | High |
| Setup time | Hours | ⚠️ **Weeks–months** (provider dependent) |
| Encryption | 📌 **IPsec (built-in)** | ⚠️ **None by default** — add MACsec (ER Direct) or IPsec over ER |
| Reaches M365 | ❌ | ✅ (Microsoft peering) |
| Reaches other on-prem sites | ❌ | ✅ **Global Reach** |
| Use for | SMB, dev/test, backup path | Enterprise, high-throughput, compliance |

⚠️ **THE ExpressRoute trap:** 📌 **ExpressRoute traffic is NOT encrypted by default.** It's *private*, not *encrypted*. If a question demands encryption over ER, the answer is **IPsec over ExpressRoute** or **MACsec (ER Direct)**.

### 📌 ExpressRoute peering types

| Peering | Reaches | Address space |
|---|---|---|
| **Private peering** | 📌 **Your VNets** (VMs, private endpoints) | Your private IPs |
| **Microsoft peering** | 📌 **Microsoft 365, Dynamics 365, Azure PaaS public endpoints** | **Public IPs** (must be owned/registered, needs route filters) |
| Public peering | ⚠️ **Deprecated** | — |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Reach Azure Storage's public endpoint over ExpressRoute." → **Microsoft peering** (or private peering + private endpoint).
⚠️ **Trap:** "Connect branch office A to branch office B via Microsoft's network." → 📌 **ExpressRoute Global Reach.**

### 📌 ExpressRoute resiliency

| Design | Notes |
|---|---|
| Single circuit | Two connections at the provider edge (built-in redundancy in the circuit) |
| **Two circuits in different peering locations** | 📌 **The recommended HA design** |
| **VPN as backup to ER** | Coexistence; requires **route-based VPN** and `GatewaySubnet` **/27 or larger** |
| **ExpressRoute Direct** | Dual 10/100 Gbps ports |

### Commands

```bash
# GatewaySubnet + PIP
az network vnet subnet create -g rg --vnet-name vnet1 -n GatewaySubnet --address-prefixes 10.0.255.0/27
az network public-ip create -g rg -n pip-vpngw --sku Standard --allocation-method Static --zone 1 2 3

# VPN gateway  (⏱ 30–45 min)
az network vnet-gateway create -g rg -n vpngw-hub \
  --vnet vnet1 --public-ip-addresses pip-vpngw \
  --gateway-type Vpn --vpn-type RouteBased --sku VpnGw2AZ \
  --asn 65515 --no-wait

# Local network gateway (on-prem)
az network local-gateway create -g rg -n lng-hq \
  --gateway-ip-address 198.51.100.10 \
  --local-address-prefixes 192.168.0.0/16 10.100.0.0/16 \
  --asn 65050 --bgp-peering-address 192.168.0.1

# Connection
az network vpn-connection create -g rg -n conn-hq \
  --vnet-gateway1 vpngw-hub --local-gateway2 lng-hq \
  --shared-key 'SuperSecretPSK123!' --enable-bgp

az network vpn-connection show -g rg -n conn-hq --query connectionStatus
az network vpn-connection list -g rg -o table

# P2S
az network vnet-gateway update -g rg -n vpngw-hub \
  --address-prefixes 172.16.201.0/24 \
  --client-protocol OpenVPN IkeV2 \
  --root-cert-name rootcert --root-cert-data ./rootcert.cer

# P2S with Entra ID (OpenVPN required)
az network vnet-gateway update -g rg -n vpngw-hub \
  --client-protocol OpenVPN \
  --aad-tenant "https://login.microsoftonline.com/<tenantId>" \
  --aad-audience "<appId>" --aad-issuer "https://sts.windows.net/<tenantId>/"

# Resize (NOT possible to/from Basic)
az network vnet-gateway update -g rg -n vpngw-hub --sku VpnGw3AZ

# ExpressRoute
az network express-route create -g rg -n er-circuit \
  --bandwidth 1000 --peering-location "Washington DC" \
  --provider "Equinix" --sku-family MeteredData --sku-tier Standard

az network express-route show -g rg -n er-circuit --query serviceKey
az network express-route peering create -g rg --circuit-name er-circuit \
  --peering-type AzurePrivatePeering --peer-asn 65050 --vlan-id 100 \
  --primary-peer-subnet 192.168.1.0/30 --secondary-peer-subnet 192.168.2.0/30

az network vpn-connection list -g rg -o table
az network watcher troubleshooting start -g rg --resource vpngw-hub \
  --resource-type vnetGateway --storage-account stacct --storage-path https://stacct.blob.core.windows.net/logs
```

---

## 5.14 Azure Bastion, NAT Gateway, Firewall

### Azure Bastion

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Azure Bastion** | 📌 **Managed jump host** providing **RDP/SSH over TLS in the browser** (or via a native client), **without any public IP on the VM**. |
| **`AzureBastionSubnet`** | 📌 The exact required subnet name. Minimum **`/26`**. |
| **Bastion SKUs** | **Developer** (free, limited, VNet-only), **Basic**, **Standard**, **Premium** |
| **Native client support** | Standard+ — `az network bastion ssh/rdp` from your local terminal |
| **IP-based connection** | Standard+ — connect to any reachable IP, including on-prem via VPN/ER |
| **Shareable link** | Standard+ — a URL for users without portal access |
| **Session recording** | Premium |
| **Private-only Bastion** | Premium — no public IP on the Bastion host |
| **Host scaling** | Standard+ — 2–50 instances |

### 📌 Bastion facts

| Fact | Detail |
|---|---|
| Subnet name | 📌 **`AzureBastionSubnet`** — exact |
| Subnet size | 📌 **`/26`** minimum (Standard+); `/26` recommended everywhere |
| Public IP | **Standard, static** required (except Developer/Premium private mode) |
| **VM needs a public IP?** | 📌 **NO — that's the entire point** |
| Ports opened on the VM | Bastion connects on **3389/22** from the `AzureBastionSubnet` — allow the **`VirtualNetwork`** source |
| Browser requirement | HTML5 browser; connects over **TCP 443** |
| Works across peered VNets? | ✅ Yes (Basic+, with peering) |
| Cost | Hourly + outbound data |
| Deployment time | ~10 minutes |
| UDR on AzureBastionSubnet | ⚠️ Not supported |
| NSG on AzureBastionSubnet | Supported but with **strict required rules** |

⚠️ **THE Bastion trap:** "Provide RDP access to VMs without exposing them to the internet." → 📌 **Azure Bastion.** Not "an NSG rule allowing your IP," not "a jump box VM with a public IP."
⚠️ **Trap:** Subnet **must** be `/26` and named `AzureBastionSubnet` exactly.

```bash
az network vnet subnet create -g rg --vnet-name vnet1 -n AzureBastionSubnet --address-prefixes 10.0.253.0/26
az network public-ip create -g rg -n pip-bastion --sku Standard --allocation-method Static
az network bastion create -g rg -n bastion-hub --public-ip-address pip-bastion \
  --vnet-name vnet1 --location eastus --sku Standard --enable-tunneling true --enable-ip-connect true

# Native client
az network bastion ssh -g rg -n bastion-hub --target-resource-id <vmId> \
  --auth-type ssh-key --username azureuser --ssh-key ~/.ssh/id_rsa
az network bastion rdp -g rg -n bastion-hub --target-resource-id <vmId>
```

### NAT Gateway

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **NAT Gateway** | 📌 A **fully managed, highly resilient outbound-only** SNAT service for a subnet. |
| Attached to | **Subnets** (up to 16 subnets in one VNet) |
| Uses | **Standard public IPs** (up to 16) and/or **public IP prefixes** |
| SNAT ports | 📌 **64,512 per public IP** — dynamically allocated on demand |
| Direction | 📌 **Outbound only** — it can **never** be used for inbound |
| Idle timeout | 4 minutes (configurable to 120) |
| Zones | Zonal (can be pinned to a zone) |
| Priority | 📌 **Overrides Load Balancer outbound rules and default outbound access** for that subnet |
| SLA | 99.99% |

📌 **NAT Gateway is THE answer for:**
- **SNAT port exhaustion**
- "All outbound traffic must come from a **predictable set of IPs**" (for a partner's allow-list)
- "Give VMs internet access **without** public IPs"
- Replacing default outbound access before its Sept 2025 retirement

⚠️ **Trap:** "Use NAT Gateway for inbound access." → **Impossible.** Outbound only.
⚠️ **Trap:** NAT Gateway **cannot** be used on `GatewaySubnet` or `AzureFirewallSubnet`.
⚠️ **Trap:** A VM with its **own public IP** bypasses the NAT Gateway (ILPIP has higher priority).

```bash
az network public-ip create -g rg -n pip-nat --sku Standard --allocation-method Static
az network public-ip prefix create -g rg -n natprefix --length 28

az network nat gateway create -g rg -n natgw --public-ip-addresses pip-nat \
  --public-ip-prefixes natprefix --idle-timeout 10 --zone 1

az network vnet subnet update -g rg --vnet-name vnet1 -n web --nat-gateway natgw
```

### Azure Firewall

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Azure Firewall** | 📌 A managed, **stateful**, cloud-native network firewall with **high availability and unrestricted scalability**. |
| **`AzureFirewallSubnet`** | 📌 Exact name, minimum **`/26`**. |
| **NAT rules** | 📌 **DNAT** — translate inbound public IP:port → private IP:port. **Highest priority.** |
| **Network rules** | 📌 **L3/L4** filtering: source/dest IP, port, protocol, **service tags**, **FQDN tags**. |
| **Application rules** | 📌 **L7 FQDN** filtering for HTTP/S/MSSQL: allow `*.microsoft.com`, block the rest. |
| **Rule collection** | A group of rules with a priority and an action. |
| **Rule collection group** | (Firewall Policy) A group of rule collections. |
| **Firewall Policy** | 📌 The modern, **reusable, hierarchical** configuration resource. Parent/child inheritance. |
| **Classic rules** | Legacy per-firewall rules. Migrate to Policy. |
| **Threat intelligence** | Alert or **Alert and deny** on known-malicious IPs/domains (Microsoft feed). |
| **IDPS** | Intrusion Detection and Prevention — **Premium only**. |
| **TLS inspection** | Decrypt and inspect outbound TLS — **Premium only**. |
| **URL filtering** | Filter on the **full path**, not just the FQDN — **Premium only**. |
| **Web categories** | Block by category (gambling, social media) — Standard (basic) / **Premium (full + custom)**. |
| **FQDN tag** | A Microsoft-managed group of FQDNs: `WindowsUpdate`, `AzureBackup`, `MicrosoftActiveProtectionService`, `AppServiceEnvironment`, `AzureKubernetesService`. |
| **Forced tunneling** | Send the firewall's own internet traffic on-prem. Requires `AzureFirewallManagementSubnet`. |
| **DNS proxy** | The firewall acts as a DNS proxy for the VNet — 📌 **required for FQDN filtering in network rules**. |
| **SNAT** | The firewall SNATs outbound traffic to its public IP(s). |

### 📌 Azure Firewall rule processing order — memorize

```
1. NAT rules          (DNAT — inbound)        ← evaluated FIRST
2. Network rules      (L3/L4)
3. Application rules  (L7 FQDN)               ← evaluated LAST
```

📌 **Within each type: lower priority number = evaluated first.**
📌 **If a NETWORK rule matches, APPLICATION rules are NEVER evaluated.**
📌 **Threat Intelligence (if set to "Alert and deny") is evaluated BEFORE all rules.**

⚠️ **THE Azure Firewall trap:** "An application rule allows `www.contoso.com` but a network rule allows `443` to `Any`. The traffic isn't being filtered by the app rule." → 📌 **Correct** — the **network rule matched first**, so the application rule was never evaluated. Remove the broad network rule.

### 📌 Azure Firewall SKUs

| | **Basic** | **Standard** | **Premium** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Throughput | 250 Mbps | 30 Gbps | 100 Gbps |
| Network + application rules | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| Threat intelligence | Alert only | Alert + deny | Alert + deny |
| **IDPS** | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| **TLS inspection** | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| **URL filtering (full path)** | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ |
| **Web categories** | ❌ | ✅ Basic | ✅ Full + custom |
| Availability Zones | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| For | SMB (<250 Mbps) | Most | Highly sensitive/regulated |

### 📌 Azure Firewall vs NSG vs WAF

| | **NSG** | **Azure Firewall** | **WAF (App GW / Front Door)** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Layer | **L3/L4** | **L3–L7** | **L7 (HTTP only)** |
| Scope | Subnet / NIC | VNet (usually a hub) | HTTP apps |
| Cost | **Free** | Expensive | Moderate |
| FQDN filtering | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ (host header) |
| Stateful | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| Logging | Flow logs | Full, rich | Full |
| Threat intel | ❌ | ✅ | Managed rules |
| DNAT | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ (as an LB) |
| Protects against SQLi/XSS | ❌ | ⚠️ IDPS (Premium) | ✅ **Yes — its purpose** |
| Use for | Basic micro-segmentation | 📌 **Centralized egress control** | 📌 **Web app protection** |

📌 **They are complementary, not alternatives.** A mature design uses **all three**: NSGs for micro-segmentation, Firewall for centralized egress + hub inspection, WAF for the web tier.

### Commands

```bash
az network vnet subnet create -g rg --vnet-name vnet-hub -n AzureFirewallSubnet --address-prefixes 10.0.254.0/26
az network public-ip create -g rg -n pip-fw --sku Standard --allocation-method Static --zone 1 2 3

az network firewall policy create -g rg -n fwpolicy --sku Standard \
  --threat-intel-mode Deny --enable-dns-proxy true

az network firewall create -g rg -n fw-hub --vnet-name vnet-hub \
  --firewall-policy fwpolicy --zone 1 2 3 --sku AZFW_VNet --tier Standard

az network firewall ip-config create -g rg -f fw-hub -n fw-config \
  --public-ip-address pip-fw --vnet-name vnet-hub

# Get the firewall's private IP for your UDRs
az network firewall show -g rg -n fw-hub --query "ipConfigurations[0].privateIPAddress" -o tsv

# Rule collection group
az network firewall policy rule-collection-group create -g rg \
  --policy-name fwpolicy -n DefaultRuleGroup --priority 200

# Network rule (L3/L4)
az network firewall policy rule-collection-group collection add-filter-collection -g rg \
  --policy-name fwpolicy --rule-collection-group-name DefaultRuleGroup \
  --name net-allow --collection-priority 100 --action Allow \
  --rule-name allow-dns --rule-type NetworkRule \
  --source-addresses 10.0.0.0/8 --destination-addresses "*" \
  --destination-ports 53 --ip-protocols UDP TCP

# Application rule (L7 FQDN)
az network firewall policy rule-collection-group collection add-filter-collection -g rg \
  --policy-name fwpolicy --rule-collection-group-name DefaultRuleGroup \
  --name app-allow --collection-priority 200 --action Allow \
  --rule-name allow-ms --rule-type ApplicationRule \
  --source-addresses 10.0.0.0/8 --protocols Http=80 Https=443 \
  --target-fqdns "*.microsoft.com" "*.windowsupdate.com"

# FQDN tag rule
az network firewall policy rule-collection-group collection add-filter-collection -g rg \
  --policy-name fwpolicy --rule-collection-group-name DefaultRuleGroup \
  --name app-wu --collection-priority 210 --action Allow \
  --rule-name allow-windowsupdate --rule-type ApplicationRule \
  --source-addresses 10.0.0.0/8 --protocols Https=443 --fqdn-tags WindowsUpdate

# DNAT rule (inbound)
az network firewall policy rule-collection-group collection add-nat-collection -g rg \
  --policy-name fwpolicy --rule-collection-group-name DefaultRuleGroup \
  --name dnat-rdp --collection-priority 100 --action DNAT \
  --rule-name rdp-to-vm1 --source-addresses 203.0.113.5 \
  --destination-addresses <fwPublicIp> --destination-ports 3389 \
  --translated-address 10.0.1.4 --translated-port 3389 --ip-protocols TCP

# UDR pointing spokes at the firewall
az network route-table route create -g rg --route-table-name rt-spoke \
  -n to-firewall --address-prefix 0.0.0.0/0 \
  --next-hop-type VirtualAppliance --next-hop-ip-address 10.0.254.4
```

---
---

# Part 6 — Domain 5: Monitor and Maintain Azure Resources (10–15%)

> The smallest domain and the most neglected. KQL, alert anatomy, and Backup vault rules are cheap marks.

## 6.1 Azure Monitor Architecture

**Azure Monitor is the umbrella service collecting, analyzing, and acting on telemetry.**

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Azure Monitor** | The full-stack observability platform: metrics, logs, alerts, insights, visualizations, autoscale. |
| **Metrics** | 📌 **Numeric, time-series** data. Lightweight, near real-time (~1 min), pre-aggregated. Stored in the **Metrics store**. |
| **Logs** | 📌 **Text/structured records** with a timestamp. Rich, queryable with KQL. Stored in a **Log Analytics workspace**. |
| **Log Analytics workspace (LAW)** | The container for log data. The KQL query target. |
| **Data source** | Where telemetry originates: platform, guest OS, application, Azure activity. |
| **Activity log** | 📌 **Subscription-level** log of **control-plane** operations: who did what, when, and the result. |
| **Resource log** (formerly diagnostic log) | 📌 **Data-plane** operations *inside* a resource. **Not collected by default** — you must add a **diagnostic setting**. |
| **Platform metrics** | Emitted by Azure resources automatically. **No agent required.** |
| **Guest OS metrics** | Inside the VM (memory, disk queue). 📌 **Requires the Azure Monitor Agent.** |
| **Application telemetry** | Requests, dependencies, exceptions — via **Application Insights**. |
| **Diagnostic setting** | 📌 The configuration that **routes** resource logs and metrics to a destination. |
| **Data Collection Rule (DCR)** | 📌 Defines **what** AMA collects and **where** it sends it. The modern replacement for legacy agent config. |
| **Data Collection Endpoint (DCE)** | The ingestion endpoint for AMA (needed for private link / some scenarios). |
| **Insights** | Curated experiences: **VM Insights**, **Container Insights**, **Network Insights**, **Storage Insights**, **Application Insights**. |
| **Workbook** | Interactive, shareable report combining text, queries, metrics, and parameters. |
| **Azure Monitor Agent (AMA)** | 📌 The current agent. Replaces MMA/OMS (**retired 31 Aug 2024**) and the Telegraf/Diagnostics extension. |
| **Azure Service Health** | Azure-side outages, planned maintenance, and health advisories affecting **your** resources. |
| **Resource Health** | The health of an **individual resource** (Available / Unavailable / Degraded / Unknown). |
| **Azure Status** | 📌 The **public** status page (`status.azure.com`) — global, not personalized. |

### 📌 Metrics vs Logs — the fundamental split

| | **Metrics** | **Logs** |
|---|---|---|
| Data type | **Numeric** time-series | **Text / structured records** |
| Structure | Fixed (timestamp, value, dimensions) | Flexible schema per table |
| Latency | 📌 **~1–3 minutes** (near real-time) | 📌 **2–10+ minutes** |
| Retention | 📌 **93 days** (platform metrics) | 📌 **30 days free**, up to **2 years** (interactive), then archive up to **12 years** |
| Query language | Metrics Explorer (no KQL for the metrics store itself) | 📌 **KQL** |
| Cost | 📌 **Free** (platform metrics) | 📌 **Charged per GB ingested + retained** |
| Alert speed | Fast (near real-time) | Slower (query-based) |
| Agent needed? | ❌ for platform metrics; ✅ for **guest OS** metrics | ✅ for guest logs; ❌ for activity log |
| Use for | Autoscale, fast alerts, dashboards | Investigation, correlation, audit, complex conditions |

📌 **The exam's favorite consequence:** **Autoscale on CPU needs no agent** (platform metric). **Autoscale on memory needs AMA** (guest metric).

⚠️ **Trap:** "Platform metrics are retained for 30 days." → **FALSE. 93 days.**
⚠️ **Trap:** "Log Analytics free retention is 90 days." → **FALSE. 31 days** (30/31 depending on how you count; the exam says **30 days** free, and **90 days free** for Application Insights / Sentinel-enabled workspaces).

### 📌 Activity Log vs Resource Log vs Guest Log

| | **Activity Log** | **Resource Log** | **Guest OS Log** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Level | **Subscription** | **Resource (data plane)** | **Inside the VM** |
| Example | "Alice deleted vm-web-01" | "A blob was read from container X" | Windows Event Log, syslog |
| Collected by default? | ✅ **Yes** (90 days, free) | ❌ **NO — needs a diagnostic setting** | ❌ **NO — needs AMA + a DCR** |
| Agent required? | ❌ | ❌ | ✅ **AMA** |
| Table in LAW | `AzureActivity` | Varies (`StorageBlobLogs`, `AzureDiagnostics`, etc.) | `Event`, `Syslog` |
| Retention default | **90 days** (in the Activity Log store) | Only what your diagnostic setting sends | Workspace retention |

⚠️ **THE monitoring trap:** 📌 **Resource logs are NOT collected by default.** You must create a **diagnostic setting** on each resource (or use an Azure Policy with a `DeployIfNotExists` effect to do it at scale).

⚠️ **Trap:** "The Activity Log is retained for 90 days for free — how do I keep it for 2 years?" → 📌 **Create a diagnostic setting on the Activity Log** exporting it to a **Log Analytics workspace** (or a storage account for cheap archival).

### 📌 Activity Log categories

| Category | Contains |
|---|---|
| **Administrative** | 📌 All create/update/delete via ARM. **"Who deleted the VM?"** |
| **Service Health** | Azure incidents affecting your resources |
| **Resource Health** | Your resource's health transitions |
| **Alert** | Alert activations |
| **Autoscale** | Autoscale engine actions |
| **Recommendation** | Advisor recommendations |
| **Security** | Defender for Cloud alerts |
| **Policy** | Policy effects (audit/deny actions) |

📌 **"Who deleted the storage account?"** → **Activity Log → Administrative category.**

### 📌 Service Health vs Resource Health vs Azure Status

| | **Azure Status** | **Service Health** | **Resource Health** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Scope | 📌 **Global, public** | 📌 **Your subscriptions/regions/services** | 📌 **One specific resource** |
| Personalized | ❌ | ✅ | ✅ |
| Shows planned maintenance | Limited | ✅ | ✅ |
| Shows health advisories | ❌ | ✅ | ❌ |
| Alertable | ❌ | ✅ **Yes** | ✅ **Yes** |
| URL / location | `status.azure.com` | Portal → Service Health | Resource → Resource Health |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Get notified about planned maintenance affecting your VMs." → 📌 **Service Health alert**, not Resource Health, not Azure Status.
⚠️ **Trap:** "Is my specific VM up?" → **Resource Health**.

---

## 6.2 Metrics

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Metric namespace** | A grouping of metrics, e.g., `Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachines`. |
| **Metric** | A measured value, e.g., `Percentage CPU`. |
| **Dimension** | A name/value attribute that splits a metric, e.g., disk metrics by `LUN`, storage by `ApiName`. |
| **Aggregation** | How raw samples are combined: **Avg, Min, Max, Sum, Count**. |
| **Granularity / time grain** | The bucket size: 1 min, 5 min, 1 h, etc. |
| **Metrics Explorer** | The portal chart builder. |
| **Multi-dimensional metrics** | Metrics with dimensions you can filter/split by. |
| **Custom metrics** | Metrics you emit (via AMA, App Insights SDK, or the REST API). |
| **Metric alert** | Near-real-time alert on a metric condition. |
| **Dynamic thresholds** | 📌 ML-based thresholds that learn the metric's normal pattern. No fixed number needed. |

### 📌 Key VM metrics — and which need an agent

| Metric | Platform (no agent) | Needs AMA |
|---|---|---|
| **Percentage CPU** | ✅ | |
| **Network In/Out Total** | ✅ | |
| **Disk Read/Write Bytes & Operations/Sec** | ✅ | |
| **CPU Credits Remaining/Consumed** (B-series) | ✅ | |
| **OS Disk / Data Disk IOPS Consumed Percentage** | ✅ | |
| **VM Availability Metric** | ✅ | |
| 📌 **Available Memory Bytes / % Committed Bytes In Use** | ❌ | ✅ **AMA** |
| 📌 **Logical Disk Free Space / Free Megabytes** | ❌ | ✅ **AMA** |
| 📌 **Disk Queue Length** (guest view) | ❌ | ✅ **AMA** |
| Process-level metrics | ❌ | ✅ **AMA** |

⚠️ **THE metrics trap:** 📌 **Memory and disk *free space* are NOT platform metrics.** Azure cannot see inside the guest OS. You need the **Azure Monitor Agent** with a **Data Collection Rule** collecting performance counters.

### 📌 Storage account metrics

| Metric | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **UsedCapacity** | Bytes used (⚠️ **daily granularity only** for account-level) |
| **Transactions** | Request count; split by `ResponseType`, `ApiName`, `Authentication` |
| **Ingress / Egress** | Bytes in/out |
| **SuccessE2ELatency** | End-to-end latency including network |
| **SuccessServerLatency** | Azure-side processing latency only |
| **Availability** | % of successful requests |

📌 **`SuccessE2ELatency` >> `SuccessServerLatency`** → the bottleneck is the **network/client**, not Azure Storage.

### Commands

```bash
az monitor metrics list --resource <resourceId> \
  --metric "Percentage CPU" --interval PT1M \
  --start-time 2026-07-15T00:00:00Z --end-time 2026-07-15T12:00:00Z \
  --aggregation Average Maximum

az monitor metrics list-definitions --resource <resourceId> -o table
az monitor metrics list-namespaces --resource <resourceId>
```

```powershell
Get-AzMetric -ResourceId <id> -MetricName "Percentage CPU" -TimeGrain 00:01:00 `
  -StartTime (Get-Date).AddHours(-6) -AggregationType Average
Get-AzMetricDefinition -ResourceId <id>
```

---

## 6.3 Logs and Log Analytics

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Log Analytics workspace (LAW)** | The container for log data. Has a region, retention, pricing tier, and access mode. |
| **Table** | A schema'd collection of records: `Heartbeat`, `Perf`, `Event`, `Syslog`, `AzureActivity`, `AzureDiagnostics`, `SecurityEvent`, `ContainerLog`. |
| **Ingestion** | Getting data in — via AMA, diagnostic settings, the Logs Ingestion API, or connectors. |
| **Retention** | 📌 **30 days free** (90 for App Insights/Sentinel), configurable **30–730 days** (interactive). |
| **Archive / long-term retention** | Cheap storage beyond interactive retention, up to **12 years (4,383 days)**. Requires a **search job** or **restore** to query. |
| **Search job** | Asynchronous query over archived data → results into a new table. |
| **Restore** | Bring archived data back into interactive tier temporarily. |
| **Basic logs / Auxiliary logs** | 📌 Cheaper ingestion tier with **limited KQL**, **8-day** interactive retention, no alerts. For high-volume, low-value logs. |
| **Analytics logs** | The standard tier — full KQL, alerts, 30–730 days. |
| **Table plan** | Per-table choice of Analytics / Basic / Auxiliary. |
| **Commitment tier** | 📌 Prepay for 100 GB–5,000 GB/day for a **discount** (up to ~30%) vs Pay-As-You-Go. |
| **Daily cap** | 📌 Stop ingestion after N GB/day to control cost. ⚠️ **Data after the cap is LOST**, and alerts may stop firing. |
| **Access mode: Workspace-context** | Users see **all** data in the workspace. |
| **Access mode: Resource-context** | 📌 Users see only data for **resources they have RBAC access to**. **Recommended.** |
| **Workspace transformation DCR** | Filter/enrich data at ingestion time to cut cost. |
| **Cross-workspace query** | `workspace("other-ws").Perf` |
| **Data export** | Continuous export of tables to a storage account or Event Hub. |

### 📌 Workspace design decisions

| Question | Guidance |
|---|---|
| **How many workspaces?** | 📌 **As few as possible.** One central workspace unless you need data sovereignty, separate retention/cost ownership, or regional data residency. |
| Region | 📌 **Ingestion is charged for cross-region data transfer.** Keep the workspace near the resources. |
| Retention | Set per-workspace, **override per-table**. |
| Access | Resource-context for most orgs. |
| Cost | Commitment tiers if >100 GB/day. |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Multiple workspaces make querying harder" — a cross-workspace query is possible but adds friction and cost. The exam favors **centralization**.
⚠️ **Trap:** "A daily cap stops the bill from growing." → It does, **but you lose the data and alerts may silently stop firing.** Use with care.

### 📌 Retention pricing

| | Free retention | Configurable to | Archive to |
|---|---|---|---|
| Standard workspace | **30 days** | **730 days** (2 years) | **4,383 days** (12 years) |
| Sentinel-enabled | **90 days** | 730 days | 12 years |
| Application Insights (workspace-based) | **90 days** | 730 days | 12 years |

### Commands

```bash
az monitor log-analytics workspace create -g rg -n law-central \
  -l eastus --retention-time 90 --sku PerGB2018

az monitor log-analytics workspace update -g rg -n law-central --retention-time 180

# Daily cap
az monitor log-analytics workspace update -g rg -n law-central \
  --set workspaceCapping.dailyQuotaGb=50

# Per-table retention + archive
az monitor log-analytics workspace table update -g rg --workspace-name law-central \
  -n Perf --retention-time 30 --total-retention-time 730

# Basic logs tier
az monitor log-analytics workspace table update -g rg --workspace-name law-central \
  -n ContainerLogV2 --plan Basic

# Run a query
az monitor log-analytics query -w <workspaceId> \
  --analytics-query "Heartbeat | summarize count() by Computer" -o table

az monitor log-analytics workspace get-shared-keys -g rg -n law-central
az monitor log-analytics workspace list-usages -g rg -n law-central
```

```powershell
New-AzOperationalInsightsWorkspace -ResourceGroupName rg -Name law-central `
  -Location eastus -Sku PerGB2018 -RetentionInDays 90

Invoke-AzOperationalInsightsQuery -WorkspaceId <id> -Query "Heartbeat | take 10"
```

---

## 6.4 KQL — Kusto Query Language

**KQL appears on the exam. You need to read it, not write masterpieces.**

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **KQL** | Read-only query language for Log Analytics, Application Insights, Resource Graph, Sentinel, and Data Explorer. |
| **Pipe (`\|`)** | Chains operators left to right. Each stage feeds the next. |
| **Tabular operator** | Takes a table in, returns a table out. |
| **Scalar function** | Returns a single value. |
| **Time range** | Set in the UI, or with `where TimeGenerated > ago(1h)`. |

### 📌 The operators you must recognize

| Operator | Does |
|---|---|
| **`where`** | 📌 **Filter rows.** Put this **first** for performance. |
| **`project`** | 📌 **Select columns** (like SQL `SELECT`). |
| `project-away` | Remove columns |
| `project-rename` | Rename columns |
| **`extend`** | 📌 **Add a calculated column.** |
| **`summarize`** | 📌 **Aggregate** (like SQL `GROUP BY`). |
| **`by`** | The grouping clause inside `summarize`. |
| **`take` / `limit`** | Return N arbitrary rows (fast sampling) |
| **`top N by X`** | 📌 Top N rows **sorted** by X |
| **`sort by` / `order by`** | Sort (`desc` default) |
| **`count`** | Count rows |
| **`distinct`** | Unique values |
| **`join`** | Combine two tables (`kind=inner/leftouter/fullouter`) |
| **`union`** | Stack tables |
| **`render`** | 📌 Draw a chart: `timechart`, `barchart`, `piechart`, `columnchart` |
| **`let`** | Define a variable / reusable subquery |
| **`parse`** | Extract fields from a string |
| **`mv-expand`** | Expand an array into rows |
| **`make-series`** | Time-series generation |
| **`bin()`** | 📌 **Round a timestamp into buckets**: `bin(TimeGenerated, 1h)` |
| **`ago()`** | 📌 Relative time: `ago(24h)`, `ago(7d)`, `ago(30m)` |
| **`now()`** | Current UTC time |
| **`datetime()` / `timespan()`** | Literals |
| **`startofday()` / `endofday()`** | Day boundaries |
| `arg_max()` / `arg_min()` | The row with the max/min of an expression |
| `countif()` / `sumif()` | Conditional aggregates |
| `percentile()` | e.g., `percentile(Duration, 95)` |
| `case()` / `iff()` | Conditionals |
| `has` / `contains` / `startswith` / `endswith` / `matches regex` | String matching (📌 `has` is **faster** than `contains` — it's token-aware) |
| `in` / `!in` | Set membership |
| `isnotempty()` / `isnull()` | Null checks |
| `todynamic()` / `tostring()` / `toint()` | Type conversion |

### 📌 Query structure — always this order

```kql
TableName                          // 1. Pick the table
| where TimeGenerated > ago(24h)   // 2. Filter EARLY (time first — it's indexed)
| where Computer == "vm-web-01"    // 3. More filters
| extend Hour = bin(TimeGenerated, 1h)   // 4. Calculate
| summarize avg(CounterValue) by Hour, Computer   // 5. Aggregate
| order by Hour asc                // 6. Sort
| render timechart                 // 7. Visualize
```

📌 **Filter first, aggregate later.** Putting `where TimeGenerated` first is the #1 performance rule.

### 📌 The exam-relevant queries

**VM heartbeat / which machines are reporting**
```kql
Heartbeat
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1h)
| summarize LastSeen = max(TimeGenerated) by Computer
| order by LastSeen desc
```

**Find VMs that STOPPED reporting (a classic alert query)**
```kql
Heartbeat
| where TimeGenerated > ago(24h)
| summarize LastHeartbeat = max(TimeGenerated) by Computer
| where LastHeartbeat < ago(15m)
```

**Average CPU per VM over time**
```kql
Perf
| where TimeGenerated > ago(24h)
| where ObjectName == "Processor" and CounterName == "% Processor Time"
| summarize AvgCPU = avg(CounterValue) by Computer, bin(TimeGenerated, 15m)
| render timechart
```

**Available memory**
```kql
Perf
| where ObjectName == "Memory" and CounterName == "Available MBytes"
| summarize AvgMemMB = avg(CounterValue) by Computer, bin(TimeGenerated, 5m)
| render timechart
```

**Free disk space below 10%**
```kql
Perf
| where ObjectName == "LogicalDisk" and CounterName == "% Free Space"
| where InstanceName != "_Total"
| summarize FreePct = avg(CounterValue) by Computer, InstanceName
| where FreePct < 10
```

**📌 Who deleted a resource? (the classic Activity Log query)**
```kql
AzureActivity
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where OperationNameValue contains "delete"
| where ActivityStatusValue == "Success"
| project TimeGenerated, Caller, OperationNameValue, ResourceGroup, _ResourceId
| order by TimeGenerated desc
```

**Failed sign-ins (Entra ID)**
```kql
SigninLogs
| where TimeGenerated > ago(24h)
| where ResultType != 0
| summarize FailCount = count() by UserPrincipalName, ResultDescription, IPAddress
| where FailCount > 5
| order by FailCount desc
```

**📌 Ingestion volume by table — the cost-control query**
```kql
Usage
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where IsBillable == true
| summarize TotalGB = sum(Quantity) / 1000 by DataType
| order by TotalGB desc
| render barchart
```

**Windows security events — account lockouts**
```kql
SecurityEvent
| where EventID == 4740
| project TimeGenerated, Account, Computer, TargetAccount
```

**Linux syslog errors**
```kql
Syslog
| where SeverityLevel in ("err", "crit", "alert", "emerg")
| summarize count() by Computer, Facility, SeverityLevel
```

**Storage: slow requests**
```kql
StorageBlobLogs
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1h)
| where DurationMs > 1000
| project TimeGenerated, OperationName, Uri, DurationMs, StatusText
| order by DurationMs desc
```

**NSG flow logs — denied traffic (Traffic Analytics)**
```kql
AzureNetworkAnalytics_CL
| where FlowStatus_s == "D"
| summarize count() by SrcIP_s, DestIP_s, DestPort_d, NSGRule_s
| order by count_ desc
```

**Using `let` and a join**
```kql
let timeRange = 24h;
let highCPU =
    Perf
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(timeRange)
    | where CounterName == "% Processor Time"
    | summarize AvgCPU = avg(CounterValue) by Computer
    | where AvgCPU > 80;
Heartbeat
| where TimeGenerated > ago(timeRange)
| distinct Computer, ResourceGroup
| join kind=inner highCPU on Computer
| project Computer, ResourceGroup, AvgCPU
```

**Application Insights — slowest requests**
```kql
requests
| where timestamp > ago(1h)
| summarize P95 = percentile(duration, 95), Count = count() by name
| order by P95 desc
| take 10
```

**Application Insights — failure rate**
```kql
requests
| where timestamp > ago(24h)
| summarize Total = count(), Failed = countif(success == false) by bin(timestamp, 1h)
| extend FailureRate = round(100.0 * Failed / Total, 2)
| render timechart
```

### 📌 KQL exam gotchas

| Gotcha | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Case sensitivity** | 📌 **Table and column names ARE case-sensitive.** `Heartbeat` ≠ `heartbeat`. String **comparisons** with `==` are case-sensitive too; use `=~` for case-insensitive. |
| `take` vs `top` | 📌 `take` = arbitrary N rows (fast, unordered). `top N by X` = the actual top N. |
| `contains` vs `has` | 📌 `has` is **much faster** (token-based). `contains` is a substring scan. |
| `summarize` drops columns | Only the `by` columns and the aggregates survive. Use `extend` before, or `arg_max()`. |
| `project` vs `extend` | `project` **replaces** the column set; `extend` **adds** to it. |
| `TimeGenerated` | Always UTC. |
| Comments | `//` |
| Pipe order | Filter → extend → summarize → sort → render |

---

## 6.5 Diagnostic Settings

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Diagnostic setting** | 📌 Routes a resource's **platform logs and metrics** to one or more destinations. |
| **Category** | The log type to send (e.g., `StorageRead`, `AuditEvent`, `GatewayDiagnosticLog`). |
| **Category group** | `allLogs` or `audit` — future-proof shortcuts. |
| **Destination: Log Analytics** | 📌 For **KQL analysis and alerting**. |
| **Destination: Storage account** | 📌 For **cheap long-term archival / compliance**. |
| **Destination: Event Hub** | 📌 For **streaming to a third-party SIEM** (Splunk, QRadar, Datadog). |
| **Destination: Partner solution** | Direct integration with certain partners. |
| **Destination-table mode** | **Azure diagnostics** (everything into `AzureDiagnostics`) vs **Resource-specific** (📌 dedicated tables — **recommended**, cheaper, easier to query). |

### 📌 The three destinations — the decision table

| Requirement | Destination |
|---|---|
| Query with KQL, build alerts and workbooks | 📌 **Log Analytics workspace** |
| Cheapest long-term retention for compliance (7 years) | 📌 **Storage account** |
| Stream to Splunk / a third-party SIEM in real time | 📌 **Event Hub** |
| All of the above | 📌 **All three at once** — a single diagnostic setting can have multiple destinations |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Send logs to Splunk." → **Event Hub.** Not a storage account, not Log Analytics.
⚠️ **Trap:** "Retain audit logs for 7 years as cheaply as possible." → **Storage account** (+ lifecycle rule to Archive tier).

### 📌 Diagnostic setting limits

| Fact | Value |
|---|---|
| Diagnostic settings per resource | **5** |
| Destinations per setting | Multiple (LAW + Storage + Event Hub) |
| Applies retroactively? | ❌ **No** — only data from creation forward |
| Enabled by default? | ❌ **NO** |
| Deploy at scale | 📌 **Azure Policy with `DeployIfNotExists`** |

⚠️ **THE diagnostic setting trap:** Resource logs are **off by default** and the setting is **not retroactive**. If someone asks "why can't I see last week's storage logs?" — because the setting didn't exist last week.

### Activity Log export

```bash
# Export the Activity Log (subscription-level) to a workspace
az monitor diagnostic-settings subscription create \
  --name activity-to-law \
  --location eastus \
  --workspace <workspaceId> \
  --logs '[{"category":"Administrative","enabled":true},
           {"category":"Security","enabled":true},
           {"category":"ServiceHealth","enabled":true},
           {"category":"Alert","enabled":true},
           {"category":"Policy","enabled":true},
           {"category":"Autoscale","enabled":true},
           {"category":"ResourceHealth","enabled":true},
           {"category":"Recommendation","enabled":true}]'
```

### Resource diagnostic settings

```bash
# Storage account blob logs → LAW + storage + event hub
az monitor diagnostic-settings create \
  --name diag-blob \
  --resource "<storageAccountId>/blobServices/default" \
  --workspace <workspaceId> \
  --storage-account <archiveStorageAccountId> \
  --event-hub-rule <eventHubAuthRuleId> \
  --logs '[{"categoryGroup":"allLogs","enabled":true}]' \
  --metrics '[{"category":"Transaction","enabled":true}]' \
  --export-to-resource-specific true

# Key Vault audit
az monitor diagnostic-settings create -n diag-kv --resource <kvId> \
  --workspace <workspaceId> \
  --logs '[{"category":"AuditEvent","enabled":true}]'

# NSG
az monitor diagnostic-settings create -n diag-nsg --resource <nsgId> \
  --workspace <workspaceId> \
  --logs '[{"category":"NetworkSecurityGroupEvent","enabled":true},
           {"category":"NetworkSecurityGroupRuleCounter","enabled":true}]'

az monitor diagnostic-settings list --resource <resourceId> -o table
az monitor diagnostic-settings categories list --resource <resourceId> -o table
```

📌 **Deploying diagnostic settings at scale = Azure Policy with `DeployIfNotExists` + a managed identity + a remediation task.** This is the correct answer to "ensure all current and future storage accounts send logs to the workspace."

---

## 6.6 Alerts and Action Groups

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Alert rule** | Scope + condition + action group + details. Runs on a schedule or continuously. |
| **Alert** | A fired instance of a rule. |
| **Signal** | What's being watched: metric, log, activity log, resource health, service health. |
| **Condition** | The threshold logic. |
| **Action group** | 📌 A **reusable** collection of notifications and actions. |
| **Alert processing rule** (formerly action rule) | Suppress alerts (maintenance windows) or apply an action group at scale. |
| **Severity** | **Sev 0 (Critical)** → **Sev 4 (Verbose)**. |
| **Alert state** | **New → Acknowledged → Closed** (user-managed). |
| **Monitor condition** | **Fired / Resolved** (system-managed). |
| **Auto-resolve / stateful alerts** | The alert closes itself when the condition clears. |
| **Evaluation frequency** | How often the rule runs. |
| **Aggregation granularity / window** | The period of data the condition evaluates. |
| **Dynamic thresholds** | 📌 ML-learned baselines instead of a static number. |
| **Alert on activity log** | e.g., "a VM was deleted", "a Service Health incident started". |
| **Smart groups** | ML-based alert correlation to cut noise. |

### 📌 Alert types

| Type | Signal | Latency | Use for |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Metric alert** | Platform/custom metrics | 📌 **~1 min (near real-time)** | CPU, memory, latency, availability |
| **Log alert** (log search) | KQL query result | 📌 **Slower** (min frequency **1–5 min**) | Complex conditions, correlation, guest logs |
| **Activity log alert** | Activity Log events | ~minutes | 📌 "VM deleted", "policy denied" |
| **Service Health alert** | Service Health events | minutes | 📌 **Azure outages / planned maintenance** |
| **Resource Health alert** | Resource Health | minutes | "This VM became unavailable" |
| **Smart detection** (App Insights) | ML anomalies | varies | Failure anomalies, performance degradation |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Alert within 1 minute of high CPU." → 📌 **Metric alert** (log alerts are too slow — minimum frequency is 1 min but with ingestion latency it's several minutes end to end).
⚠️ **Trap:** "Alert when someone deletes a resource." → 📌 **Activity log alert**, not a metric alert.
⚠️ **Trap:** "Get notified of a planned maintenance event." → 📌 **Service Health alert**.

### 📌 Action group actions

| Action | Notes |
|---|---|
| **Email** | To addresses or 📌 **Azure Resource Manager roles** (e.g., email all Owners/Contributors) |
| **SMS** | Rate limit: 📌 **1 SMS every 5 minutes** per phone number |
| **Voice call** | Rate limit: 📌 **1 call every 5 minutes** per number |
| **Push (Azure mobile app)** | 📌 **1 push every 5 minutes** |
| **Webhook** | POST the alert payload to a URL |
| **Secure webhook** | Webhook with Entra ID auth |
| **Azure Function** | Run custom code |
| **Logic App** | Run a workflow (e.g., create a ServiceNow ticket) |
| **Automation Runbook** | 📌 Run a PowerShell/Python runbook — e.g., **restart the VM**, **deallocate to stop spend** |
| **ITSM connector** | ServiceNow, etc. |
| **Event Hub** | Stream the alert |

### 📌 Action group limits — memorize

| Limit | Value |
|---|---|
| Action groups per subscription | 2,000 |
| Actions of each type per group | 📌 **10** email, **10** SMS, **10** voice, **10** push, **10** webhook |
| **Email rate limit** | 📌 **100 emails per hour** |
| **SMS rate limit** | 📌 **1 per 5 minutes** |
| **Voice rate limit** | 📌 **1 per 5 minutes** |
| **Push rate limit** | 📌 **1 per 5 minutes** |
| Webhook rate limit | ⚠️ **1,500 per hour** |
| Webhook timeout | 10 seconds |
| Global or regional? | Action groups are **global** (with a regional option for data residency) |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Why did I only get one SMS despite 20 alerts?" → 📌 **SMS is rate-limited to 1 per 5 minutes.**

### 📌 Log alert rule anatomy

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Query** | The KQL |
| **Measure** | Table rows (`count`) or a column value |
| **Aggregation type** | Count / Average / Total / Min / Max |
| **Aggregation granularity** | The window the query covers (e.g., 5 min) |
| **Operator + Threshold** | e.g., `Greater than 5` |
| **Frequency of evaluation** | 📌 **1 min – 24 h** |
| **Number of violations to trigger** | e.g., 3 out of 5 evaluations |
| **Split by dimensions** | 📌 Fire a **separate alert per Computer/resource** instead of one lumped alert |
| **Auto-mitigate** | Resolve automatically when the condition clears |

📌 **"Split by dimensions"** is the answer to "I want one alert per VM, not one alert for all VMs."

### Commands

```bash
# Action group
az monitor action-group create -g rg -n ag-ops --short-name ops \
  --action email oncall ops@contoso.com \
  --action sms oncall-sms 1 5551234567 \
  --action webhook slack "https://hooks.slack.com/..." \
  --action azurefunction fn <functionResourceId> <functionName> <httpTriggerUrl> \
  --action logicapp la <logicAppResourceId> <callbackUrl> \
  --action automationrunbook restart-vm <automationAccountId> <runbookName> <webhookUri> false

# Email an ARM role
az monitor action-group create -g rg -n ag-owners --short-name owners \
  --action armrole owners-role Owner

# Metric alert
az monitor metrics alert create -g rg -n alert-high-cpu \
  --scopes <vmResourceId> \
  --condition "avg Percentage CPU > 80" \
  --window-size 5m --evaluation-frequency 1m \
  --severity 2 --description "CPU over 80% for 5 minutes" \
  --action ag-ops --auto-mitigate true

# Metric alert with dynamic thresholds
az monitor metrics alert create -g rg -n alert-dyn-cpu \
  --scopes <vmResourceId> \
  --condition "avg Percentage CPU > dynamic medium 2 of 4" \
  --window-size 5m --evaluation-frequency 5m --action ag-ops

# Metric alert with a dimension split
az monitor metrics alert create -g rg -n alert-disk \
  --scopes <vmResourceId> \
  --condition "avg OS Disk IOPS Consumed Percentage > 90 where LUN includes *" \
  --window-size 5m --action ag-ops

# Log alert (scheduled query rule)
az monitor scheduled-query create -g rg -n alert-heartbeat-missing \
  --scopes <workspaceId> \
  --condition "count 'heartbeat' < 1" \
  --condition-query heartbeat="Heartbeat | where TimeGenerated > ago(10m) | summarize count() by Computer" \
  --evaluation-frequency 5m --window-size 10m \
  --severity 1 --action-groups <actionGroupId> \
  --description "VM stopped sending heartbeats"

# Activity log alert — resource deletion
az monitor activity-log alert create -g rg -n alert-vm-deleted \
  --scope /subscriptions/<subId> \
  --condition category=Administrative and operationName=Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachines/delete \
  --action-group ag-ops \
  --description "A VM was deleted"

# Service Health alert
az monitor activity-log alert create -g rg -n alert-service-health \
  --scope /subscriptions/<subId> \
  --condition category=ServiceHealth \
  --action-group ag-ops

# Alert processing rule (suppression during maintenance)
az monitor alert-processing-rule create -g rg -n suppress-maintenance \
  --rule-type RemoveAllActionGroups \
  --scopes /subscriptions/<subId>/resourceGroups/rg-prod \
  --schedule-recurrence-type Weekly --schedule-recurrence 'Saturday' \
  --schedule-start-time "02:00:00" --schedule-end-time "06:00:00"

az monitor metrics alert list -g rg -o table
az monitor activity-log alert list -g rg -o table
```

```powershell
$email = New-AzActionGroupReceiver -Name "oncall" -EmailReceiver -EmailAddress ops@contoso.com
Set-AzActionGroup -ResourceGroupName rg -Name ag-ops -ShortName ops -Receiver $email

$condition = New-AzMetricAlertRuleV2Criteria -MetricName "Percentage CPU" `
  -TimeAggregation Average -Operator GreaterThan -Threshold 80
Add-AzMetricAlertRuleV2 -Name alert-high-cpu -ResourceGroupName rg `
  -WindowSize 00:05:00 -Frequency 00:01:00 -TargetResourceId <vmId> `
  -Condition $condition -ActionGroupId <agId> -Severity 2
```

---

## 6.7 Azure Monitor Agent and VM Insights

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Azure Monitor Agent (AMA)** | 📌 The **current, single** agent for collecting guest metrics and logs. |
| **Log Analytics agent (MMA/OMS)** | 📌 **Legacy — RETIRED 31 August 2024.** |
| **Diagnostics extension (WAD/LAD)** | Legacy metrics/logs extension. |
| **Data Collection Rule (DCR)** | 📌 Defines **what** AMA collects (data sources) and **where** it sends it (destinations). Associated with VMs. |
| **DCR association (DCRA)** | Links a DCR to a VM/VMSS/Arc machine. |
| **Data Collection Endpoint (DCE)** | Ingestion endpoint; needed for private link, custom logs, and some regions. |
| **VM Insights** | 📌 A curated experience: performance charts + **Map** (dependency/process view) + health. |
| **Dependency agent** | 📌 Required **only** for the **Map** feature of VM Insights. |
| **Container Insights** | The AKS/container equivalent. |
| **Azure Arc-enabled servers** | On-prem/other-cloud machines managed like Azure VMs — AMA works on them. |

### 📌 AMA vs the legacy agents

| | **AMA** | Log Analytics agent (MMA) |
|---|---|---|
| Status | 📌 **Current** | 📌 **RETIRED 31 Aug 2024** |
| Configuration | 📌 **Data Collection Rules** (in Azure) | Workspace config (in the workspace) |
| Multiple destinations | ✅ Multiple workspaces + Azure Monitor Metrics | 1 workspace (+ multi-homing hacks) |
| Filtering at collection | ✅ (XPath, transformations) | Limited |
| Managed identity support | ✅ | ❌ |
| Cost | Cheaper (send less) | More |
| Windows + Linux | ✅ | ✅ |
| Arc support | ✅ | ✅ |

📌 **If a question mentions the Log Analytics agent / MMA / OMS agent, the modern correct answer is AMA + DCR.**

### 📌 The AMA setup chain

```
1. Create a Log Analytics workspace
2. Create a Data Collection Rule (DCR)
     - Data sources: Performance counters / Windows Event Logs / Syslog / custom
     - Destinations: the LAW (and/or Azure Monitor Metrics)
3. Install the AMA extension on the VM
4. Associate the DCR with the VM  (DCRA)
5. (Optional) Install the Dependency Agent for VM Insights Map
6. Data flows
```

⚠️ **THE AMA trap:** 📌 **Installing AMA alone collects NOTHING.** Without a **DCR association**, the agent sits idle. "The agent is installed but no data appears" → **missing DCR association**.

### 📌 What a DCR can collect

| Data source | Examples |
|---|---|
| **Performance counters** | `\Processor(_Total)\% Processor Time`, `\Memory\Available MBytes`, `\LogicalDisk(*)\% Free Space` |
| **Windows Event Logs** | Application, System, Security (XPath filters supported) |
| **Syslog** | Facilities + severities (Linux) |
| **IIS logs** | W3C logs |
| **Custom text/JSON logs** | Via the Logs Ingestion API |
| **Change tracking** | File/registry/software changes |

### 📌 VM Insights

| Feature | Requires |
|---|---|
| Performance charts (CPU, memory, disk, network) | **AMA + DCR** |
| **Map** (process + dependency visualization) | 📌 **AMA + Dependency Agent** |
| Health (deprecated) | — |

⚠️ **Trap:** "See which processes on VM-A are talking to VM-B." → 📌 **VM Insights Map** → needs the **Dependency Agent**.

### Commands

```bash
# Install AMA
az vm extension set -g rg --vm-name vm-web-01 \
  -n AzureMonitorWindowsAgent --publisher Microsoft.Azure.Monitor \
  --enable-auto-upgrade true

az vm extension set -g rg --vm-name vm-lnx-01 \
  -n AzureMonitorLinuxAgent --publisher Microsoft.Azure.Monitor \
  --enable-auto-upgrade true

# Dependency agent (for the Map)
az vm extension set -g rg --vm-name vm-web-01 \
  -n DependencyAgentWindows --publisher Microsoft.Azure.Monitoring.DependencyAgent

# DCR
az monitor data-collection rule create -g rg -n dcr-vm-perf -l eastus --rule-file dcr.json

# Associate the DCR with a VM  ⚠️ REQUIRED
az monitor data-collection rule association create \
  -n dcra-vm-web-01 --rule-id <dcrId> --resource <vmId>

az monitor data-collection rule list -g rg -o table
az monitor data-collection rule association list --resource <vmId> -o table
```

**Minimal DCR JSON:**
```json
{
  "location": "eastus",
  "properties": {
    "dataSources": {
      "performanceCounters": [{
        "name": "perfCounters",
        "streams": ["Microsoft-Perf"],
        "samplingFrequencyInSeconds": 60,
        "counterSpecifiers": [
          "\\Processor(_Total)\\% Processor Time",
          "\\Memory\\Available MBytes",
          "\\LogicalDisk(_Total)\\% Free Space"
        ]
      }],
      "windowsEventLogs": [{
        "name": "eventLogs",
        "streams": ["Microsoft-Event"],
        "xPathQueries": ["System!*[System[(Level=1 or Level=2 or Level=3)]]"]
      }]
    },
    "destinations": {
      "logAnalytics": [{ "workspaceResourceId": "<workspaceId>", "name": "law-dest" }]
    },
    "dataFlows": [{
      "streams": ["Microsoft-Perf", "Microsoft-Event"],
      "destinations": ["law-dest"]
    }]
  }
}
```

---

## 6.8 Application Insights

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Application Insights** | APM (Application Performance Monitoring) — telemetry for your app code. |
| **Workspace-based** | 📌 **The current model** — data lives in a **Log Analytics workspace**. |
| **Classic** | ⚠️ **Retired Feb 2024.** |
| **Instrumentation key** | Legacy identifier. |
| **Connection string** | 📌 **The current way** to point an app at App Insights. |
| **Auto-instrumentation / codeless** | Attach without changing code (App Service, Functions, AKS). |
| **Live Metrics Stream** | Real-time (1-second) telemetry with no sampling. |
| **Application Map** | Visual dependency graph of your components. |
| **Availability test** | 📌 Synthetic outside-in probing: **URL ping test**, **standard test**, **custom TrackAvailability**. |
| **Sampling** | Reduce ingested volume: **adaptive** (SDK-side, default), **fixed-rate**, **ingestion** (server-side). |
| **Smart detection** | ML anomaly detection (failure anomalies, memory leaks, slow response). |
| **Transaction search** | Find individual requests/traces. |
| **Distributed tracing** | Follow a request across microservices via `operation_Id`. |
| **Profiler / Snapshot Debugger** | Deep .NET performance and exception capture. |

### 📌 App Insights tables

| Table | Contains |
|---|---|
| `requests` | Incoming HTTP requests |
| `dependencies` | Outgoing calls (SQL, HTTP, storage) |
| `exceptions` | Unhandled and tracked exceptions |
| `traces` | Log messages |
| `customEvents` | Your business events |
| `customMetrics` | Your metrics |
| `pageViews` | Browser page loads |
| `availabilityResults` | Availability test results |
| `performanceCounters` | Perf counters |
| `browserTimings` | Client-side timings |

### 📌 Availability tests

| Test type | Notes |
|---|---|
| **URL ping test** | ⚠️ **Retired 30 Sept 2026** — simple GET, single URL |
| **Standard test** | 📌 The replacement: HTTP verb, headers, body, TLS validation, custom success criteria |
| **Custom TrackAvailability** | Code-based, for complex multi-step flows |
| Locations | 📌 **Recommend at least 5** test locations |
| Alert rule | Fire when **X of Y** locations report failure |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Test the app from multiple geographies without writing code." → 📌 **Standard availability test** with 5+ locations.

```bash
az monitor app-insights component create -g rg -a ai-contoso -l eastus \
  --workspace <workspaceId> --application-type web

az monitor app-insights component show -g rg -a ai-contoso --query connectionString

# Wire it into an App Service (codeless)
az webapp config appsettings set -g rg -n app-contoso --settings \
  APPLICATIONINSIGHTS_CONNECTION_STRING="<connString>" \
  ApplicationInsightsAgent_EXTENSION_VERSION="~3"

az monitor app-insights query -a ai-contoso \
  --analytics-query "requests | summarize count() by resultCode | order by count_ desc"
```

---

## 6.9 Azure Backup

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Recovery Services vault (RSV)** | 📌 The vault for **VMs, Azure Files, SQL/SAP in VMs, and on-prem (MARS/MABS/DPM)**. |
| **Backup vault** | 📌 The **newer** vault for **Azure Blobs, Azure Disks, Azure Database for PostgreSQL/MySQL flexible server, and Kubernetes**. |
| **Backup policy** | Schedule + retention (daily/weekly/monthly/yearly). |
| **Recovery point** | A restorable snapshot in time. |
| **Instant Restore** | 📌 Snapshots kept **locally** for fast restore. Default **2 days**, configurable **1–5 days**. |
| **GRS vault** | Backups replicated to the paired region. |
| **Cross Region Restore (CRR)** | 📌 Restore from the **secondary region** on demand. Requires **GRS** and must be **explicitly enabled**. |
| **Soft delete** | 📌 Deleted backup data retained **14 days** (default, free); can extend to **180 days** (paid). **On by default.** |
| **Immutable vault** | Prevents backup data deletion/shortening of retention. Can be **locked** (irreversible). |
| **Multi-user authorization (MUA)** | Requires a second approver (via a Resource Guard) for destructive operations. |
| **MARS agent** | 📌 **Microsoft Azure Recovery Services agent** — backs up **files/folders and system state** from Windows machines **directly to Azure**. |
| **MABS / DPM** | Microsoft Azure Backup Server / Data Protection Manager — application-aware on-prem backup that then ships to Azure. |
| **Backup extension** | `VMSnapshot` — installed on Azure VMs automatically. |
| **Application-consistent backup** | Uses **VSS** (Windows) or a **pre/post script** (Linux) so DBs are transactionally consistent. |
| **File-consistent / crash-consistent** | Weaker consistency levels. |
| **Selective disk backup** | Back up only some data disks. |
| **Backup center** | The central management pane across vaults. |

### 📌 Which vault? — a guaranteed question

| Workload | Vault |
|---|---|
| **Azure VM** | 📌 **Recovery Services vault** |
| **Azure Files share** | 📌 **Recovery Services vault** |
| SQL Server **in an Azure VM** | 📌 **Recovery Services vault** |
| SAP HANA **in an Azure VM** | 📌 **Recovery Services vault** |
| On-prem files/folders (**MARS**) | 📌 **Recovery Services vault** |
| On-prem VMs via **MABS/DPM** | 📌 **Recovery Services vault** |
| **Azure Blobs** | 📌 **Backup vault** |
| **Azure Managed Disks** | 📌 **Backup vault** |
| **Azure Database for PostgreSQL / MySQL (flexible)** | 📌 **Backup vault** |
| **AKS / Kubernetes** | 📌 **Backup vault** |

⚠️ **THE vault trap:** **VMs → Recovery Services vault. Blobs and Disks → Backup vault.** Mixing these up is the single most common Backup mistake.

### 📌 Recovery Services vault rules

| Rule | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Region** | 📌 The vault **must be in the SAME region** as the VMs it protects |
| Cross-region protection | ❌ You cannot back up a West Europe VM into an East US vault |
| **Redundancy (LRS/GRS/ZRS)** | 📌 **Can only be changed BEFORE the first backup is configured.** After that it's **locked**. |
| **Cross Region Restore** | Requires **GRS**; must be explicitly enabled |
| Moving a vault | Possible between RGs/subscriptions with restrictions |
| Deleting a vault | Must **stop backup + delete data** for every protected item first |
| **Soft delete** | **14 days**, on by default, **free**; extendable to 180 days (paid) |
| Vaults per subscription per region | 500 |
| VMs per vault | 2,000 (1,000 for classic) |
| Policies per vault | 200 |

⚠️ **THE redundancy trap:** 📌 **You cannot change a Recovery Services vault from GRS to LRS (or vice versa) once ANY backup item is protected.** You must create a new vault. This is asked constantly.

⚠️ **Trap:** "Delete the vault." → Fails while protected items exist. You must **Stop backup → Delete backup data → wait out soft delete (or undelete+purge) → delete the vault.**

### 📌 VM backup policy — retention

| Frequency | Max retention |
|---|---|
| **Daily** | **9,999 days** (~27 years) |
| **Weekly** | 5,163 weeks |
| **Monthly** | 1,188 months |
| **Yearly** | 99 years |
| **Instant Restore snapshots** | 📌 **1–5 days** (default **2**) |
| **Backups per day (Standard policy)** | 📌 **1** |
| **Backups per day (Enhanced policy)** | 📌 Up to **hourly** (every 4/6/8/12 h) |

📌 **Enhanced policy** is required for: **hourly backups**, **Trusted Launch VMs**, and multi-disk crash consistency.

⚠️ **Trap:** "Back up the VM every 4 hours." → 📌 **Enhanced backup policy** (Standard is once daily).
⚠️ **Trap:** "Back up a Trusted Launch VM." → 📌 **Enhanced policy** required.

### 📌 VM restore options

| Option | Result |
|---|---|
| **Create new VM** | Restores a full new VM (choose a name, VNet, subnet) |
| **Restore disks** | 📌 Restores the **disks + a deployment template** — you then create the VM yourself. **The most flexible option.** |
| **Replace existing** | Overwrites the current VM's disks (requires unmanaged→managed constraints; **not for unmanaged**) |
| **File recovery / Item-level restore** | 📌 Mounts the recovery point as a **local drive via a downloadable script** — restore **individual files** without restoring the whole VM |
| **Cross Region Restore** | Restore in the paired region (GRS + CRR enabled) |
| **Cross-subscription restore** | Restore into a different subscription |
| **Restore to a different zone** | Supported with zone-pinned restores |

⚠️ **THE restore trap:** "Restore **one file** from a VM backup." → 📌 **File recovery** (item-level restore) — download and run the script, which mounts the recovery point as a drive. **Don't restore the whole VM.**

⚠️ **Trap:** "Restore a VM into a different region." → 📌 Requires **GRS + Cross Region Restore enabled**.

### 📌 Azure Files backup

| Fact | Detail |
|---|---|
| Vault | Recovery Services vault (same region as the storage account) |
| Mechanism | 📌 **Share snapshots** — no agent |
| Snapshots per share | 200 |
| Retention | Daily up to 10 years (with vaulted backup) |
| Restore | Full share or **individual files/folders** |
| Types | **Snapshot backup** (local) and **Vaulted backup** (offsite, GRS) |

### 📌 MARS agent

| Fact | Detail |
|---|---|
| Backs up | 📌 **Files, folders, and Windows system state** |
| Can back up | On-prem Windows machines **and** Azure VMs (file-level) |
| Cannot back up | 📌 **A whole VM/bare metal** (that's MABS/DPM/ASR), Linux |
| Retention | Up to 9,999 recovery points |
| Encryption | 📌 A **passphrase you set** — ⚠️ **Microsoft cannot recover it. Lose it and your backups are gone forever.** |
| Backups per day | **3** |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Back up on-prem file server files to Azure with the simplest solution." → 📌 **MARS agent**.
⚠️ **Trap:** "Back up on-prem Hyper-V/VMware VMs (application-aware, with a local disk staging copy)." → 📌 **MABS or DPM**.
⚠️ **Trap:** "Lost the MARS passphrase." → 📌 **The data is unrecoverable.** Microsoft has no copy.

### 📌 Backup RBAC roles

| Role | Can |
|---|---|
| **Backup Contributor** | Everything except granting access and **deleting the vault** |
| **Backup Operator** | Everything except removing backups and managing policy |
| **Backup Reader** | Monitor only |

### Commands

```bash
# Vault + redundancy (SET REDUNDANCY BEFORE THE FIRST BACKUP)
az backup vault create -g rg -n rsv-prod -l eastus
az backup vault backup-properties set -g rg -n rsv-prod --backup-storage-redundancy GeoRedundant
az backup vault backup-properties set -g rg -n rsv-prod --cross-region-restore-flag true

# Soft delete
az backup vault backup-properties set -g rg -n rsv-prod --soft-delete-feature-state Enable

# Enable VM backup
az backup protection enable-for-vm -g rg -v rsv-prod \
  --vm vm-web-01 --policy-name DefaultPolicy

# On-demand backup
az backup protection backup-now -g rg -v rsv-prod \
  -c <containerName> -i vm-web-01 --retain-until 31-12-2026

# List recovery points
az backup recoverypoint list -g rg -v rsv-prod -c <containerName> -i vm-web-01 -o table

# Restore disks
az backup restore restore-disks -g rg -v rsv-prod \
  -c <containerName> -i vm-web-01 --rp-name <recoveryPointName> \
  --storage-account stacct --target-resource-group rg-restore

# File recovery (item-level)
az backup restore files mount-rp -g rg -v rsv-prod \
  -c <containerName> -i vm-web-01 --rp-name <recoveryPointName>
az backup restore files unmount-rp -g rg -v rsv-prod -c <containerName> -i vm-web-01 --rp-name <rp>

# Policy
az backup policy create -g rg -v rsv-prod -n policy-daily \
  --backup-management-type AzureIaasVM --policy @policy.json

az backup protection update-for-vm -g rg -v rsv-prod \
  -c <containerName> -i vm-web-01 --policy-name policy-daily

# Stop / resume
az backup protection disable -g rg -v rsv-prod -c <c> -i vm-web-01 --retain-recovery-points true
az backup protection disable -g rg -v rsv-prod -c <c> -i vm-web-01 --delete-backup-data true --yes
az backup protection resume -g rg -v rsv-prod -c <c> -i vm-web-01 --policy-name policy-daily

# Azure Files backup
az backup protection enable-for-azurefileshare -g rg -v rsv-prod \
  --storage-account stacct --azure-file-share myshare --policy-name DefaultPolicy

az backup job list -g rg -v rsv-prod -o table
```

```powershell
$vault = New-AzRecoveryServicesVault -ResourceGroupName rg -Name rsv-prod -Location eastus
Set-AzRecoveryServicesBackupProperty -Vault $vault -BackupStorageRedundancy GeoRedundant
Set-AzRecoveryServicesVaultContext -Vault $vault

$policy = Get-AzRecoveryServicesBackupProtectionPolicy -Name "DefaultPolicy"
Enable-AzRecoveryServicesBackupProtection -Policy $policy -Name vm-web-01 -ResourceGroupName rg

Backup-AzRecoveryServicesBackupItem -Item $item -ExpiryDateTimeUTC (Get-Date).AddYears(1)
Get-AzRecoveryServicesBackupRecoveryPoint -Item $item
Restore-AzRecoveryServicesBackupItem -RecoveryPoint $rp -StorageAccountName stacct -StorageAccountResourceGroupName rg
```

## 6.10 Azure Site Recovery

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Azure Site Recovery (ASR)** | 📌 **Disaster recovery** — continuous replication + orchestrated failover. |
| **Replication** | Continuous copying of disk writes to the target region. |
| **Recovery point** | Crash-consistent (📌 every **5 minutes**) or app-consistent (📌 default **every 4 hours**, configurable 1–12 h). |
| **RPO** | 📌 ASR RPO for Azure-to-Azure: **~30 seconds** (continuous replication). |
| **RTO** | The time to bring the workload up — depends on the plan; ASR SLA target is **2 hours** for most workloads. |
| **Cache storage account** | 📌 A storage account in the **SOURCE** region holding replication logs before they ship to the target. |
| **Target region** | Where the replicas land. |
| **Recovery plan** | 📌 An ordered failover **runbook**: groups, manual actions, and **Automation runbooks**. |
| **Test failover** | 📌 **Non-disruptive** — creates VMs in an isolated network. **Production keeps running.** Doesn't affect replication. |
| **Planned failover** | Zero data loss; shuts down the source first. (Not available for Azure-to-Azure.) |
| **Unplanned / Failover** | For real disasters; may lose data since the last recovery point. |
| **Commit** | 📌 Finalize the failover — you can no longer pick a different recovery point. |
| **Re-protect** | Set up replication back to the original region. |
| **Failback** | Fail back to the original region (requires re-protect first). |
| **Mobility service** | The agent installed in guest VMs for VMware/physical scenarios. |
| **Process server / Configuration server** | On-prem components for VMware/physical replication. |
| **Multi-VM consistency** | Replication group so multiple VMs share crash-consistent points (for clusters). |

### 📌 ASR scenarios

| Source | Target | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Azure VM → Azure region** | Another region | 📌 The most common. Agentless (extension-based). |
| **Azure VM → another zone** | Same region | Zone-to-zone DR |
| On-prem **Hyper-V** → Azure | | With/without VMM |
| On-prem **VMware / physical** → Azure | | Uses configuration/process servers + Mobility service |
| Azure → on-prem | | Failback |

### 📌 Backup vs Site Recovery — THE comparison

| | **Azure Backup** | **Azure Site Recovery** |
|---|---|---|
| Purpose | 📌 **Data protection / retention** | 📌 **Business continuity / DR** |
| Protects against | Accidental deletion, corruption, ransomware | 📌 **Region/datacenter outage** |
| **RPO** | Hours (daily backup) — 📌 up to 24 h | 📌 **~30 seconds** |
| **RTO** | Hours | 📌 **Minutes** |
| Data copies | Point-in-time recovery points | 📌 **Continuous replica** |
| Retention | 📌 **Years** (up to 99) | 📌 **Hours–days** (up to 72 h of recovery points) |
| Cost | Storage per GB retained | 📌 **Replica VM disks + license per instance** (compute only billed on failover) |
| Restores individual files? | ✅ **Yes** | ❌ **No** |
| Failover orchestration | ❌ | ✅ **Recovery plans** |
| Target VM running? | n/a | ❌ **Not until failover** — you pay for **storage only** |

📌 **The rule: Backup = time machine. ASR = a warm standby copy in another region.** They are complementary, not alternatives.

⚠️ **Trap:** "Recover a deleted file from 3 months ago." → 📌 **Backup** (ASR keeps only ~72 hours).
⚠️ **Trap:** "Fail over the whole application to another region in under 15 minutes." → 📌 **ASR + a recovery plan**.
⚠️ **Trap:** "Which is cheaper for DR — ASR or a second running VM?" → ASR. 📌 **The replica VM is not running; you only pay for its storage and the ASR license until you fail over.**

### 📌 Test failover facts

| Fact | Detail |
|---|---|
| Disruptive? | 📌 **No** — production keeps running |
| Affects replication? | 📌 **No** — replication continues |
| Network | 📌 Use an **isolated VNet** (not the production DR network) |
| Cleanup | 📌 **Always "Clean up test failover"** afterwards to delete the test VMs and stop the charges |
| Recommended frequency | 📌 At least **every 6 months** (compliance often demands it) |

⚠️ **Trap:** "Test DR without impacting production." → 📌 **Test failover into an isolated network.** Not "Failover."

### Commands

```bash
az resource create -g rg -n rsv-dr --resource-type Microsoft.RecoveryServices/vaults \
  --is-full-object -p '{"location":"westus2","sku":{"name":"Standard"},"properties":{}}'

# ASR is mostly portal/PowerShell driven
```

```powershell
$vault = Get-AzRecoveryServicesVault -Name rsv-dr
Set-AzRecoveryServicesAsrVaultContext -Vault $vault

# Test failover
$rp = Get-AzRecoveryServicesAsrRecoveryPoint -ReplicationProtectedItem $pi | Select -Last 1
Start-AzRecoveryServicesAsrTestFailoverJob -ReplicationProtectedItem $pi `
  -Direction PrimaryToRecovery -AzureVMNetworkId $testVnetId -RecoveryPoint $rp

# Cleanup — ALWAYS
Start-AzRecoveryServicesAsrTestFailoverCleanupJob -ReplicationProtectedItem $pi

# Real failover
Start-AzRecoveryServicesAsrUnplannedFailoverJob -ReplicationProtectedItem $pi `
  -Direction PrimaryToRecovery -RecoveryPoint $rp
Start-AzRecoveryServicesAsrCommitFailoverJob -ReplicationProtectedItem $pi

# Recovery plan
Start-AzRecoveryServicesAsrTestFailoverJob -RecoveryPlan $plan -Direction PrimaryToRecovery
```
