## 6.11 Update Manager

### Key Terms

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Azure Update Manager** | 📌 The **current** unified patch management service. **No Log Analytics workspace or Automation account required.** |
| **Update Management (legacy)** | ⚠️ **Retired 31 Aug 2024.** Required an Automation account + LAW. |
| **Periodic assessment** | Scans for missing updates every **24 hours**. |
| **Maintenance configuration** | 📌 The **schedule** resource: window, recurrence, reboot behavior, and update classifications. |
| **Maintenance window** | Duration (📌 **min 1h 30m**, max 3h 55m). ⚠️ **No new update starts in the last 20 minutes.** |
| **Dynamic scope** | Target VMs by **subscription/RG/location/tags** — new matching VMs are picked up automatically. |
| **Static scope** | An explicit machine list. |
| **Automatic VM guest patching** | 📌 Azure automatically installs **Critical and Security** patches during off-peak hours; requires `patchMode=AutomaticByPlatform`. |
| **Patch orchestration** | `AutomaticByPlatform` (Azure Update Manager / auto patching), `AutomaticByOS` (Windows Update), `Manual` / `ImageDefault` (Linux). |
| **Hotpatching** | 📌 Patch **without a reboot** — Windows Server **Azure Edition** with Server Core, on Azure or Arc. |
| **Update classification** | Critical, Security, Definition, Update Rollup, Feature Pack, Service Pack, Tools, Updates. |
| **Include/Exclude KBs / packages** | Fine-grained control. |
| **Reboot setting** | Reboot if required / Never reboot / Always reboot. |
| **Arc-enabled servers** | On-prem/other-cloud machines patched the same way. |

### 📌 Update Manager facts

| Fact | Detail |
|---|---|
| Requires a LAW? | 📌 **No** (the legacy solution did) |
| Requires an Automation account? | 📌 **No** |
| Requires an agent? | Uses the **VM extension** (auto-deployed); **no MMA** |
| Supports Arc machines? | ✅ Yes |
| Assessment frequency | Every **24 hours** (periodic assessment) |
| **Maintenance window min** | 📌 **1 hour 30 minutes** |
| Maintenance window max | 3 hours 55 minutes |
| **Last 20 minutes** | 📌 **No new update installation starts** |
| Cost | Free for Azure VMs; charged for Arc machines (except with Defender for Servers P2) |

⚠️ **Trap:** "The maintenance window is 1 hour." → 📌 **Invalid.** The minimum is **1h 30m**.
⚠️ **Trap:** "Patching still hasn't finished at the end of the window." → 📌 In-flight updates finish, but nothing new starts in the last **20 minutes**, and the run ends at the window's close.
⚠️ **Trap:** "Patch VMs without a reboot." → 📌 **Hotpatching** on **Windows Server Azure Edition (Server Core)**.

### 📌 Automatic VM guest patching

| Fact | Detail |
|---|---|
| Enable via | `--enable-auto-update true --patch-mode AutomaticByPlatform` |
| Installs | 📌 **Critical and Security** patches only |
| When | Off-peak hours, VM local time |
| Reboots? | ✅ Yes if required (or configure `rebootSetting`) |
| Requires | The VM Agent, and a supported marketplace image |
| Assessment | 📌 Automatic every ~24 h |

```bash
# Automatic guest patching
az vm update -g rg -n vm-web-01 \
  --set osProfile.windowsConfiguration.patchSettings.patchMode=AutomaticByPlatform \
        osProfile.windowsConfiguration.patchSettings.assessmentMode=AutomaticByPlatform

az vm update -g rg -n vm-lnx-01 \
  --set osProfile.linuxConfiguration.patchSettings.patchMode=AutomaticByPlatform

# One-off assessment and install
az vm assess-patches -g rg -n vm-web-01
az vm install-patches -g rg -n vm-web-01 --maximum-duration PT2H \
  --reboot-setting IfRequired --classifications-to-include-win Critical Security

# Maintenance configuration
az maintenance configuration create -g rg -n mc-monthly-patch -l eastus \
  --maintenance-scope InGuestPatch \
  --start-date-time "2026-08-01 02:00" --duration "03:00" --time-zone "Eastern Standard Time" \
  --recur-every "1Month Second Saturday" \
  --reboot-setting IfRequired \
  --windows-parameters classifications-to-include="Critical,Security"

# Assign it (static scope)
az maintenance assignment create -g rg -n assign-vm-web-01 \
  --maintenance-configuration-id <mcId> \
  --resource-name vm-web-01 --resource-type virtualMachines \
  --provider-name Microsoft.Compute -l eastus

# Dynamic scope by tag
az maintenance assignment create-or-update-subscription \
  --maintenance-configuration-id <mcId> -n dyn-scope \
  --filter-tags '{"patchGroup":["ring1"]}'
```

### Azure Automation (context)

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Automation account** | Container for runbooks, variables, credentials, schedules, and DSC. |
| **Runbook** | PowerShell / PowerShell Workflow / Python / graphical script. |
| **Hybrid Runbook Worker** | Runs runbooks on-prem or in another cloud. |
| **Webhook** | Trigger a runbook via HTTP POST — 📌 **how an Action Group calls a runbook**. |
| **Schedule** | Time-based trigger. |
| **Shared resources** | Variables, credentials, certificates, connections, modules. |
| **Run As account** | ⚠️ **Retired 30 Sept 2023** — use **managed identities**. |
| **State Configuration (DSC)** | ⚠️ Being retired — use Azure Machine Configuration (Guest Configuration). |
| **Change Tracking and Inventory** | Track file/registry/software/service changes. Now uses AMA. |

📌 **The classic automation pattern:** Budget alert → Action Group → Automation runbook → deallocate dev VMs.

```bash
az network dns zone create -g rg -n contoso.com
az network dns zone show -g rg -n contoso.com --query nameServers
az network dns record-set a add-record -g rg -z contoso.com -n www -a 203.0.113.5
az network dns record-set a create -g rg -z contoso.com -n @ --target-resource <pipId>

az network private-dns zone create -g rg -n contoso.internal
az network private-dns link vnet create -g rg -n link1 -z contoso.internal -v vnet1 -e true

az network private-endpoint create -g rg -n pe1 --vnet-name vnet1 --subnet subnet1 \
  --private-connection-resource-id <saId> --group-id blob --connection-name conn1

az network vnet-gateway create -g rg -n vpngw --vnet vnet1 --public-ip-addresses pip-gw \
  --gateway-type Vpn --vpn-type RouteBased --sku VpnGw2AZ --no-wait

az network vpn-connection create -g rg -n conn1 --vnet-gateway1 vpngw --local-gateway2 lng1 --shared-key 'PSK'

az network bastion create -g rg -n bastion1 --public-ip-address pip-bastion \
  --vnet-name vnet1 -l eastus --sku Standard

az network firewall policy create -g rg -n fwpol --sku Standard --enable-dns-proxy true

# TROUBLESHOOTING
az network nic list-effective-nsg -g rg -n nic1

az network watcher test-ip-flow -g rg --vm vm01 --direction Inbound --protocol TCP \
  --local 10.0.1.4:443 --remote 203.0.113.5:60000
az network watcher show-next-hop -g rg --vm vm01 --source-ip 10.0.1.4 --dest-ip 10.2.0.5
az network watcher test-connectivity -g rg --source-resource vm01 --dest-address www.microsoft.com --dest-port 443
az network watcher flow-log create -g rg -n fl1 -l eastus --nsg nsg1 --storage-account stacct --traffic-analytics true
```

```bash
az monitor data-collection rule association create -n dcra1 --rule-id <dcrId> --resource <vmId>

az backup restore files mount-rp -g rg -v rsv1 -c <container> -i vm01 --rp-name <rp>
az backup job list -g rg -v rsv1 -o table

az vm assess-patches -g rg -n vm01
az vm install-patches -g rg -n vm01 --maximum-duration PT2H --reboot-setting IfRequired
az maintenance configuration create -g rg -n mc1 -l eastus --maintenance-scope InGuestPatch \
  --start-date-time "2026-08-01 02:00" --duration "03:00" --recur-every "1Month Second Saturday"
```

## B. PowerShell Cheat Sheet

### Setup

```powershell
Install-Module -Name Az -Repository PSGallery -Force -AllowClobber
Install-Module Microsoft.Graph -Scope CurrentUser
Update-Module -Name Az

Connect-AzAccount
Connect-AzAccount -UseDeviceAuthentication
Connect-AzAccount -Identity
Connect-AzAccount -ServicePrincipal -Credential $cred -Tenant <tid>
Connect-MgGraph -Scopes "User.ReadWrite.All","Group.ReadWrite.All"

Get-AzContext
Get-AzSubscription
Set-AzContext -Subscription "Prod"
Get-AzTenant
Disconnect-AzAccount
Get-Module Az.* -ListAvailable
```

### The verb-noun pattern

| Verb | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `Get-` | Retrieve |
| `New-` | Create (resource doesn't exist) |
| `Set-` | Configure (⚠️ often **replaces**) |
| `Update-` | Modify (⚠️ often needs a fetched object) |
| `Add-` | Add to a collection |
| `Remove-` | Delete |
| `Start-` / `Stop-` / `Restart-` | Lifecycle |
| `Enable-` / `Disable-` | Toggle |
| `Test-` | Validate |
| `Invoke-` | Execute an action |

📌 **The Azure PowerShell pattern for edits:** *Get the object → modify the object in memory → push it back with `Set-`/`Update-`.*

```powershell
$vnet = Get-AzVirtualNetwork -ResourceGroupName rg -Name vnet1
Add-AzVirtualNetworkSubnetConfig -Name db -AddressPrefix 10.0.3.0/24 -VirtualNetwork $vnet
$vnet | Set-AzVirtualNetwork          # ← without this, nothing happens
```

⚠️ **THE PowerShell trap:** Forgetting the final `Set-` / `Update-`. The config cmdlets only change the **in-memory** object.

### Resource groups and resources

```powershell
New-AzResourceGroup -Name rg-prod -Location eastus -Tag @{env="prod"}
Get-AzResourceGroup | Format-Table ResourceGroupName, Location
Remove-AzResourceGroup -Name rg-prod -Force -AsJob
Export-AzResourceGroup -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -Path ./template.json

Get-AzResource -ResourceGroupName rg-prod | Format-Table Name, ResourceType
Get-AzResource -Tag @{env="prod"}
Move-AzResource -DestinationResourceGroupName rg-target -ResourceId <id>
Set-AzResource -ResourceId <id> -Tag @{env="prod"} -Force
Update-AzTag -ResourceId <id> -Tag @{owner="alice"} -Operation Merge

New-AzResourceLock -LockName nodel -LockLevel CanNotDelete -ResourceGroupName rg-prod
Get-AzResourceLock -ResourceGroupName rg-prod
Remove-AzResourceLock -LockName nodel -ResourceGroupName rg-prod -Force

Register-AzResourceProvider -ProviderNamespace Microsoft.Compute
Get-AzResourceProvider -ListAvailable | Where RegistrationState -eq "Registered"
```

### Identity (Microsoft Graph)

```powershell
Connect-MgGraph -Scopes "User.ReadWrite.All"

$pwd = @{ Password = "P@ssw0rd!"; ForceChangePasswordNextSignIn = $true }
New-MgUser -DisplayName "Alice" -UserPrincipalName alice@contoso.com `
  -MailNickname alice -AccountEnabled -PasswordProfile $pwd -UsageLocation US

Get-MgUser -Filter "startswith(displayName,'A')" | Select DisplayName, UserPrincipalName
Get-MgUser -UserId alice@contoso.com
Update-MgUser -UserId alice@contoso.com -JobTitle "Engineer" -UsageLocation US
Remove-MgUser -UserId alice@contoso.com
Get-MgDirectoryDeletedItemAsUser
Restore-MgDirectoryDeletedItem -DirectoryObjectId <oid>

New-MgGroup -DisplayName Sales -MailEnabled:$false -MailNickname sales -SecurityEnabled
New-MgGroup -DisplayName Sales-Dyn -MailEnabled:$false -MailNickname salesdyn -SecurityEnabled `
  -GroupTypes "DynamicMembership" -MembershipRule '(user.department -eq "Sales")' `
  -MembershipRuleProcessingState "On"
Add-MgGroupMember -GroupId <gid> -DirectoryObjectId <uid>
Get-MgGroupMember -GroupId <gid>
Remove-MgGroupMemberByRef -GroupId <gid> -DirectoryObjectId <uid>

Get-MgSubscribedSku | Select SkuPartNumber, ConsumedUnits
Set-MgUserLicense -UserId alice@contoso.com -AddLicenses @{SkuId="<id>"} -RemoveLicenses @()

New-MgDirectoryAdministrativeUnit -DisplayName "Europe"
```

### RBAC

```powershell
Get-AzRoleDefinition -Name Contributor
Get-AzRoleDefinition | Where IsCustom -eq $true
Get-AzProviderOperation "Microsoft.Compute/*" | Select Operation, Description

New-AzRoleAssignment -SignInName alice@contoso.com -RoleDefinitionName Reader -Scope "/subscriptions/<id>"
New-AzRoleAssignment -ObjectId <gid> -RoleDefinitionName Contributor -ResourceGroupName rg-prod
New-AzRoleAssignment -ApplicationId <appId> -RoleDefinitionName "Storage Blob Data Reader" -Scope <saId>

Get-AzRoleAssignment -SignInName alice@contoso.com -ExpandPrincipalGroups
Get-AzRoleAssignment -Scope "/subscriptions/<id>" -IncludeClassicAdministrators
Remove-AzRoleAssignment -SignInName alice@contoso.com -RoleDefinitionName Reader -Scope "/subscriptions/<id>"

# Custom role from a clone
$r = Get-AzRoleDefinition "Virtual Machine Contributor"
$r.Id = $null; $r.Name = "VM Operator"; $r.IsCustom = $true
$r.Actions.Clear(); $r.Actions.Add("Microsoft.Compute/virtualMachines/start/action")
$r.AssignableScopes.Clear(); $r.AssignableScopes.Add("/subscriptions/<id>")
New-AzRoleDefinition -Role $r
```

### Policy

```powershell
$def = New-AzPolicyDefinition -Name req-tag -Policy ./rule.json -Mode Indexed
New-AzPolicyAssignment -Name req-tag-prod -PolicyDefinition $def -Scope "/subscriptions/<id>"
Get-AzPolicyAssignment -Scope "/subscriptions/<id>"
Get-AzPolicyState -ResourceGroupName rg-prod
Start-AzPolicyComplianceScan -ResourceGroupName rg-prod
Start-AzPolicyRemediation -Name fix -PolicyAssignmentId <id>
Remove-AzPolicyAssignment -Name req-tag-prod -Scope "/subscriptions/<id>"

New-AzManagementGroup -GroupName Platform -DisplayName Platform
New-AzManagementGroupSubscription -GroupName Platform -SubscriptionId <id>
```

### Storage

```powershell
New-AzStorageAccount -ResourceGroupName rg -Name stacct -Location eastus `
  -SkuName Standard_GRS -Kind StorageV2 -AccessTier Hot `
  -EnableHttpsTrafficOnly $true -MinimumTlsVersion TLS1_2 -AllowBlobPublicAccess $false

Get-AzStorageAccount -ResourceGroupName rg
Get-AzStorageAccountNameAvailability -Name stacct
Set-AzStorageAccount -ResourceGroupName rg -Name stacct -SkuName Standard_RAGRS
Get-AzStorageAccountKey -ResourceGroupName rg -Name stacct
New-AzStorageAccountKey -ResourceGroupName rg -Name stacct -KeyName key1

$ctx = New-AzStorageContext -StorageAccountName stacct -UseConnectedAccount
# or: $ctx = (Get-AzStorageAccount -ResourceGroupName rg -Name stacct).Context

New-AzStorageContainer -Name data -Context $ctx -Permission Off
Get-AzStorageContainer -Context $ctx
Set-AzStorageBlobContent -File ./f.txt -Container data -Blob f.txt -Context $ctx -StandardBlobTier Cool
Get-AzStorageBlob -Container data -Context $ctx
Get-AzStorageBlobContent -Container data -Blob f.txt -Destination ./ -Context $ctx
Remove-AzStorageBlob -Container data -Blob f.txt -Context $ctx

New-AzStorageBlobSASToken -Container data -Blob f.txt -Permission r `
  -ExpiryTime (Get-Date).AddHours(1) -Context $ctx -FullUri
New-AzStorageContainerStoredAccessPolicy -Container data -Policy mypolicy `
  -Permission rl -ExpiryTime (Get-Date).AddDays(30) -Context $ctx
Remove-AzStorageContainerStoredAccessPolicy -Container data -Policy mypolicy -Context $ctx

New-AzRmStorageShare -ResourceGroupName rg -StorageAccountName stacct -Name myshare -QuotaGiB 1024
Update-AzStorageBlobServiceProperty -ResourceGroupName rg -AccountName stacct `
  -EnableChangeFeed $true -IsVersioningEnabled $true -DeleteRetentionPolicy $true -RetentionDays 7

Add-AzStorageAccountNetworkRule -ResourceGroupName rg -Name stacct -IPAddressOrRange "203.0.113.0/24"
Update-AzStorageAccountNetworkRuleSet -ResourceGroupName rg -Name stacct -DefaultAction Deny
```

### Compute

```powershell
New-AzVm -ResourceGroupName rg -Name vm01 -Location eastus -Image Win2022Datacenter `
  -Size Standard_D2s_v5 -VirtualNetworkName vnet1 -SubnetName web -Zone 1 -Credential (Get-Credential)

Get-AzVM -Status | Select Name, PowerState
Start-AzVM -ResourceGroupName rg -Name vm01
Restart-AzVM -ResourceGroupName rg -Name vm01
Stop-AzVM -ResourceGroupName rg -Name vm01 -Force                 # deallocates
Stop-AzVM -ResourceGroupName rg -Name vm01 -StayProvisioned       # keeps billing!
Remove-AzVM -ResourceGroupName rg -Name vm01 -Force
Set-AzVM -ResourceGroupName rg -Name vm01 -Redeploy
Set-AzVM -ResourceGroupName rg -Name vm01 -Generalize

$vm = Get-AzVM -ResourceGroupName rg -Name vm01
$vm.HardwareProfile.VmSize = "Standard_D4s_v5"
Update-AzVM -ResourceGroupName rg -VM $vm

$disk = New-AzDiskConfig -Location eastus -CreateOption Empty -DiskSizeGB 128 -SkuName Premium_LRS
$d = New-AzDisk -ResourceGroupName rg -DiskName disk1 -Disk $disk
Add-AzVMDataDisk -VM $vm -Name disk1 -CreateOption Attach -ManagedDiskId $d.Id -Lun 0 -Caching ReadOnly
Update-AzVM -ResourceGroupName rg -VM $vm

New-AzSnapshotConfig -SourceUri $d.Id -Location eastus -CreateOption Copy -Incremental |
  New-AzSnapshot -ResourceGroupName rg -SnapshotName snap1

New-AzAvailabilitySet -ResourceGroupName rg -Name as1 -Location eastus `
  -PlatformFaultDomainCount 3 -PlatformUpdateDomainCount 5 -Sku Aligned

Set-AzVMExtension -ResourceGroupName rg -VMName vm01 -Name CustomScript `
  -Publisher Microsoft.Compute -ExtensionType CustomScriptExtension -TypeHandlerVersion 1.10 `
  -SettingString '{"commandToExecute":"powershell -File setup.ps1"}'

Invoke-AzVMRunCommand -ResourceGroupName rg -Name vm01 -CommandId RunPowerShellScript -ScriptPath ./s.ps1

New-AzVmss -ResourceGroupName rg -VMScaleSetName vmss1 -Location eastus `
  -OrchestrationMode Flexible -InstanceCount 2 -Credential (Get-Credential)
Update-AzVmss -ResourceGroupName rg -VMScaleSetName vmss1 -SkuCapacity 5
Get-AzVmssVM -ResourceGroupName rg -VMScaleSetName vmss1
```

```powershell
$sub = New-AzVirtualNetworkSubnetConfig -Name web -AddressPrefix 10.0.1.0/24

Add-AzRouteConfig -RouteTable $rt -Name to-fw -AddressPrefix 0.0.0.0/0 `

$rt | Set-AzRouteTable

# TROUBLESHOOTING
Get-AzEffectiveNetworkSecurityGroup -NetworkInterfaceName nic1 -ResourceGroupName rg
Get-AzEffectiveRouteTable -NetworkInterfaceName nic1 -ResourceGroupName rg | Format-Table
Test-AzNetworkWatcherIPFlow -NetworkWatcher $nw -TargetVirtualMachineId <vmId> `

  -SourceIPAddress 10.0.1.4 -DestinationIPAddress 10.2.0.5
```

| Item | Number |
|---|---|
| Management group **max depth** | **6** |
| Management groups per directory | 10,000 |
| Subscriptions per management group | Unlimited |
| Resource groups per subscription | 980 |
| Resources per RG (per type) | ~800 |
| **Role assignments per subscription** | **4,000** |
| Role assignments per management group | 500 |
| Custom roles per tenant | 5,000 |
| **Tags per resource** | **50** |
| Tag name / value length | **512** / **256** chars |
| **Policy definitions per management group** | 500 |
| Policy assignments per scope | 200 |
| Initiatives per management group | 200 |
| **Rules per NSG** | **1,000** |
| **NSG rule priority range** | **100–4096** |
| NSG default rule priorities | 65000, 65001, 65500 |
| NSGs per NIC / subnet | **1** |
| **Routes per route table** | **400** |
| Route tables per subnet | **1** |
| **Subnets per VNet** | 3,000 |
| **VNet peerings per VNet** | 500 |
| ASGs per IP configuration | 20 |
| **Public IPs per subscription (Standard)** | 1,000 |
| **VMs per availability set** | 200 |
| **Fault domains per availability set** | **2 or 3** (max 3) |
| **Update domains per availability set** | **5 default, 20 max** |
| **VMSS max instances** | **1,000** |
| **Load Balancer backend pool (Standard)** | **1,000** |
| **Storage account name length** | **3–24**, lowercase alphanumeric |
| **Container name length** | 3–63 |
| **Windows VM computer name** | **1–15** chars |
| Linux VM name | 1–64 chars |
| **Max storage account capacity** | ~**5 PiB** |
| Block blob max size | ~190.7 TiB |
| **Queue message max size** | **64 KB** |
| Table entity max size | 1 MB |
| **Stored access policies per container** | **5** |
| **Lifecycle rules per policy** | **100** |
| **Diagnostic settings per resource** | **5** |
| **Managed disk max size** | **32 TiB** (Ultra/PSSDv2: 64 TiB) |
| OS disk max (Gen2) | 4 TiB |
| **Data disks per VM** | Size-dependent (up to 64) |
| **Azure Files standard share max** | 5 TiB (**100 TiB** with large file shares) |
| **File Sync: cloud endpoints per sync group** | **1** |
| File Sync: server endpoints per sync group | 100 |
| **Action group: email/SMS/etc. per type** | **10** |
| **Recovery Services vaults per sub/region** | 500 |
| **VMs per Recovery Services vault** | 2,000 |
| ARM template resources | 800 |
| ARM template parameters | 256 |
| ARM template outputs | 64 |

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| **Smallest subnet** | **/29** (3 usable) |
| **Reserved IPs per subnet** | **5** |
| Largest VNet | **/8** |

| **AzureFirewallSubnet** | **/26** |
| **AzureBastionSubnet** | **/26** |
| RouteServerSubnet | /27 |

| /24 usable IPs | **251** |
| /16 usable IPs | 65,531 |

### Times and durations

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| **Deleted Entra user retention** | **30 days** |
| **Deleted storage account recovery** | **14 days** |
| **Blob/container soft delete** | **1–365 days** |
| **Backup soft delete** | **14 days** (extendable to 180) |
| **Key Vault soft delete** | **7–90 days** (default 90) |
| **Platform metrics retention** | **93 days** |
| **Activity Log retention** | **90 days** |
| **Log Analytics free retention** | **30 days** (90 for App Insights/Sentinel) |
| **Log Analytics max interactive retention** | **730 days** (2 years) |
| **Log Analytics archive** | **12 years** (4,383 days) |
| **Cool tier minimum** | **30 days** |
| **Cold tier minimum** | **90 days** |
| **Archive tier minimum** | **180 days** |
| **Archive rehydration (Standard)** | ≤ **15 hours** |
| **Archive rehydration (High)** | < **1 hour** (<10 GB) |
| **Lifecycle policy first run** | up to **48 hours** |
| **File Sync cloud change detection** | **24 hours** |
| **Entra Connect sync cycle** | **30 minutes** |
| **VPN gateway deployment** | **30–45 minutes** |
| **VMSS autoscale cool-down default** | **5 minutes** |
| **LB health probe interval** | **5 seconds** |
| **LB unhealthy threshold** | **2** failures (~10 s to mark down) |
| **Cloud Shell timeout** | **20 minutes** |
| **App Service idle unload (Free/Shared)** | **20 minutes** |
| **Update domain reboot gap** | **30 minutes** |
| **Instant Restore snapshots** | **1–5 days** (default 2) |
| **ASR RPO (Azure-to-Azure)** | ~**30 seconds** |
| **ASR crash-consistent RP** | every **5 minutes** |
| **ASR app-consistent RP** | every **4 hours** (default) |
| **GRS RPO** | < **15 minutes** |
| **Update Manager maintenance window** | **1h30m – 3h55m** |
| **Update Manager: no new updates** | last **20 minutes** |
| **SAS: user delegation max** | **7 days** |
| **Action group SMS/voice/push rate limit** | **1 per 5 minutes** |
| **Action group email rate limit** | **100 per hour** |
| **NAT Gateway idle timeout** | **4 min** (up to 120) |
| **LB idle timeout** | **4 min** (up to 30) |
| **SNAT ports per public IP** | **64,000** (NAT GW: 64,512) |
| **Role assignment propagation** | up to **~10 minutes** |

### SLAs

| Config | SLA |
|---|---|
| **Single VM, Premium/Ultra disks** | **99.9%** |
| Single VM, Standard SSD | 99.5% |
| Single VM, Standard HDD | 95% |
| **Availability Set (2+ VMs)** | **99.95%** |
| **Availability Zones (2+ zones)** | **99.99%** |

| **Basic Load Balancer** | **None** |

| Redundancy | Copies | Durability |

| **ZRS** | 3 | 12 nines |

## D. Master Glossary

| Term | One-line definition |
|---|---|
| **AAD / Entra ID** | Microsoft's cloud identity service. Formerly Azure AD. |
| **ACI** | Azure Container Instances — serverless containers. |
| **ACR** | Azure Container Registry — private Docker registry. |
| **ADE** | Azure Disk Encryption — in-guest BitLocker/dm-crypt. |
| **ADLS Gen2** | Data Lake Storage — StorageV2 + hierarchical namespace. |
| **AGIC** | Application Gateway Ingress Controller for AKS. |
| **AHB** | Azure Hybrid Benefit — reuse on-prem Windows/SQL licenses. |
| **AKS** | Azure Kubernetes Service — managed Kubernetes. |
| **AMA** | Azure Monitor Agent — the current telemetry agent. |
| **App GW** | Application Gateway — regional L7 load balancer. |
| **ARM** | Azure Resource Manager — the control plane. |
| **ASE** | App Service Environment — single-tenant, VNet-injected App Service. |
| **ASG** | Application Security Group — named group of NICs for NSG rules. |
| **ASN** | Autonomous System Number — BGP identifier. Azure default 65515. |
| **ASR** | Azure Site Recovery — DR replication and orchestration. |
| **ASP** | App Service Plan — the compute your web apps run on. |
| **AU** | Administrative Unit — scopes Entra roles to a subset of the directory. |
| **AZ** | Availability Zone — physically separate DC within a region. |
| **AzCopy** | CLI tool for high-performance storage data transfer. |
| **B2B** | Business-to-Business — invite external users as guests. |
| **B2C** | Business-to-Consumer — separate tenant for customer identity. |
| **Bastion** | Managed jump host for RDP/SSH over TLS in the browser. |
| **BGP** | Border Gateway Protocol — dynamic routing. |
| **Bicep** | DSL that transpiles to ARM JSON. No state file. |
| **Blob** | Binary Large Object — unstructured storage. |
| **Block blob** | Default blob type. For files, images, backups. |
| **Append blob** | For log files — append-only. |
| **Page blob** | For VHDs/disks — 512-byte random access. |
| **CAF** | Cloud Adoption Framework — Microsoft's guidance. |
| **CanNotDelete** | Lock type allowing read/modify but not delete. |
| **CIDR** | Address notation like 10.0.0.0/24. |
| **CMK** | Customer-Managed Key (in Key Vault). |
| **Cloud Shell** | Browser shell; needs a storage account. |
| **cloud-init** | Linux-only first-boot configuration. |
| **Composite SLA** | Multiply dependent SLAs together. |
| **Conditional Access** | Policy engine requiring P1. |
| **Container group** | ACI's pod-equivalent scheduling unit. |
| **Contributor** | Full control **except** assigning roles. |
| **Cool tier** | 30-day minimum, cheaper storage, pricier access. |
| **Cold tier** | 90-day minimum. |
| **CRR** | Cross Region Restore — restore from the paired region. |
| **DCR** | Data Collection Rule — tells AMA what to collect. |
| **DCE** | Data Collection Endpoint. |
| **Deallocated** | Stopped **and** compute billing stopped. |
| **Deny assignment** | Blocks actions; overrides everything; not user-creatable. |
| **DINE** | DeployIfNotExists policy effect; needs a managed identity. |
| **DNAT** | Destination NAT — Azure Firewall inbound rules. |
| **DSR** | Direct Server Return / floating IP. |
| **Effective routes/rules** | The computed final NSG/route state on a NIC. |
| **Elevate access** | Global Admin grants themselves User Access Administrator at root `/`. |
| **Ephemeral OS disk** | OS disk on the local host — free, fast, stateless. |
| **ExpressRoute** | Private circuit to Microsoft; **not encrypted** by default. |
| **FD** | Fault domain — shared power/network. |
| **File Sync** | Turns Windows Server into a cache of an Azure file share. |
| **Flexible orchestration** | Modern VMSS mode; instances are real VMs. |
| **Forced tunneling** | Route 0.0.0.0/0 to on-prem or a firewall. |
| **Front Door** | Global L7 load balancer + CDN + WAF. |
| **Generalized image** | Sysprep'd — a reusable template. |
| **Global peering** | VNet peering across regions. |
| **GRS** | Geo-Redundant Storage — 6 copies, secondary not readable. |
| **GZRS** | Geo-Zone-Redundant — ZRS primary + geo secondary. |
| **HA Ports** | Standard internal LB rule for all ports. |
| **HNS** | Hierarchical namespace — enables ADLS Gen2. |
| **Hot tier** | Frequent access, no minimum retention. |
| **Hotpatching** | Patch without a reboot (Windows Server Azure Edition). |
| **Hub-and-spoke** | Central hub VNet with shared services + peered spokes. |
| **Hybrid Entra join** | Joined to both on-prem AD and Entra ID. |
| **Idempotent** | Repeating a deployment produces the same result. |
| **ILB** | Internal Load Balancer — private frontend. |
| **ILPIP** | Instance-Level Public IP — a PIP directly on a VM. |
| **IMDS** | Instance Metadata Service — `169.254.169.254`. |
| **Immutable storage** | WORM; locked policies cannot be shortened. |
| **Incremental mode** | Default ARM deploy — leaves untemplated resources alone. |
| **Complete mode** | ARM deploy that **deletes** untemplated resources in the RG. |
| **Initiative** | A group of policy definitions. |
| **Instant Restore** | Local snapshots for fast backup restore, 1–5 days. |
| **IP forwarding** | NIC setting required for NVAs to forward traffic. |
| **KQL** | Kusto Query Language — for Log Analytics/Resource Graph. |
| **kubenet** | AKS networking where pods use a logical IP space. |
| **Azure CNI** | AKS networking where pods get real VNet IPs. |
| **LAW** | Log Analytics workspace. |
| **LNG** | Local Network Gateway — represents your on-prem site. |
| **Longest prefix match** | The most specific route wins. |
| **LRS** | Locally Redundant Storage — 3 copies, 1 DC. |
| **MABS / DPM** | On-prem application-aware backup server. |
| **Managed disk** | Azure-managed disk storage. The default. |
| **Managed identity** | An Azure-managed service principal for a resource. |
| **MARS** | The agent backing up on-prem **files/folders/system state**. |
| **MG** | Management group. |
| **MMA / OMS agent** | Legacy Log Analytics agent — **retired Aug 2024**. |
| **MUA** | Multi-user authorization for backup deletion. |
| **NAT Gateway** | Managed outbound-only SNAT for a subnet. |
| **NIC** | Network interface card resource. |
| **NSG** | Network Security Group — L3/L4 stateful filter. |
| **NVA** | Network Virtual Appliance — a firewall/router VM. |
| **Owner** | Contributor + User Access Administrator. |
| **P2S** | Point-to-Site VPN — single client device. |
| **PE** | Private Endpoint — gives a PaaS service a private IP in your VNet. |
| **PHS** | Password Hash Sync — recommended hybrid identity method. |
| **PIM** | Privileged Identity Management — JIT roles. Requires P2. |
| **PIP** | Public IP address resource. |
| **PPG** | Proximity Placement Group — co-locate for low latency. |
| **PSK** | Pre-Shared Key for a VPN connection. |
| **PTA** | Pass-through Authentication — validates on-prem. |
| **RA-GRS** | GRS with a **readable** secondary endpoint. |
| **RBAC** | Role-Based Access Control — additive, no user deny. |
| **Reader** | `*/read` — view only. |
| **Reserved Instance** | 1/3-year commitment for up to ~72% savings. |
| **Resource lock** | CanNotDelete or ReadOnly; applies to everyone. |
| **Resource Graph** | KQL query service for resource inventory. |
| **RG** | Resource group. |
| **RPO** | Recovery Point Objective — max acceptable data loss. |
| **RTO** | Recovery Time Objective — max acceptable downtime. |
| **RSV** | Recovery Services vault — for VMs and Files. |
| **S2S** | Site-to-Site VPN — on-prem network to VNet. |
| **SAS** | Shared Access Signature — a scoped, signed URL. |
| **Service endpoint** | Free subnet-to-service restriction; no private IP. |
| **Service tag** | Microsoft-managed IP group for NSG/Firewall rules. |
| **SNAT** | Source NAT for outbound traffic. |
| **Soft delete** | Recoverable deletion window. |
| **Specialized image** | Non-generalized clone; keeps hostname/SIDs. |
| **Spot VM** | Deep-discount evictable capacity. |
| **SSE** | Storage Service Encryption — always on. |
| **SSPR** | Self-Service Password Reset. |
| **Standard PIP/LB** | Secure by default (closed); needs an NSG. |
| **Stored access policy** | Container-level policy enabling **SAS revocation**. |
| **Sysprep** | Windows generalization tool. |
| **Template spec** | An ARM template stored as an Azure resource. |
| **Traffic Manager** | **DNS-based** global routing; not in the data path. |
| **Trusted Launch** | Secure Boot + vTPM. Gen2 only. |
| **UD** | Update domain — rebooted together during maintenance. |
| **UDR** | User-Defined Route. |
| **UPN** | User Principal Name — the sign-in name. |
| **User delegation SAS** | Entra-signed SAS; max 7 days; most secure. |
| **VMSS** | Virtual Machine Scale Set. |
| **VNet** | Virtual Network — regional, single subscription. |
| **VNet integration** | App Service **outbound** access to a VNet. |
| **VPN Gateway** | Encrypted tunnel over the internet. |
| **WAF** | Web Application Firewall — OWASP protection. |
| **WORM** | Write Once, Read Many — immutable storage. |
| **Workbook** | Interactive Azure Monitor report. |
| **ZRS** | Zone-Redundant Storage — 3 copies across 3 AZs. |
| **`168.63.129.16`** | Azure's magic IP: DNS, DHCP, health probes, platform comms. |
| **`169.254.169.254`** | IMDS — instance metadata and managed identity tokens. |

---

## E. Practice Questions

> Answers with reasoning follow each block. Cover the answers first.

### Domain 1 — Identity and Governance

**Q1.** A user has the **Contributor** role at the subscription scope and the **Reader** role at the resource group `rg-prod`. What can they do in `rg-prod`?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Full Contributor actions** (create, update, delete resources — but not assign roles).

RBAC is **additive**. The union of Contributor ∪ Reader = Contributor. Assigning a *lesser* role at a child scope **does not restrict** anything. To limit them, remove the subscription-level Contributor assignment.
</details>

**Q2.** A Global Administrator reports they cannot see any subscriptions in the Azure portal. What should they do?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Elevate access.** Entra ID → Properties → *Access management for Azure resources* → **Yes**. This grants **User Access Administrator** at the root scope `/`.

Global Administrator is an **Entra** role and grants **no** Azure resource permissions by default. Turn the toggle back off afterwards.
</details>

**Q3.** You need to delegate password resets to a helpdesk team, but only for users in the London office. What do you configure?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

An **Administrative Unit** containing the London users, with the **Helpdesk Administrator** Entra role **scoped to that AU**. Requires **Entra ID P1**.

Not RBAC (that's for Azure resources). Not a resource group.
</details>

**Q4.** An Azure Policy with a **Deny** effect for VM sizes larger than `Standard_D4s_v5` is assigned to a subscription containing 50 existing `Standard_D8s_v5` VMs. What happens?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Nothing happens to the existing VMs — they keep running.** They are marked **non-compliant**. New VMs or updates that violate the rule are **blocked**.

Policy is **not retroactive**. Only `Modify` and `DeployIfNotExists` can remediate, and `Deny` is neither.
</details>

**Q5.** A resource group has a **ReadOnly** lock. An application that uses the storage account inside it starts failing. Why?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**`listKeys` is a POST operation**, which ReadOnly blocks. The app can no longer retrieve the account keys.

This is why `CanNotDelete` is the safer default lock.
</details>

**Q6.** You add the tag `environment=prod` to `rg-prod`. Do the 20 VMs inside inherit it?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**No.** Tags are **not inherited**.

Use the built-in policy **"Inherit a tag from the resource group"** (`Modify` effect) with a **managed identity**, then run a **remediation task** to apply it to existing resources.
</details>

**Q7.** A monthly budget of $5,000 is configured on the subscription with an alert at 100%. Spend reaches $6,000. What happened?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Alerts were sent; nothing was stopped.** Budgets **never** block spending.

To actually stop spend, attach an **Action Group** → **Automation runbook** to deallocate resources.
</details>

**Q8.** You need a role that lets someone start and restart VMs but not create or delete them. The built-in roles don't fit. What are the two constraints on your custom role's `AssignableScopes`?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

1. It **cannot be the root scope `/`**.
2. It **cannot use wildcards**.

It must list at least one explicit MG/subscription/RG scope.
</details>

**Q9.** Which two features definitively require **Entra ID P2** (not P1)?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Privileged Identity Management (PIM)** and **Identity Protection** (risk-based policies). **Access Reviews** and **Entitlement Management** are also P2.

P1 gives Conditional Access, dynamic groups, group-based licensing, and SSPR writeback.
</details>

**Q10.** You move a subscription from Tenant A to Tenant B. What happens to the RBAC role assignments?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**They are all removed.** You must recreate every assignment in the new tenant.

Moving a subscription between **management groups** within the same tenant preserves assignments.
</details>

### Domain 2 — Storage

**Q11.** You need to read storage data during a regional outage **without initiating a failover**. Which redundancy option?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**RA-GRS** or **RA-GZRS**.

Plain GRS/GZRS secondaries are **not readable**. Only the RA- variants expose the `-secondary` endpoint.
</details>

**Q12.** A blob is in the **Archive** tier. An application tries to download it. What happens?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**The read fails.** Archive is **offline**.

You must **rehydrate** first: change the tier (Standard ≤15 h, High <1 h) or **copy** the blob to a new online blob.
</details>

**Q13.** You must be able to revoke a specific customer's SAS without affecting any other client. How?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

Issue the SAS **against a stored access policy** (`si=<policyId>`), then **delete or modify the policy** to revoke it.

Regenerating the account key would invalidate **every** SAS. Max **5** stored access policies per container.
</details>

**Q14.** A private endpoint for a storage account shows **Approved**, but the VM still connects to the public IP. Why?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**DNS.** The name still resolves publicly.

Create the private DNS zone **`privatelink.blob.core.windows.net`**, **link it to the VNet**, and add the **A record** (or use a DNS zone group). Verify with `nslookup`.
</details>

**Q15.** You configured **infrastructure encryption** requirements after creating a storage account. Can you enable it now?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**No.** Infrastructure encryption can **only be enabled at account creation**. You must create a new account and migrate the data.
</details>

**Q16.** A GPv1 storage account needs lifecycle management to tier blobs to Cool after 30 days. What must you do first?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Upgrade the account to GPv2.** Lifecycle management is **not supported on GPv1**. The upgrade is in-place and free.
</details>

**Q17.** Users at the office cannot mount an Azure file share, but it works from an Azure VM. What's the cause?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**TCP port 445 is blocked** by the ISP/corporate firewall.

Verify with `Test-NetConnection <acct>.file.core.windows.net -Port 445`. Fix: VPN, ExpressRoute, or a private endpoint reached over a tunnel.
</details>

**Q18.** You need to configure **GRS** for a **premium file share**. How?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**You can't.** Premium file shares (FileStorage accounts) support **LRS and ZRS only**.

Use a standard file share, or protect the premium share with Azure Backup / File Sync.
</details>

**Q19.** You must transfer 60 TB from on-prem to Azure. Your internet link would take 3 weeks. What do you use?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Azure Data Box** (80 TB usable).

Rule of thumb: if the network transfer would take more than a week, ship a Data Box.
</details>

**Q20.** Point-in-time restore for blobs isn't available in the portal. What's missing?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

The prerequisite chain: **soft delete → versioning → change feed**, then PITR. Also, **`restore-days` must be less than `delete-retention-days`**.
</details>

### Domain 3 — Compute

**Q21.** A VM was shut down from inside the guest OS a month ago. The bill shows compute charges. Why?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

The VM is in the **"Stopped"** state, **not "Stopped (deallocated)"**. The host is still reserved, so **compute is still billed**.

Use `az vm deallocate` / `Stop-AzVM -Force` to actually stop the compute charge.
</details>

**Q22.** You need to add an existing production VM to an availability set. How?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**You can't.** The availability set must be set **at VM creation**.

You must **delete the VM (keeping the disks)** and **recreate** it in the availability set from the existing disks.
</details>

**Q23.** A VM needs an **Ultra Disk** as its OS disk. Is this possible?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**No.** Ultra Disk (and Premium SSD v2) can only be **data disks**. Also: **no host caching** and **no availability sets** — Ultra requires Availability **Zones**.
</details>

**Q24.** A SQL Server VM has slow transaction log writes. What caching should the log disk use?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**None.**

Data files → **ReadOnly**. Log files → **None**. ReadWrite caching on SQL disks risks corruption.
</details>

**Q25.** A Premium SSD P30 (1 TiB) is only 30% full but is hitting an IOPS limit. What do you do?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Increase the disk size** (to P40+) or **stripe multiple disks**.

Premium SSD performance is **tied to size**, not to how full it is.
</details>

**Q26.** You need VMSS to autoscale on **memory** usage. What must you install?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

The **Azure Monitor Agent** with a **Data Collection Rule** collecting the memory performance counter.

Memory is a **guest** metric — Azure can't see inside the VM. CPU, network, and disk are host metrics needing no agent.
</details>

**Q27.** Deployment slots are needed on an App Service currently running on a **Basic (B1)** plan. What must change?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Scale up to Standard (S1) or higher.**

**Basic has zero deployment slots.** Standard = 5, Premium = 20.
</details>

**Q28.** The staging slot must use a test database and production the prod database. The connection string keeps swapping. What do you configure?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

Mark the connection string as a **"Deployment slot setting"** (sticky) so it **stays with the slot** and does not swap.
</details>

**Q29.** An ACR must be replicated to three regions and reachable only via a private endpoint. Which SKU?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Premium.**

Geo-replication, private endpoints/Private Link, content trust, CMK, scoped tokens, and zone redundancy are all **Premium-only**.
</details>

**Q30.** An ACI batch job keeps restarting after it completes successfully. Why?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

The **restart policy is `Always`** (the default).

Set it to **`OnFailure`** (retry on error) or **`Never`** (run once).
</details>

**Q31.** After Sysprep + capture as a generalized image, can you still use the source VM?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**No.** A generalized VM is unusable and must be deleted.

If you need to keep the source, use a **specialized** image or take a **snapshot** instead.
</details>

**Q32.** An AKS deployment fails with "insufficient IP addresses." The cluster uses Azure CNI. Why?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Azure CNI assigns every pod a real VNet IP.** The subnet is too small.

Needed = (nodes + surge) × (maxPods + 1). Fix: enlarge the subnet, reduce `--max-pods`, or use **CNI Overlay**/kubenet.
</details>

### Domain 4 — Networking

**Q33.** The subnet NSG allows TCP 443 inbound. The NIC NSG has no rule for 443. Can traffic reach the VM?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**No.** The NIC NSG's default `DenyAllInBound` (priority 65500) blocks it.

**BOTH** NSGs must allow the traffic. Inbound order: **subnet → NIC**. Outbound: **NIC → subnet**.
</details>

**Q34.** VNet-A is peered to VNet-Hub. VNet-B is peered to VNet-Hub. Can A reach B?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**No.** VNet peering is **non-transitive**.

Fix: direct A↔B peering, or a **firewall/NVA in the hub** with **UDRs** on the spokes plus **"Allow forwarded traffic"** on both hub peerings, or **Virtual WAN**.
</details>

**Q35.** A UDR sends `0.0.0.0/0` to an NVA. The BGP route from the on-prem VPN also advertises `0.0.0.0/0`. Which wins?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**The UDR.**

Route precedence (for identical prefixes): **UDR > BGP > System**. Across different prefixes, **longest prefix match** decides first.
</details>

**Q36.** Traffic is being dropped at an NVA even though the UDR points to its private IP. What's missing?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**IP forwarding** on the NVA's **NIC** (`az network nic update --ip-forwarding true`) — and in the **guest OS**.

Azure drops packets a NIC receives that aren't addressed to it unless IP forwarding is enabled.
</details>

**Q37.** All backend VMs behind a Standard Load Balancer show as unhealthy. The app works when you browse directly to a VM's private IP. What's the likely cause?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

The **health probe from `168.63.129.16`** is blocked — either an NSG rule overriding **`AllowAzureLoadBalancerInBound`**, or the **guest OS firewall** blocking the probe port. Or the probe path returns something other than **HTTP 200**.
</details>

**Q38.** You need to point `contoso.com` (the apex, no `www`) at an Azure public IP that may change. What record type?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

An **Alias record of type A** targeting the **Public IP resource**.

A **CNAME cannot exist at the zone apex** (DNS protocol rule). Alias records auto-update and work at the apex.
</details>

**Q39.** VMs in VNet-B can't resolve names in a private DNS zone linked to VNet-A. VNet-A and VNet-B are peered. Why?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Peering does not share DNS resolution.**

You must create a **virtual network link** from the private DNS zone to **VNet-B**. (Auto-registration is limited to one zone per VNet, but resolution links are unlimited.)
</details>

**Q40.** Users are losing shopping cart contents when browsing a load-balanced site. What do you configure?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Session persistence = `Client IP`** (2-tuple) on the Load Balancer rule — or **cookie-based affinity** on an Application Gateway.

Better long-term: make the app stateless with external session state (Redis).
</details>

**Q41.** You need `/images/*` to go to one backend pool and `/video/*` to another. Which service?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Application Gateway** with **URL path-based routing** (regional), or **Front Door** (global).

Load Balancer is L4 and cannot see URLs.
</details>

**Q42.** EU customers' requests must be served only by EU endpoints for GDPR compliance. Which Traffic Manager routing method?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Geographic**.

Not Performance — Performance routes by **latency** and could send a Paris user to a faster US endpoint.
</details>

**Q43.** VMs behind a Standard LB have intermittent outbound connection failures under load. What's happening and how do you fix it?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**SNAT port exhaustion.**

Fix: attach a **NAT Gateway** to the subnet (the modern answer). Alternatives: more frontend public IPs, configure outbound rules with more ports, use connection pooling, or use private endpoints for Azure PaaS.
</details>

**Q44.** An Application Gateway shows "Unhealthy" and cannot be managed. The subnet NSG denies all inbound. What rule is missing?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

Allow inbound **TCP 65200–65535** from the **`GatewayManager`** service tag (v2), plus **`AzureLoadBalancer`**.

This is the App Gateway control-plane channel.
</details>

**Q45.** Which Network Watcher tool tells you **which NSG rule** is blocking traffic?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**IP Flow Verify** (or NSG Diagnostics). It returns Allow/Deny **and the rule name**.

**Connection Troubleshoot** actually tests the connection end-to-end. **Connection Monitor** does it continuously.
</details>

**Q46.** You need P2S VPN with **Microsoft Entra ID authentication**. Which tunnel protocol is required?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**OpenVPN**.

SSTP is Windows-only; IKEv2 works with certificate/RADIUS. **Entra ID auth requires OpenVPN.**
</details>

**Q47.** An Azure Firewall application rule allows only `*.contoso.com`, but a network rule allows TCP 443 to `Any`. Users can reach any site. Why?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Network rules are evaluated BEFORE application rules.** The permissive network rule matched, so the application rule was never evaluated.

Order: **NAT → Network → Application**. Remove the broad network rule.
</details>

**Q48.** You need RDP access to VMs with no public IPs and no VPN. What's the answer?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Azure Bastion** in a subnet named exactly **`AzureBastionSubnet`**, minimum **`/26`**, with a **Standard static public IP**.
</details>

### Domain 5 — Monitoring and Maintenance

**Q49.** You need to know who deleted a storage account last week. Where do you look?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

The **Activity Log**, **Administrative** category. KQL:
```kql
AzureActivity
| where OperationNameValue contains "delete"
| where ActivityStatusValue == "Success"
| project TimeGenerated, Caller, OperationNameValue, _ResourceId
```
The Activity Log is retained **90 days** by default.
</details>

**Q50.** Key Vault logs from last month aren't in Log Analytics. The diagnostic setting was created yesterday. Can you get them?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**No.** Diagnostic settings are **not retroactive**. Resource logs are **off by default** and only capture data from the moment the setting exists.

Deploy settings at scale with **Azure Policy + `DeployIfNotExists`**.
</details>

**Q51.** Logs must be streamed to **Splunk** in real time. Which diagnostic setting destination?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Event Hub**.

Log Analytics = KQL/alerts. Storage account = cheap archival. Event Hub = third-party SIEM streaming.
</details>

**Q52.** AMA is installed on 50 VMs but no data is arriving. What's missing?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**A Data Collection Rule association.**

AMA collects **nothing** without a DCR telling it what to gather and where to send it.
</details>

**Q53.** You need an alert within one minute of CPU exceeding 80%. Metric alert or log alert?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Metric alert** — near real-time (~1 min).

Log alerts run on a schedule (min 1–5 min) plus ingestion latency, so they're too slow.
</details>

**Q54.** You want one alert **per VM** rather than one lumped alert for all VMs. What do you configure?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Split by dimensions** on the alert rule (e.g., split by `Computer` or `ResourceId`).
</details>

**Q55.** Twenty alerts fired but only one SMS arrived. Why?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**SMS is rate-limited to 1 per 5 minutes** per phone number. Same for voice and push. Email is limited to **100/hour**.
</details>

**Q56.** You need to back up **Azure Blobs**. Which vault type?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Backup vault.**

Recovery Services vault = VMs, Azure Files, SQL/SAP in VMs, MARS/MABS. Backup vault = **Blobs, Disks, PostgreSQL/MySQL flexible, AKS**.
</details>

**Q57.** A Recovery Services vault is LRS and protecting 100 VMs. You need geo-redundancy. What do you do?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Create a new vault** with GRS and re-protect the VMs.

Redundancy can **only be changed before the first backup item is protected**. After that it's locked.
</details>

**Q58.** You need to restore a **single file** from a VM backup. What's the fastest option?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**File recovery (item-level restore).** Download and run the provided script — it mounts the recovery point as a local drive and you copy the file out.

Don't restore the whole VM.
</details>

**Q59.** You need hourly backups of a VM. The Standard policy only offers daily. What do you use?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

An **Enhanced backup policy** (supports every 4/6/8/12 hours). Also required for **Trusted Launch** VMs.
</details>

**Q60.** You must test DR failover without impacting production. What do you use?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**ASR Test Failover** into an **isolated VNet**. It's non-disruptive, replication continues, and production keeps running.

Always run **"Clean up test failover"** afterwards.
</details>

**Q61.** Backup vs Site Recovery — you need to recover a file deleted three months ago. Which one?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Azure Backup.**

ASR keeps only ~**72 hours** of recovery points and cannot restore individual files. Backup retains up to 99 years.
</details>

**Q62.** An Update Manager maintenance window is set to 1 hour. The schedule won't save. Why?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

The **minimum maintenance window is 1 hour 30 minutes** (max 3h55m). Also note that **no new update starts in the final 20 minutes**.
</details>

**Q63.** You must patch Windows Servers **without rebooting**. What do you use?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**Hotpatching** on **Windows Server Azure Edition** with **Server Core**, on Azure or Azure Arc.
</details>

**Q64.** Which KQL operator groups rows and aggregates, and which one adds a calculated column?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

**`summarize`** aggregates (SQL `GROUP BY`). **`extend`** adds a calculated column. **`project`** selects/replaces columns.

Note: `summarize` **drops** all columns except the `by` keys and the aggregates.
</details>

**Q65.** You need to be notified of **planned Azure maintenance** affecting your VMs. Which alert?

<details><summary>Answer</summary>

A **Service Health alert** (Activity Log, `ServiceHealth` category).

Resource Health = your specific resource's state. Azure Status = the public global page, not alertable.
</details>

---

## F. Exam-Day Strategy

### The week before

- [ ] Take **at least two full-length practice exams** under timed conditions.
- [ ] Review **every** wrong answer and write down *why* the right answer is right.
- [ ] Re-read **Appendix C (Numbers)** daily — it's pure recall and cheap marks.
- [ ] Do one hands-on lab per domain, especially: build a hub-spoke with a firewall, configure a SAS with a stored access policy, restore a file from a VM backup.
- [ ] Skim the official **Skills Measured** PDF one final time to catch any recent changes.

### The 30 minutes before

- Review only the **traps** (⚠️ markers). Don't try to learn anything new.
- The five highest-yield facts to hold in working memory:
  1. **RBAC is additive; there is no user-authorable deny.**
  2. **Peering is non-transitive.**
  3. **Both subnet and NIC NSGs must allow the traffic.**
  4. **VMs → Recovery Services vault; Blobs/Disks → Backup vault.**
  5. **Regional+HTTP = App Gateway. Global+HTTP = Front Door. Regional+L4 = Load Balancer. Global+DNS = Traffic Manager.**

### During the exam

| Situation | Strategy |
|---|---|
| **Case studies** | 📌 Read the **requirements** and **questions first**, then skim the narrative for the relevant facts. The narrative is padded. |
| **Yes/No repeated scenarios** | 📌 You **cannot go back**. Judge each proposed solution **independently**. A solution can be technically valid but fail to meet the *stated goal* → "No". |
| **"Select all that apply"** | No partial credit. If unsure of one box, reason from the traps: does this option violate a hard rule? |
| **Drag and drop / ordering** | Think about **dependencies**. E.g., Sysprep → deallocate → generalize → capture. Or: GatewaySubnet → PIP → VNet Gateway → LNG → Connection. |
| **Labs** | Do them **last** if the exam lets you navigate. They're time sinks. Read the **whole task list first** — some tasks depend on earlier ones. |
| **Stuck on a question** | **Mark for review and move on.** Never burn 5 minutes on one item. |
| **Two answers look right** | Ask: *which one does Microsoft recommend?* The exam rewards the **modern, managed, least-privilege** answer (Bastion over a jump box; managed identity over a service principal secret; AMA over MMA; Private Endpoint over a service endpoint when on-prem is involved; NAT Gateway over LB outbound rules). |
| **An answer mentions a retired feature** | It's almost certainly a distractor: MMA/OMS agent, Basic Load Balancer, Basic Public IP, App Gateway v1, Run As accounts, Azure Blueprints, classic Application Insights. |
| **Time management** | ~2 minutes per question. At the halfway time mark you should be halfway through the questions. |

### The distractor patterns Microsoft loves

| Pattern | Example |
|---|---|
| **The plausible-but-retroactive claim** | "Applying the policy will fix existing resources." → No. |
| **The inheritance assumption** | "Resources inherit RG tags." → No. |
| **The transitivity assumption** | "Peering A-Hub-B lets A reach B." → No. |
| **Confusing the two role systems** | "Global Admin can manage the VM." → Not without elevating access. |
| **Confusing the two vaults** | "Back up blobs to a Recovery Services vault." → No. |
| **Confusing control plane and data plane** | "Reader can read blob contents." → No; that's Storage Blob Data Reader. |
| **The 'more restrictive assignment restricts' fallacy** | "Assign Reader at the resource to limit the Owner." → No; RBAC is additive. |
| **Ignoring the creation-time-only constraint** | Availability set, zone, HNS, infrastructure encryption, vault redundancy after first backup. |
| **The wrong scale direction** | "Scale up to add more instances." → Scale up = bigger SKU; scale **out** = more instances. |
| **Overreaching a free tier** | "Use Free App Service with a custom domain and an SLA." → Neither exists. |

### A final mental checklist for any scenario question

1. **What's the scope?** MG / subscription / RG / resource — inheritance flows down.
2. **Control plane or data plane?** This decides which role you need.
3. **Regional or global?** This decides which load balancer / which service.
4. **Is it creation-time-only?** Availability set, zone, HNS, infra encryption, vault redundancy.
5. **Does the answer require an agent?** Memory/disk-free-space metrics and guest logs do.
6. **Is there a DNS step?** Private endpoints almost always have one.
7. **Is the feature retired?** If so, it's a distractor.
8. **What's the least-privilege / most-managed option?** That's usually the intended answer.

---

## Good luck

You've read a lot of tables. The exam is, at bottom, a test of whether you know **precisely** what each term means and **exactly** where the boundaries sit — 30 days for Cool, 180 for Archive, `/29` for the smallest subnet, `/26` for Bastion, no deny in RBAC, no transitivity in peering.

Get the vocabulary right and most questions answer themselves.

---

*Study guide compiled July 2026. Azure changes constantly — verify current limits, SKU availability, retirement dates, and the official Skills Measured document on Microsoft Learn before your exam date.*
